“Don’t tell them,” whispered Mr. French desperately.

“What’ll you give me not to?”

Furtively Mr. French pressed a two-shilling piece into his hand.

Glorious vistas opened before William’s eyes He decided finally that Mr. French must join the family. Life then would be an endless succession of half-crowns and two-shilling pieces.

The next day was Sunday, and William went to the shed directly after breakfast to continue the teaching of Rufus, the dancing rat. Rufus was to be taught to dance, the other, now christened Cromwell, was to be taught to be friends with Jumble. So far this training had only reached the point of Cromwell’s sitting motionless in the cage, while in front of it William violently restrained the enraged Jumble from murder. Still, William thought, if they looked at each other long enough, friendship would grow. So they looked at each other each day till William’s arm ached. As yet friendship had not grown.

===Chinese===
「就是你给我的那半克朗闹的。」

「千万别告诉他们。」弗伦奇先生绝望地悄声说。

「你不让说，打算给我什么？」

弗伦奇先生偷偷把一枚两先令的硬币塞进他手里。

光辉灿烂的前景在威廉眼前展开。他最终拿定主意：弗伦奇先生必须成为这个家庭的一员。那样，生活就是一场没完没了的半克朗和两先令硬币的接力。

第二天是礼拜天，威廉一吃过早饭就直奔棚屋，继续训练会跳舞的老鼠鲁弗斯。鲁弗斯要学跳舞，另一只——如今起名叫克伦威尔——要学着跟杂拌儿交朋友。到目前为止，这项训练只进行到如下地步：克伦威尔一动不动地蹲在笼子里，笼子前面，威廉死命拽住怒火中烧、杀心大起的杂拌儿。不过威廉想，只要它们互相看得够久，友谊总会滋长的。于是它们天天这样互相对视，看得威廉胳膊酸痛。友谊至今尚未滋长。

===Original===
“William! It’s time for church.”

William groaned. That was the worst of Sundays. He was sure that with another half-hour’s practice Rufus would dance and Cromwell would be friends with Jumble. He was a boy not to be daunted by circumstance. He put Rufus in his pocket and put the cage containing Cromwell on the top of a pile of boxes, leaving Jumble to continue the gaze of friendship from the floor.

He walked to church quietly and demurely behind his family, one hand clutching his prayerbook, the other in his pocket clasping Rufus. He hoped to be able to continue the training during the Litany. He was not disappointed. Ethel was on one side of him, and there was no one on the other. He knelt down devoutly, one hand shading his face, the other firmly holding Rufus’s front paws as he walked it round and round on the floor. He grew more and more interested in its progress.

“Tell William to kneel up and not to fidget,” Mrs. Brown passed down via Ethel.

===Chinese===
「威廉！该上教堂了。」

威廉哀叹了一声。这就是礼拜天最糟糕的地方。他确信再练上半个钟头鲁弗斯就会跳舞了，克伦威尔也会跟杂拌儿交上朋友。他是个不为环境所吓倒的孩子。他把鲁弗斯装进口袋，把装克伦威尔的笼子放到一摞箱子的顶上，让杂拌儿留在地板上继续这场友谊的对望。

他安安静静、规规矩矩地跟在家人的身后走向教堂，一只手攥着祈祷书，另一只手在口袋里握着鲁弗斯。他指望在连祷文的当口继续训练。他没有失望。埃塞尔坐在他的一侧，另一侧没有人。他虔诚地跪下，一只手支着脸，另一只手稳稳捏着鲁弗斯的前爪，带着它在地板上转了一圈又一圈。他对它的进展越来越感兴趣。

「叫威廉跪直了，别扭来扭去。」布朗太太的话经由埃塞尔传了下来。

===Original===
William gave her a virulent glance as he received the message and, turning his back on her, continued the dancing lesson.

The Litany passed more quickly than he ever remembered its doing before. He replaced the rat in his pocket as they rose for the hymn. It was during the hymn that the catastrophe occurred.

The Browns occupied the front seat of the church. While the second verse was being sung, the congregation was electrified by the sight of a small, long-tailed white creature appearing suddenly upon Mr. Brown’s shoulder. Ethel’s scream almost drowned the organ. Mr. Brown put up his hand and the intruder jumped upon his head and stood there for a second, digging his claws into his victim’s scalp. Mr. Brown turned upon his son a purple face that promised future vengeance. The choir turned fascinated eyes upon it, and the hymn died away. William’s face was a mask of horror. Rufus next appeared running along the rim of the pulpit. There was a sudden unceremonial exit of most of the female portion of the congregation. The clergyman grew pale as Rufus approached and slid up his reading-desk. A choirboy quickly grabbed it, and retired into the vestry and thence home before his right to its possession could be questioned. William found his voice.

“He’s took it,” he said in a sibilant whisper. “It’s mine! He took it!”

“*Sh!*” said Ethel.

“It’s mine,” persisted William. “It’s what Mr. French give me for being took ill that day, you know.”

===Chinese===
威廉接到这句传话时狠狠剜了她一眼，随即背过身去，继续舞蹈课。

连祷文过得比他记忆中的任何时候都快。起立唱赞美诗时，他把老鼠放回了口袋。灾难正是发生在赞美诗中间。

布朗一家坐在教堂的头排座位上。唱到第二节时，全体会众被眼前的景象惊呆了：一只小小的、长尾巴的白毛动物突然出现在布朗先生的肩膀上。埃塞尔的尖叫几乎盖过了管风琴。布朗先生一抬手，那位不速之客跳上他的头顶，在那儿站了一秒钟，爪子抠进受害者的头皮。布朗先生朝儿子转过一张预示着日后清算的紫红脸。唱诗班全员着迷地望着它，赞美诗声渐渐消散。威廉的脸成了一副惊骇的面具。接着鲁弗斯出现在讲道坛的边沿上奔跑。会众中的女性大多突然毫无仪态地夺路而逃。鲁弗斯逼近时，牧师脸色发白，它顺着他读经台往上溜。一个唱诗班男孩一把抓住它，退进法衣室，又从那儿回了家，赶在他的所有权受到质疑之前。威廉找回了自己的声音。

「他把它拿走了。」他咝咝地悄声说，「它是我的！他拿走的！」

「*嘘*！」埃塞尔说。

「它是我的。」威廉坚持，「是弗伦奇先生因为我那天病了给我的，你知道吧。」

===Original===
“What?” said Ethel, leaning towards him.

The hymn was in full swing again now.

“He gave it me for being took ill so’s he could come and carry me home ’cause he was gone on you an’ it’s mine an’ that boy’s took it an’ it was jus’ gettin’ to dance an’﻿—”

“*Sh!*” hissed Mr. Brown violently.

“I shall never look anyone in the face again,” lamented Mrs. Brown on the way home. “I think *everyone* was in church! And the way Ethel screamed! It was *awful*! I shall dream of it for nights. William, I don’t know how you *could*!”

“Well, it’s mine,” said William. “That boy’d no business to take it. It was gettin’ to know *me*. I di’n’t *mean* it to get loose, an’ get on Father’s head an’ scare folks. I di’n’t mean it to. I meant it to be quiet and stay in my pocket. It’s mine, anyway, an’ that boy took it.”

===Chinese===
「什么？」埃塞尔朝他侧过身来问。

赞美诗此刻又正唱得响亮。

「他因为我病了才给我，好来背我回家，因为他迷上你了，它是我的，那个男孩把它拿走了，它刚学会跳舞，而且——」

「*嘘*！」布朗先生厉声呵斥。

「我再也无脸见人了。」回家的路上布朗太太哀叹。「我想教堂里*人人*都在！埃塞尔叫得那个样子！*可怕极了*！我会连着好几夜梦见它。威廉，我真不知道你怎么*能*这样！」

「哼，它是我的。」威廉说，「那个男孩没权利拿走它。它正跟我混熟呢。我不是*故意*让它跑出来、爬到爸爸脑袋上吓唬人的。我不是故意的。我要它乖乖待在我口袋里。反正它是我的，是那个男孩拿走了它。」

===Original===
“It’s not yours any more, my son,” said Mr. Brown firmly.

Ethel walked along with lips tight shut.

In the distance, walking towards them, was a tall, jaunty figure. It was Mr. French, who, ignorant of what had happened, was coming gaily on to meet them returning from church. He was smiling as he came, secure in his reception, composing airy compliments in his mind. As Ethel came on he raised his hat with a flourish and beamed at her effusively. Ethel walked past him, without a glance and with head high, leaving him, aghast and despairing, staring after her down the road. He never saw Mr. and Mrs. Brown. William realised the situation. The future half-crowns and two-shilling pieces seemed to vanish away. He protested vehemently.

“Ethel, don’t get mad at Mr. French. He di’n’t mean anything! He only wanted to do sumthin’ for you ’cause he was mad on you.”

“It’s *horrible*!” said Ethel. “First you bringing that dreadful animal to church, and then I find that he’s deceived me and you helped him. I hope Father takes the other one away.”

===Chinese===
「它不再是你的了，我的儿子。」布朗先生坚定地说。

埃塞尔紧闭着嘴唇走在一边。

远处，一个高挑神气的身影正朝他们走来。那是弗伦奇先生，他对发生的一切一无所知，正兴冲冲地迎着他们做礼拜归来。他一边走来一边微笑着，对自己的受欢迎稳操胜券，心里编排着轻飘飘的恭维话。埃塞尔走近时，他潇洒地举帽，喜气洋洋地向她绽开笑脸。埃塞尔目不斜视、高昂着头从他身边走过，把他惊呆在那里，绝望地目送她沿路远去。他压根儿没看见布朗先生和太太。威廉看清了局势。未来的半克朗和两先令硬币眼看烟消云散。他激烈地抗议。

「埃塞尔，别生弗伦奇先生的气。他不是有意的！他只是想为你做点什么，因为他迷上你了。」

「*恶心极了*！」埃塞尔说，「先是你就把那可怕的畜生带进教堂，接着我又发现他骗了我，你还帮着他。但愿爸爸把另一只也没收走。」

===Original===
“He won’t,” said William. “He never said anything about that. The other’s learnin’ to be friends with Jumble in the shed. I say, Ethel, don’t be mad at Mr. French. He﻿—”

“Oh, don’t *talk* about him,” said Ethel angrily.

William, who was something of a philosopher, accepted failure, and the loss of any riches a future allied with Mr. French might have brought him.

“All right!” he said. “Well, I’ve got the other one left, anyway.”

They entered the drive and began to walk up to the front-door. From the bushes came a scampering and breaking of twigs as Jumble dashed out to greet his master. His demeanour held more than ordinary pleasure: it expressed pride and triumph. At his master’s feet he laid his proud offering﻿—the mangled remains of Cromwell.

===Chinese===
「他不会的。」威廉说，「他根本没提过那茬儿。另一只正在棚屋里学着跟杂拌儿交朋友呢。我说，埃塞尔，别生弗伦奇先生的气。他——」

「噢，别*提*他了。」埃塞尔怒气冲冲地说。

威廉多少算个哲学家，坦然接受了失败，也接受了与弗伦奇先生结盟的未来可能带给他的任何财富的丧失。

「好吧！」他说，「反正我还剩一只呢。」

他们走上车道，朝前门走去。灌木丛里传来一阵急窜和树枝断裂的声响，杂拌儿冲出来迎接主人。它的神态超乎寻常的欢喜：那分明是骄傲和胜利。它在主人脚边摆下了它骄傲的献礼——克伦威尔血肉模糊的遗骸。

===Original===
William gasped.

“Oh, William!” said Ethel, “I’m so *sorry*.”

William assumed an expression of proud, restrained sorrow.

“All right!” he said generously. “It’s not your fault really. An’ it’s not Jumble’s fault. P’r’aps he thought it was what I was tryin’ to teach him to do. It’s jus’ no one’s fault. We’ll have to bury it.” His spirits rose. “I’ll do the reel buryin’ service out of the Prayer Book.”

He stood still gazing down at what was left of Jumble’s friend. Jumble stood by it, proud and pleased, looking up with his head on one side and his tail wagging. Sadly William reviewed the downfall of his hopes. Gone was Mr. French and all he stood for. Gone was Rufus. Gone was Cromwell. He put his hand into his pocket and it came in contact with the two-shilling piece.

“Well,” he said slowly and philosophically, “I’ve got *that* left anyway.”

===Chinese===
威廉倒吸了一口气。

「噢，威廉！」埃塞尔说，「我太*难过*了。」

威廉摆出一副高傲而克制的悲痛表情。

「没关系！」他宽宏大量地说，「其实这不怪你。也不怪杂拌儿。它八成以为这正是我一直想教它做的事。这事就是谁都不怪。咱们得把它埋了。」他的情绪又高涨起来。「我要照着公祷书，给它行一场正经的葬礼。」

他一动不动地站着，俯视着杂拌儿这位朋友的残余。杂拌儿站在旁边，又骄傲又得意，歪着头仰望他，尾巴摇个不停。威廉悲哀地回顾了自己希望的覆灭。弗伦奇先生和他所代表的一切都完了。鲁弗斯没了。克伦威尔也没了。他把手伸进口袋，碰到了那枚两先令硬币。

「好吧，」他慢吞吞地、达观地说，「不管怎么说我*还剩这个*。」

## IV The Fall of the Idol / 四 偶像的倒塌

===Original===

William was bored. He sat at his desk in the sunny schoolroom and gazed dispassionately at a row of figures on the blackboard.

“It isn’t *sense*,” he murmured scornfully.

Miss Drew was also bored, but, unlike William, she tried to hide the fact.

“If the interest on a hundred pounds for one year is five pounds,” she said wearily, then, “William Brown, do sit up and don’t look so stupid!”

===Chinese===

威廉腻烦透了。他坐在洒满阳光的教室里自己的课桌前，无精打采地瞪着黑板上的一排数字。

“这玩意儿不讲道理。”他轻蔑地嘟囔。

德鲁小姐（Miss Drew）也腻烦，不过跟威廉不一样，她努力掩饰这一点。

“假如一百镑一年的利息是五镑，”她有气无力地说，随后又道，“威廉·布朗，坐直了，别摆出那么一副蠢相！”

===Original===

William changed his position from that of lolling over one side of his desk to that of lolling over the other, and began to justify himself.

“Well, I can’t unner*stand* any of it. It’s enough to make anyone look stupid when he can’t unner*stand* any of it. I can’t think why people go on givin’ people bits of money for givin’ ’em lots of money and go on an’ on doin’ it. It dun’t seem sense. Anyone’s a mug for givin’ anyone a hundred pounds just ’cause he says he’ll go on givin’ him five pounds and go on stickin’ to his hundred pounds. How’s he to *know* he will? Well,” he warmed to his subject, “what’s to stop him not givin’ any five pounds once he’s got hold of the hundred pounds an’ goin’ on stickin’ to the hundred pounds﻿—”

Miss Drew checked him by a slim, upraised hand.

“William,” she said patiently, “just listen to me. Now suppose,” her eyes roved round the room and settled on a small red-haired boy, “suppose that Eric wanted a hundred pounds for something and you lent it to him﻿—”

“I wun’t lend Eric a hundred pounds,” he said firmly, “ ’cause I ha’n’t got it. I’ve only got 3½d, an’ I wun’t lend that to Eric, ’cause I’m not such a mug, ’cause I lent him my mouth-organ once an’ he bit a bit off an’﻿—”

===Chinese===

威廉把趴在课桌这侧的姿势换成趴在那侧，开始为自己辩护。

“唉，我一句都听不*懂*。谁要是连一句都听不懂，那模样不蠢才怪呢。我想不通，人们干嘛要因为别人给了他们一大笔钱，就一直一直付给人家一小笔一小笔的钱，没完没了地付。这看着就不讲道理。就凭一个人说他要一直给你五镑、还要一直攥着他那一百镑，就把一百镑白白交给他，谁这么干谁就是冤大头。他怎么*知道*他准会一直给？呃，”他说到兴头上，“等他把一百镑弄到手，什么能拦着他不再给那五镑，接着攥着他的那一百镑不放——”

德鲁小姐抬起一只纤细的手，止住了他。

“威廉，”她耐心地说，“听我讲。现在假设，”她的目光在教室里转了一圈，落在一个红头发的小个子男孩身上，“假设埃里克（Eric）因为什么事需要一百镑，而你借给了他——”

“我才不借给埃里克一百镑呢，”他斩钉截铁地说，“因为我没有。我统共只有三便士半，连这个我也不借给埃里克，因为我才不那么冤大头呢，因为有一回我把口琴借给他，他咬掉了一块，还有——”

===Original===

Miss Drew interrupted sharply. Teaching on a hot afternoon is rather trying.

“You’d better stay in after school, William, and I’ll explain.”

William scowled, emitted his monosyllable of scornful disdain “Huh!” and relapsed into gloom.

He brightened, however, on remembering a lizard he had caught on the way to school, and drew it from its hiding-place in his pocket. But the lizard had abandoned the unequal struggle for existence among the stones, top, penknife, bits of putty, and other small objects that inhabited William’s pocket. The housing problem had been too much for it.

===Chinese===

德鲁小姐厉声打断了他。大热天的下午教书，实在够人受的。

“你最好放学后留下，威廉，我给你讲讲。”

威廉眉头一皱，从鼻子里挤出一声轻蔑之至的单音节“哼！”，重新坠入阴郁。

不过想起上学路上捉到的一只蜥蜴，他又高兴起来，把它从口袋里的藏身之处掏了出来。可是那只蜥蜴已经在石子、陀螺、铅笔刀、几块油灰以及威廉口袋里栖身的其他小物件之间，放弃了这场力量悬殊的生存斗争。住房问题把它压垮了。

===Original===

William in disgust shrouded the remains in blotting paper, and disposed of it in his neighbour’s ink-pot. The neighbour protested and an enlivening scrimmage ensued.

Finally the lizard was dropped down the neck of an inveterate enemy of William’s in the next row, and was extracted only with the help of obliging friends. Threats of vengeance followed, couched in bloodcurdling terms, and written on blotting-paper.

Meanwhile Miss Drew explained Simple Practice to a small but earnest coterie of admirers in the front row. And William, in the back row, whiled away the hours for which his father paid the education authorities a substantial sum.

But his turn was to come.

===Chinese===

威廉厌恶地用吸墨纸把遗骸裹起来，处理进了邻座同学的墨水瓶。邻座同学提出了抗议，于是一场令人振奋的混战随之而来。

最后，那只蜥蜴被塞进了下一排威廉的一个死对头的后领里，靠几位热心朋友的帮忙才弄了出来。随之而来的是复仇的威胁，措辞骇人，写在吸墨纸上。

与此同时，德鲁小姐正给前排一小群热切的崇拜者讲解简单利息。而后排的威廉，把父亲替他向教育局缴纳了大笔银子的那些钟点，就这么消磨了过去。

但他的时运就要来了。

===Original===

At the end of afternoon school one by one the class departed, leaving William only nonchalantly chewing an india-rubber and glaring at Miss Drew.

“Now, William.”

Miss Drew was severely patient.

William went up to the platform and stood by her desk.

===Chinese===

下午放学时，同学们一个个走了，教室里只剩威廉漫不经心地嚼着一块橡皮，瞪着德鲁小姐。

“好了，威廉。”

德鲁小姐耐心得近乎严厉。

威廉走上讲台，站到她的书桌旁。

===Original===

“You see, if someone borrows a hundred pounds from someone else﻿—”

She wrote down the figures on a piece of paper, bending low over her desk. The sun poured in through the window, showing the little golden curls in the nape of her neck. She lifted to William eyes that were stern and frowning, but blue as blue above flushed cheeks.

“Don’t you *see*, William?” she said.

There was a faint perfume about her, and William the devil-may-care pirate and robber-chief, the stern despiser of all things effeminate, felt the first dart of the malicious blind god. He blushed and simpered.

“Yes, I see all about it now,” he assured her. “You’ve explained it all plain now. I cudn’t unner*stand* it before. It’s a bit soft﻿—in’t it﻿—anyway, to go lending hundred pounds about just ’cause someone says they’ll give you five pounds next year. Some folks is mugs. But I do unner*stand* now. I cudn’t unnerstand it before.”

===Chinese===

“你瞧，要是有人向别人借一百镑——”

她把数字写在一张纸上，在书桌前深深地俯下身去。阳光从窗口倾泻进来，照亮她后颈上细细的金色鬈发。她抬眼望向威廉，那双眼睛严厉地蹙着眉，可蓝得不能再蓝，衬着微红的脸颊。

“你还不明白吗，威廉？”她说。

她身上有一缕淡淡的香气。威廉，这个天不怕地不怕的海盗兼强盗头子、一切娘娘腔事物的严厉鄙视者，感到那恶毒又盲目的爱神之箭第一次射中了自己。他红了脸，傻笑起来。

“是，我现在全明白了，”他向她保证，“您现在讲得一清二楚。我先前就是听不*懂*。这事儿有点犯傻——不是吗——就凭人家说一句明年给你五镑，就把一百镑借出去。有些人是真冤大头。不过我如今是真*懂*了。我先前怎么也听不懂。”

===Original===

“You’d have found it simpler if you hadn’t played with dead lizards all the time,” she said wearily, closing her books.

William gasped.

He went home her devoted slave. Certain members of the class always deposited dainty bouquets on her desk in the morning. William was determined to outshine the rest. He went into the garden with a large basket and a pair of scissors the next morning before he set out for school.

It happened that no one was about. He went first to the hothouse. It was a riot of colour. He worked there with a thoroughness and concentration worthy of a nobler cause. He came out staggering beneath a piled-up basket of hothouse blooms. The hothouse itself was bare and desolate.

===Chinese===

“你要是别整天玩死蜥蜴，早就觉得简单了。”她疲惫地说着，合上了书。

威廉倒抽一口气。

他回家成了她死心塌地的奴隶。班里总有几个同学，每天早上在她桌上放几束雅致的鲜花。威廉决心压倒所有人。第二天早晨动身上学之前，他提上一只大篮子、拿上一把剪刀，进了花园。

碰巧四下无人。他先去了温室。温室里花开得一片绚烂。他干起活来那股彻底和专注劲儿，配得上一项更崇高的事业。出来时，他在满满一篮温室花朵的重压下步履蹒跚。温室本身已经光秃秃、一片荒凉。

===Original===

Hearing a sound in the back garden he hastily decided to delay no longer, but to set out to school at once. He set out as unostentatiously as possible.

Miss Drew, entering her classroom, was aghast to see instead of the usual small array of buttonholes on her desk, a mass of already withering hothouse flowers completely covering her desk and chair.

William was a boy who never did things by halves.

“Good Heavens!” she cried in consternation.

===Chinese===

听见后花园有动静，他当机立断不再耽搁，立刻动身去上学。他尽可能不动声色地出了门。

德鲁小姐走进教室，惊骇地发现，她桌上平日那一小排胸花不见了，取而代之的是一大堆已经蔫萎的温室花朵，把她的书桌和椅子盖了个严严实实。

威廉是个做事从来不留一半的孩子。

“老天爷！”她惊慌失措地叫道。

===Original===

William blushed with pleasure.

He changed his seat to one in the front row. All that morning he sat, his eyes fixed on her earnestly, dreaming of moments in which he rescued her from robbers and pirates (here he was somewhat inconsistent with his own favourite role of robber-chief and pirate), and bore her fainting in his strong arms to safety. Then she clung to him in love and gratitude, and they were married at once by the Archbishops of Canterbury and York.

William would have no half-measures. They were to be married by the Archbishops of Canterbury and York, or else the Pope. He wasn’t sure that he wouldn’t rather have the Pope. He would wear his black pirate suit with the skull and crossbones. No, that would not do﻿—

“What have I just been saying, William?” said Miss Drew.

===Chinese===

威廉高兴得涨红了脸。

他换到了前排的座位。整整一个上午，他坐在那里，目光热切地锁定她，幻想着自己把她从强盗和海盗手里救出来（这一点与他本人最钟爱的强盗头子兼海盗角色略有出入），用有力的臂膀抱着昏厥的她脱险。然后她满怀爱意和感激地依偎着他，他们立刻由坎特伯雷大主教和约克大主教主持成了婚。

威廉不要折中的安排。婚礼就得由坎特伯雷大主教和约克大主教来主持，再不然就教皇。他说不准自己是不是更想要教皇。他要穿上他那件绣着骷髅旗的黑海盗服。不，那不行——

“我刚才讲到哪儿了，威廉？”德鲁小姐说。

===Original===

William coughed and gazed at her soulfully.

“ ’Bout lendin’ money?” he said, hopefully.

“William!” she snapped. “This isn’t an arithmetic lesson. I’m trying to teach you about the Armada.”

“Oh, *that*!” said William brightly and ingratiatingly. “Oh, yes.”

“Tell me something about it.”

===Chinese===

威廉咳嗽一声，深情款款地望着她。

“讲借钱的？”他满怀希望地说。

“威廉！”她厉声道，“这不是算术课。我在给你们讲无敌舰队。”

“哦，*那个*呀！”威廉一脸明朗讨好地说，“哦，是。”

“跟我讲讲它。”

===Original===

“I don’t *know* anything﻿—not jus’ yet﻿—”

“I’ve been *telling* you about it. I do wish you’d listen,” she said despairingly.

William relapsed into silence, nonplussed, but by no means cowed.

When he reached home that evening he found that the garden was the scene of excitement and hubbub. One policeman was measuring the panes of glass in the conservatory door, and another was on his knees examining the beds near. His grown-up sister, Ethel, was standing at the front door.

===Chinese===

“我什么都还——还不知道——”

“我一直在*讲*呢。你要是肯听就好了。”她绝望地说。

威廉重新陷入沉默，一脸窘迫，但绝不屈服。

那天傍晚他回到家，发现花园里一片骚动喧哗。一个警察正在量温室门上的玻璃，另一个跪在近旁检查花坛。他成年的姐姐埃塞尔站在大门口。

===Original===

“Every single flower has been stolen from the conservatory some time this morning,” she said excitedly. “We’ve only just been able to get the police. William, did you see anyone about when you went to school this morning?”

William pondered deeply. His most guileless and innocent expression came to his face.

“No,” he said at last. “No, Ethel, I didn’t see nobody.”

William coughed and discreetly withdrew.

===Chinese===

“今天早上不知什么时候，温室里的花一朵不剩全被偷了，”她激动地说，“我们好不容易才请来警察。威廉，你今天早上上学的时候，看见什么人没有？”

威廉深深地思索。他一脸最天真无邪的表情。

“没有，”他终于说，“没有，埃塞尔，我谁也没看见。”

威廉咳嗽一声，审慎地退下了。

===Original===

That evening he settled down at the library table, spreading out his books around him, a determined frown upon his small face.

His father was sitting in an armchair by the window reading the evening paper.

“Father,” said William suddenly, “s’pose I came to you an’ said you was to give me a hundred pounds an’ I’d give you five pounds next year an’ so on, would you give it me?”

“I should not, my son,” said his father firmly.

===Chinese===

当晚他在书房的书桌前坐定，把书本在四周摊开，小脸上一副下定决心的眉头。

他父亲正坐在窗边的扶手椅里读晚报。

“爸爸，”威廉突然说，“要是我来找您，说您得给我一百镑，我明年给您五镑，往后一直这样，您给不给？”

“不给，我的儿子。”他父亲斩钉截铁。

===Original===

William sighed.

“I knew there was something wrong with it,” he said.

Mr. Brown returned to the leading article, but not for long.

“Father, what was the date of the Armada?”

===Chinese===

威廉叹了口气。

“我就知道这里头有毛病。”他说。

布朗先生回去读社论，可没读多久。

“爸爸，无敌舰队是哪一年的？”

===Original===

“Good Heavens! How should I know? I wasn’t there.”

William sighed.

“Well, I’m tryin’ to write about it and why it failed an’﻿—why did it fail?”

Mr. Brown groaned, gathered up his paper, and retired to the dining-room.

===Chinese===

“老天爷！我怎么会知道？我又不在场。”

威廉叹了口气。

“唉，我正想写它呢，写它为什么失败了——它为什么失败了？”

布朗先生呻吟一声，收起报纸，撤退到饭厅去了。

===Original===

He had almost finished the leading article when William appeared, his arms full of books, and sat down quietly at the table.

“Father, what’s the French for ‘my aunt is walking in the garden’?”

“What on earth are you doing?” said Mr. Brown irritably.

“I’m doing my home-lessons,” said William virtuously.

===Chinese===

社论快读完时，威廉出现了，怀里抱着一大摞书，悄悄在桌边坐下。

“爸爸，『我姨妈正在花园里散步』用法语怎么说？”

“你到底在搞什么名堂？”布朗先生恼火地说。

“我在做家庭作业。”威廉一脸正气地说。

===Original===

“I never even knew you had the things to do.”

“No,” William admitted gently, “I don’t generally take much bother over them, but I’m goin’ to now﻿—’cause Miss Drew”﻿—he blushed slightly and paused﻿—“ ’cause Miss Drew”﻿—he blushed more deeply and began to stammer, “ ’c﻿—cause Miss Drew”﻿—he was almost apoplectic.

Mr. Brown quietly gathered up his paper and crept out to the verandah, where his wife sat with the week’s mending.

“William’s gone raving mad in the dining-room,” he said pleasantly, as he sat down. “Takes the form of a wild thirst for knowledge, and a babbling of a Miss Drawing, or Drew, or something. He’s best left alone.”

===Chinese===

“我都不知道你有这东西要做。”

“是啊，”威廉温和地承认，“我平时不大为它们操心，可我往后要操心了——因为德鲁小姐”——他微微红了脸，顿了顿——“因为德鲁小姐”——他脸红得更深，开始结巴起来，“因——因为德鲁小姐”——他几乎要中风了。

布朗先生悄悄收起报纸，溜到游廊上去，他妻子正坐在那儿做这个星期该缝补的活计。

“威廉在饭厅里发疯了，”他落座时愉快地说，“表现为一种对知识如痴如狂的渴求，嘴里念叨着一位什么德劳英小姐，要不就是德鲁小姐，反正差不多。最好别去理他。”

===Original===

Mrs. Brown merely smiled placidly over the mending.

Mr. Brown had finished one leading article and begun another before William appeared again. He stood in the doorway frowning and stern.

“Father, what’s the capital of Holland?”

“Good Heavens!” said his father. “Buy him an encyclopedia. Anything, anything. What does he think I am? What﻿—”

===Chinese===

布朗太太只对着针线活安详地笑了笑。

布朗先生读完一篇社论、刚开下一篇的头，威廉又出现了。他站在门口，皱着眉，板着脸。

“爸爸，荷兰的首都是哪儿？”

“老天爷！”他父亲说，“给他买本百科全书。什么都行，什么都行。他把我当什么了？什么——”

===Original===

“I’d better set apart a special room for his homework,” said Mrs. Brown soothingly, “now that he’s beginning to take such an interest.”

“A room!” echoed his father bitterly. “He wants a whole house.”

===Chinese===

“我看如今他既然开始这么用功了，”布朗太太抚慰道，“不如专门腾出一间屋子给他做功课。”

“一间屋子！”他父亲悻悻地应道，“他要的是一整座房子。”

===Original===

Miss Drew was surprised and touched by William’s earnestness and attention the next day. At the end of the afternoon school he kindly offered to carry her books home for her. He waved aside all protests. He marched home by her side discoursing pleasantly, his small freckled face beaming devotion.

“I like pirates, don’t you, Miss Drew? An’ robbers an’ things like that? Miss Drew, would you like to be married to a robber?”

He was trying to reconcile his old beloved dream of his future estate with the new one of becoming Miss Drew’s husband.

“No,” she said firmly.

===Chinese===

第二天，德鲁小姐对威廉的热忱和专注又惊讶又感动。下午放学时，他殷勤地提出要替她把书抱回家。他挥手挡开一切推辞。他走在她身边一路谈笑风生，长着雀斑的小脸上焕发着爱慕的光彩。

“我喜欢海盗，您呢，德鲁小姐？还有强盗和诸如此类的？德鲁小姐，您愿意嫁给一个强盗吗？”

他正努力把旧日心爱的前程美梦，跟娶德鲁小姐为妻的新梦调和到一处。

“不愿意。”她斩钉截铁。

===Original===

His heart sank.

“Nor a pirate?” he said sadly.

“No.”

“They’re quite nice really﻿—pirates,” he assured her.

===Chinese===

他的心往下一沉。

“海盗也不行？”他悲哀地说。

“不行。”

“其实海盗挺好的——真的。”他向她保证。

===Original===

“I think not.”

“Well,” he said resignedly, “we’ll jus’ have to go huntin’ wild animals and things. That’ll be all right.”

“Who?” she said, bewildered.

“Well﻿—jus’ you wait,” he said darkly.

===Chinese===

“我看不见得。”

“唉，”他认命地说，“那咱们只好去猎野兽什么的了。那样也挺好。”

“谁？”她茫然地说。

“呃——您等着瞧吧。”他神秘莫测地说。

===Original===

Then: “Would you rather be married by the Archbishop of York or the Pope?”

“The Archbishop, I think,” she said gravely.

He nodded.

“All right.”

===Chinese===

随后又问：“您愿意由约克大主教给您主持婚礼，还是教皇？”

“我想是大主教吧。”她一本正经地说。

他点点头。

“行。”

===Original===

She was distinctly amused. She was less amused the next evening. Miss Drew had a male cousin﻿—a very nice-looking male cousin, with whom she often went for walks in the evening. This evening, by chance, they passed William’s house, and William, who was in the garden, threw aside his temporary role of pirate and joined them. He trotted happily on the other side of Miss Drew. He entirely monopolised the conversation. The male cousin seemed to encourage him, and this annoyed Miss Drew. He refused to depart in spite of Miss Drew’s strong hints. He had various items of interest to impart, and he imparted them with the air of one assured of an appreciative hearing. He had found a dead rat the day before and given it to his dog, but his dog didn’t like ’em dead and neither did the ole cat, so he’d buried it. Did Miss Drew like all those flowers he’d got her the other day? He was afraid that he cudn’t bring any more like that jus’ yet. Were there pirates now? Well, what would folks do to one if there was one? He din’t see why there shun’t be pirates now. He thought he’d start it, anyway. He’d like to shoot a lion. He was goin’ to one day. He’d shoot a lion an’ a tiger. He’d bring the skin home to Miss Drew, if she liked. He grew recklessly generous. He’d bring home lots of skins of all sorts of animals for Miss Drew.

===Chinese===

她分明觉得很有趣。第二天傍晚她就不觉得有趣了。德鲁小姐有一位表哥——一位相貌非常英俊的表哥，她常在傍晚跟他一起散步。这天傍晚，他们恰好路过威廉家，正在花园里的威廉丢下他临时充当的海盗角色，加入了他们。他小跑着待在德鲁小姐的另一侧，快活得很。他把谈话整个儿垄断了。那位表哥似乎还挺鼓励他，这让德鲁小姐很恼火。德鲁小姐重重暗示，他却拒绝离开。他有一肚子有趣的事要讲，而且讲起来一副笃定听众爱听的架势。他前一天捡到一只死老鼠，给了他的狗，可他的狗不爱吃死的，那只老猫也不爱吃，所以他就把它埋了。德鲁小姐喜欢不喜欢前几天他送她的那些花？恐怕那种花他一时半会儿弄不到更多了。现在还有海盗吗？呃，要是有一个的话，大伙儿会拿他怎么办？他不明白为什么现在就不能有海盗。反正他想带头干起来。他想打一头狮子。他总有一天要打的。他要打一头狮子，再打一头老虎。她要是喜欢，他就把那张皮带回家送给德鲁小姐。他越来越大方得出格。他要给德鲁小姐带回家各种各样的兽皮，一大堆。

===Original===

“Don’t you think you ought to be going home, William?” said Miss Drew coldly.

William hastened to reassure her.

“Oh, no﻿—not for ever so long yet,” he said.

===Chinese===

“你不觉得你该回家了吗，威廉？”德鲁小姐冷冷地说。

威廉赶紧宽慰她。

“哦，不——还早得很呢。”他说。

===Original===

“Isn’t it your bedtime?”

“Oh, no﻿—not yet﻿—not for ever so long.”

The male cousin was giving William his whole attention.

“What does Miss Drew teach you at school, William?” he said.

===Chinese===

“不是到你的睡觉时间了吗？”

“哦，不——还没——还早得很。”

那位表哥全副注意力都放在了威廉身上。

“德鲁小姐在学校教你们什么呀，威廉？”他说。

===Original===

“Oh, jus’ ornery things. Armadas an’ things. An’ ’bout lending a hundred pounds. That’s a norful *soft* thing. I unner*stand* it,” he added hastily, fearing further explanation, “but it’s *soft*. My father thinks it is, too, an’ he oughter *know*. He’s bin abroad lots of times. He’s bin chased by a bull, my father has﻿—”

The shades of night were falling fast when William reached Miss Drew’s house still discoursing volubly. He was drunk with success. He interpreted his idol’s silence as the silence of rapt admiration.

He was passing through the gate with his two companions with the air of one assured of welcome, when Miss Drew shut the gate upon him firmly.

===Chinese===

“哦，就是些平常玩意儿。无敌舰队什么的。还有借一百镑的事。那可真是件*犯傻*的事。我听*懂*了，”他赶紧补充，生怕又要被讲解一遍，“可它就是*犯傻*。我爸爸也这么觉得，他应该*懂行*。他出过好多回国。他还被公牛追过，我爸爸——”

夜幕四合时，威廉一路滔滔不绝，走到了德鲁小姐家门口。他陶醉在成功里。他把偶像的沉默，理解成听得入迷的沉默。

他带着两位同伴往院门里走，一副笃定会受到欢迎的架势，不想德鲁小姐却把他干脆地关在了门外。

===Original===

“You’d better go home now, William,” she said.

William hesitated.

“I don’t mind comin’ in a bit,” he said. “I’m not tired.”

But Miss Drew and the male cousin were already halfway up the walk.

===Chinese===

“你现在最好回家去，威廉。”她说。

威廉犹豫了一下。

“我不介意进去坐一会儿，”他说，“我不累。”

可德鲁小姐和那位表哥已经走到小路一半了。

===Original===

William turned his steps homeward. He met Ethel near the gate.

“William, where *have* you been? I’ve been looking for you everywhere. It’s *hours* past your bedtime.”

“I was goin’ a walk with Miss Drew.”

“But you should have come home at your bedtime.”

===Chinese===

威廉转身踏上归途。他在院门附近遇见了埃塞尔。

“威廉，你到底跑哪儿去了？我到处找你。早过你的睡觉时间好几个钟头了。”

“我跟德鲁小姐散步去了。”

“可你到点就该回家呀。”

===Original===

“I don’t think she wanted me to go,” he said with dignity. “I think it wun’t of bin p’lite.”

William found that a new and serious element had entered his life. It was not without its disadvantages. Many had been the little diversions by which William had been wont to while away the hours of instruction. In spite of his devotion to Miss Drew, he missed the old days of carefree exuberance, but he kept his new seat in the front row, and clung to his role of earnest student. He was beginning to find also, that a conscientious performance of home lessons limited his activities after school hours, but at present he hugged his chains. Miss Drew, from her seat on the platform, found William’s soulful concentrated gaze somewhat embarrassing, and his questions even more so.

===Chinese===

“我看她并不想让我走，”他很有尊严地说，“我要是走了，怕是不*礼貌*。”

威廉发现一种新的、严肃的要素进入了他的生活。它并非没有坏处。威廉向来有种种小小的消遣，用来打发上课的钟点。尽管他对德鲁小姐一片痴心，他还是怀念从前无忧无虑、尽情撒欢的日子；但他守着前排的新座位，紧抓着用功学生的角色不放。他还渐渐发现，认认真真做家庭作业，限制了他放学后的活动，不过眼下他甘之如饴地抱着这副枷锁。德鲁小姐坐在讲台上，觉得威廉那深情专注的目光有些叫人窘迫，他提的问题更是如此。

===Original===

As he went out of school he heard her talking to another mistress.

“I’m very fond of syringa,” she was saying. “I’d love to have some.”

William decided to bring her syringa, handfuls of syringa, armfuls of syringa.

He went straight home to the gardener.

===Chinese===

放学走出教室时，他听见她在跟另一位女教师说话。

“我特别喜欢山梅花，”她正说着，“真想有一些。”

威廉决定给她送山梅花，一把一把的山梅花，一抱一抱的山梅花。

他径直回家找园丁。

===Original===

“No, I ain’t got no syringa. Please step off my rose-bed, Mister William. No, there ain’t any syringa in this ’ere garding. I dunno for why. Please leave my ’ose pipe alone, Mister William.”

“Huh!” ejaculated William, scornfully turning away.

He went round the garden. The gardener had been quite right. There were guelder roses everywhere, but no syringa.

He climbed the fence and surveyed the next garden. There were guelder roses everywhere, but no syringa. It must have been some peculiarity in the soil.

===Chinese===

“没有，我没栽什么山梅花。请别踩我的玫瑰花坛，威廉小先生。没有，这座园子里就没有山梅花。我也说不上为什么。请放开我的水管，威廉小先生。”

“哼！”威廉鄙夷地从鼻子里哼出一声，转身走了。

他绕着花园转了一圈。园丁说得一点不错。到处都是雪球花，就是没有山梅花。

他爬上围栏，察看隔壁的花园。到处都是雪球花，就是没有山梅花。想必是土质里有什么特别之处。

===Original===

William strolled down the road, scanning the gardens as he went. All had guelder roses. None had syringa.

Suddenly he stopped.

On a table in the window of a small house at the bottom of the road was a vase of syringa. He did not know who lived there. He entered the garden cautiously. No one was about.

He looked into the room. It was empty. The window was open at the bottom.

===Chinese===

威廉顺着大路溜达，一路扫视各家花园。家家都有雪球花，家家都没有山梅花。

突然，他停住了。

路尾一座小房子的窗台上，摆着一瓶山梅花。他不知道那里住的是谁。他小心翼翼地进了院子。四下无人。

他朝屋里张望。屋里没人。窗户下面开着。

===Original===

He scrambled in, removing several layers of white paint from the windowsill as he did so. He was determined to have that syringa. He took it dripping from the vase, and was preparing to depart, when the door opened and a fat woman appeared upon the threshold. The scream that she emitted at sight of William curdled the very blood in his veins. She dashed to the window, and William, in self-defence, dodged round the table and out of the door. The back door was open, and William blindly fled by it. The fat woman did not pursue. She was leaning out of the window, and her shrieks rent the air.

“Police! Help! Murder! Robbers!”

The quiet little street rang with the raucous sounds.

William felt cold shivers creeping up and down his spine. He was in a small back garden from which he could see no exit.

===Chinese===

他翻窗而入，顺手从窗台上蹭掉了几层白漆。他铁了心要拿到那束山梅花。他从花瓶里把它拎出来，水淋淋的，正准备撤离，房门开了，一个胖女人出现在门口。她一看见威廉发出的那声尖叫，吓得威廉血液都凝固了。她朝窗口冲过来，威廉出于自卫，绕着桌子一闪，夺门而出。后门开着，威廉昏头昏脑地从那儿逃了出去。胖女人没有追。她正探身窗外，尖叫声刺破长空。

“警察！救命！杀人啦！强盗啊！”

安静的小街被这刺耳的声音震得嗡嗡响。

威廉觉得一阵阵寒意顺着脊梁骨爬上爬下。他置身在一个小小的后花园里，看不见出口。

===Original===

Meanwhile the shrieks were redoubled.

“Help! *Help!* *Help!*”

Then came sounds of the front-door opening and men’s voices.

“Hello! Who is it? What is it?”

===Chinese===

与此同时，尖叫声又翻了一倍。

“救命！*救命*！*救命*！”

接着传来前门打开的声音和男人的说话声。

“喂！谁在那儿？怎么回事？”

===Original===

William glared round wildly. There was a henhouse in the corner of the garden, and into this he dashed, tearing open the door and plunging through a mass of flying feathers and angry, disturbed hens.

William crouched in a corner of the dark henhouse determinedly clutching his bunch of syringa.

Distant voices were at first all he could hear. Then they came nearer, and he heard the fat lady’s voice loudly declaiming.

“He was quite a small man, but with such an evil face. I just had one glimpse of him as he dashed past me. I’m sure he’d have murdered me if I hadn’t cried for help. Oh, the coward! And a poor defenceless woman! He was standing by the silver table. I disturbed him at his work of crime. I feel so upset. I shan’t sleep for nights. I shall see his evil, murderous face. And a poor unarmed woman!”

===Chinese===

威廉发疯似的四下张望。花园角落里有一间鸡舍，他一头冲了进去，扯开鸡舍门，穿过一团乱飞的鸡毛和一群怒气冲冲、被搅了清梦的母鸡。

威廉蜷在黑洞洞的鸡舍一角，死死攥着他那束山梅花。

起初他只能听见远远的人声。随后人声近了，他听见那胖女士高声滔滔的控诉。

“那是个挺矮小的男人，可那张脸邪气透了。他从我面前冲过去时，我就瞥了一眼。我要不是喊了救命，他准把我杀了。哦，这个懦夫！对手还是个手无寸铁的女人！他当时就站在摆银器的桌子旁边。我搅了他的犯罪勾当。我心里乱极了。多少个晚上都睡不着了。我眼前全是那张凶神恶煞的杀人脸。对手还是个手无寸铁的女人！”

===Original===

“Can you give us no details, madam?” said a man’s voice. “Could you recognise him again?”

“*Anywhere!*” she said firmly. “Such a criminal face. You’ve no idea how upset I am. I might have been a lifeless corpse now, if I hadn’t had the courage to cry for help.”

“We’re measuring the footprints, madam. You say he went out by the front door?”

“I’m convinced he did. I’m convinced he’s hiding in the bushes by the gate. Such a low face. My nerves are absolutely jarred.”

===Chinese===

“您能提供些细节吗，夫人？”一个男人的声音说，“您还能认出他来吗？”

“*走到哪儿都认得出*！”她斩钉截铁，“那么一张罪犯的脸。您不知道我乱成什么样。我要不是鼓起勇气喊救命，这会儿已经是一具冰凉的尸首了。”

“我们在量脚印，夫人。您说他是从前门出去的？”

“我敢肯定。我敢肯定他就藏在大门边的树丛里。那么一张下作的脸。我的神经全震坏了。”

===Original===

“We’ll search the bushes again, madam,” said the other voice wearily, “but I expect he has escaped by now.”

“The brute!” said the fat lady. “Oh, the *brute*! And that *face*. If I hadn’t had the courage to cry out﻿—”

The voices died away and William was left alone in a corner of the henhouse.

A white hen appeared in the little doorway, squawked at him angrily, and retired, cackling indignation. Visions of lifelong penal servitude or hanging passed before William’s eyes. He’d rather be executed, really. He hoped they’d execute him.

===Chinese===

“我们再搜一遍树丛，夫人，”另一个声音疲惫地说，“不过我看他这会儿早跑了。”

“畜生！”胖女士说，“哦，这个*畜生*！还有那张*脸*。要不是我鼓起勇气喊出声——”

人声渐渐远去，威廉独自留在鸡舍的一角。

一只白母鸡出现在小门口，冲他愤怒地咯咯一叫，退了回去，愤愤不平地咕哒个不停。终身苦役或者绞刑的图景在威廉眼前一一掠过。真要说起来，他宁愿被处决。他希望他们把他处决算了。

===Original===

Then he heard the fat lady bidding goodbye to the policeman. Then she came to the back garden evidently with a friend, and continued to pour forth her troubles.

“And he *dashed* past me, dear. Quite a small man, but with such an evil face.”

A black hen appeared in the little doorway, and with an angry squawk at William, returned to the back garden.

“I think you’re *splendid*, dear,” said the invisible friend. “How you had the *courage*.”

===Chinese===

随后他听见胖女士跟警察道别。接着她显然是同一位朋友来到了后花园，继续倾诉她的劫难。

“他*冲*过我面前的时候，亲爱的。一个挺矮小的男人，可那张脸邪气透了。”

一只黑母鸡出现在小门口，冲威廉愤怒地咯咯一叫，回后花园去了。

“我觉得您真是*了不起*，亲爱的，”那位看不见的朋友说，“您哪来的这份*胆量*。”

===Original===

The white hen gave a sardonic scream.

“You’d better come in and rest, darling,” said the friend.

“I’d better,” said the fat lady in a plaintive, suffering voice. “I do feel very﻿ ﻿… shaken.﻿ ﻿…”

Their voices ceased, the door was closed, and all was still.

===Chinese===

白母鸡发出一声冷嘲的尖叫。

“您最好进来歇歇，宝贝。”朋友说。

“我也正想呢。”胖女士用哀婉的、受苦受难的声音说，“我真是觉得非常……受惊了。……”

说话声停了，屋门关上，万籁俱寂。

===Original===

Cautiously, very cautiously, a much-dishevelled William crept from the henhouse and round the side of the house. Here he found a locked side-gate over which he climbed, and very quietly he glided down to the front gate and to the road.

“Where’s William this evening?” said Mrs. Brown. “I do hope he won’t stay out after his bedtime.”

“Oh, I’ve just met him,” said Ethel. “He was going up to his bedroom. He was covered with hen feathers and holding a bunch of syringa.”

“Mad!” sighed his father. “Mad! mad! mad!”

===Chinese===

小心翼翼地，万分小心地，一个蓬头垢面的威廉从鸡舍里爬出来，绕过房子侧面。他在那儿发现一道上了锁的边门，翻了过去，然后悄没声儿地溜到大门，溜到街上。

“威廉今晚到哪儿去了？”布朗太太说，“真希望他别过了睡觉时间还在外头待着。”

“哦，我刚碰见他了，”埃塞尔说，“他正上楼回卧室去呢。他浑身都是鸡毛，手里还攥着一束山梅花。”

“疯子！”他父亲叹道，“疯子！疯子！疯子！”

===Original===

The next morning William laid a bunch of syringa upon Miss Drew’s desk. He performed the offering with an air of quiet, manly pride. Miss Drew recoiled.

“*Not* syringa, William. I simply can’t *bear* the smell!”

William gazed at her in silent astonishment for a few moments.

Then: “But you *said*﻿ ﻿… you *said*﻿ ﻿… you said you were fond of syringa an’ that you’d like to have them.”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，威廉把一束山梅花放在德鲁小姐的桌上。他行这献礼时带着一种安静的、有男子气概的自豪。德鲁小姐直往后缩。

“*别*拿山梅花，威廉。这个味道我简直*受不了*！”

威廉默默惊愕地瞪了她好一会儿。

然后：“可您*说过*……您*说过*……您说过您喜欢山梅花，还说想要一些的。”

===Original===

“Did I say syringa?” said Miss Drew vaguely. “I meant guelder roses.”

William’s gaze was one of stony contempt.

He went slowly back to his old seat at the back of the room.

That evening he made a bonfire with several choice friends, and played Red Indians in the garden. There was a certain thrill in returning to the old life.

===Chinese===

“我说的是山梅花吗？”德鲁小姐迷迷糊糊地说，“我说的是雪球花。”

威廉的目光成了一种石头般的轻蔑。

他慢慢走回教室后排他的老座位。

当晚，他同几位精选的好友生了一堆篝火，在花园里扮印第安人玩。重返旧日生活自有一种快意。

===Original===

“Hello!” said his father, encountering William creeping on all fours among the bushes. “I thought you did home lessons now?”

William arose to an upright position.

“I’m not goin’ to take much bother over ’em now,” said William. “Miss Drew, she can’t talk straight. She dunno what she *means*.”

“That’s always the trouble with women,” agreed his father. “William says his idol has feet of clay,” he said to his wife, who had approached.

“I dunno as she’s got feet of clay,” said William, the literal. “All I say is she can’t talk straight. I took no end of trouble an’ she dunno what she means. I think her feet’s all right. She walks all right. ’Sides, when they make folks false feet, they make ’em of wood, not clay.”

===Chinese===

“喂！”他父亲撞见正四肢着地在树丛里匍匐的威廉，“我以为你现在做家庭作业了呢？”

威廉直起了身。

“我往后不为它们多操心了，”威廉说，“德鲁小姐，她说话不算数。她自己都不知道自己*什么意思*。”

“女人向来有这毛病，”他父亲深表赞同，“威廉说他的偶像有一双泥足。”他对走过来的妻子说。

“我可没说她有泥足，”威廉说得实打实，“我只说她说话不算数。我费了天大的劲，她自己都不知道自己什么意思。我看她的脚好好的。她走路好好的。再说，人家给装假脚，用的也是木头，不是泥。”

## VIII The Outlaws / VIII 亡命徒帮

===Original===
It was a half-holiday and William was in his bedroom making careful preparations for the afternoon. On the mantelpiece stood in readiness half a cake (the result of a successful raid on the larder) and a bottle of licorice water. This beverage was made by shaking up a piece of licorice in water. It was much patronised by the band of Outlaws to which William belonged and which met secretly every half-holiday in a disused barn about a quarter of a mile from William’s house.

So far the Outlaws had limited their activities to wrestling matches, adventure seeking, and culinary operations. The week before, they had cooked two sausages which William had taken from the larder on cook’s night out and had conveyed to the barn beneath his shirt and next his skin. Perhaps “cooked” is too euphemistic a term. To be quite accurate, they had held the sausages over a smoking fire till completely blackened, and then consumed the charred remains with the utmost relish.

William put the bottle of licorice water in one pocket and the half cake in another and was preparing to leave the house in his usual stealthy fashion﻿—through the bathroom window, down the scullery roof, and down the water-pipe hand over hand to the back garden. Even when unencumbered by the presence of a purloined half cake, William infinitely preferred this mode of exit to the simpler one of walking out of the front-door. As he came out on to the landing, however, he heard the sound of the opening and shutting of the hall door and of exuberant greetings in the hall.

===Chinese===
这天放半日假，威廉正在自己的卧室里为下午的行动精心做着准备。壁炉台上整整齐齐地摆着半块蛋糕（一次成功突袭食品储藏室的战果）和一瓶甘草水。这种饮料的制法，是把一小截甘草放进水里使劲摇匀。威廉所属的亡命徒帮对它青睐有加——这个帮派每逢半日假都要在一座废弃谷仓里秘密集会，谷仓离威廉家约有四分之一英里。

到目前为止，亡命徒帮的活动还仅限于摔跤比赛、寻访奇遇和烹饪作业。上礼拜他们煮了两根香肠——那是厨娘休假那晚威廉从食品储藏室里顺出来的，贴着皮肉藏在衬衫底下运到了谷仓。说「煮」也许太过委婉了。准确地说，他们把香肠架在一堆冒烟的火上，直到完全焦黑，然后津津有味地把那堆炭化残骸吃了个精光。

威廉把那瓶甘草水装进一个口袋，半块蛋糕装进另一个口袋，正准备照他惯用的隐秘路线溜出家门——翻浴室窗户，下洗碗间的屋顶，再顺着水管一把一把地落到后花园。即便身上没有这半块顺来的蛋糕拖累，威廉也觉得这种出门方式远远胜过走前门那种平淡无奇的路数。可是他刚走到楼梯口，就听见大厅的门一开一关，接着是大厅里热烈洋溢的寒暄声。

===Original===
“Oh! I’m *so* glad you’ve come, dear. And is this the baby! The *duck*! Well, den, how’s ’oo, den? Go﻿—o﻿—oo.”

This was William’s mother.

“Oh, crumbs!” said William and retreated hastily. He sat down on his bed to wait till the coast was clear. Soon came the sound of footsteps ascending the stairs.

“Oh, William,” said his mother, as she entered his room, “Mrs. Butler’s come with her baby to spend the afternoon, and we’d arranged to go out till teatime with the baby, but she’s got such a headache, I’m insisting on her lying down for the afternoon in the drawing-room. But she’s so worried about the baby not getting out this nice afternoon.”

===Chinese===
「噢！你可来了，我真高兴，亲爱的。这就是宝宝吧！小乖乖！来，让阿姨瞧瞧，宝宝好呀？乖——哦——哦。」

这是威廉的母亲。

「噢，我的天！」威廉说着赶紧撤了回去。他坐到床上，等着「敌情」解除。不一会儿，传来了上楼的脚步声。

「噢，威廉，」她走进房间时说，「巴特勒太太带着宝宝来过一下午，我们本来商量好带宝宝出去逛到喝茶的时候，可她头疼得厉害，我正劝她在客厅里躺一下午呢。可她又愁着，今天下午天气这么好，宝宝出不了门。」

===Original===
“Oh!” said William, without interest.

“Well, cook’s out and Emma has to get the tea and answer the door, and Ethel’s away, and I told Mrs. Butler I was *sure* you wouldn’t mind taking the baby out for a bit in the perambulator!”

William stared at her, speechless. The Medusa’s classic expression of horror was as nothing to William’s at that moment. Then he moistened his lips and spoke in a hoarse voice.

“*Me?*” he said. “*Me?* *Me* take a baby out in a pram?”

“Well, dear,” said his mother deprecatingly, “I know it’s your half holiday, but you’d be out of doors getting the fresh air, which is the great thing. It’s a nice baby and a nice pram and not heavy to push, and Mrs. Butler would be *so* grateful to you.”

===Chinese===
「哦！」威廉应了一声，毫无兴趣。

「是这样，厨娘出去了，埃玛要备茶点还要应门，埃塞尔又不在家，我已经跟巴特勒太太说了，你*肯定*不会介意推着婴儿车带宝宝出去遛一小会儿的！」

威廉瞪着她，说不出话来。美杜莎那副经典的惊骇表情，比起此刻威廉的表情简直不算什么。他舔了舔嘴唇，用嘶哑的嗓音开了口。

「*我*？」他说。「*我*？要*我*推着婴儿车带宝宝出去？」

「哎呀，亲爱的，」母亲带着息事宁人的口气说，「我知道这是你的半日假，可你能到户外去呼吸新鲜空气呀，这才是顶要紧的。宝宝又乖，车又好，推起来不沉，巴特勒太太还会*那么*感激你呢。」

===Original===
“Yes, I should think she’d be that,” said William bitterly. “She’d have a right to be that if I took the baby out in a pram.”

“Now, William, I’m sure you’d like to help, and I’m sure you wouldn’t like your father to hear that you wouldn’t even do a little thing like that for poor Mrs. Butler. And she’s got such a headache.”

“*A little thing like that!*” repeated William out of the bitterness of his soul.

But the Fates were closing round him. He was aware that he would know no peace till he had done the horrible thing demanded of him. Sorrowfully and reluctantly he bowed to the inevitable.

“All right,” he muttered, “I’ll be down in a minute.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，她该感激，这我也想到了。」威廉恨恨地说，「要是我推着婴儿车带宝宝出去，她确实有权利感激个够。」

「好了，威廉，我敢说你肯定愿意帮忙的，我也敢说你肯定不愿意让你爸爸听说，你连这么一丁点儿小事都不肯为可怜的巴特勒太太做。再说她还头疼得那么厉害。」

「*这么一丁点儿小事*！」威廉从灵魂深处的苦涩里重复了一遍。

可是命运之神正在向他合围。他心里明白，不干完这件强加于他的可怕差事，他休想有一天安宁。他悲悲切切、勉勉强强地向不可抗拒的命运低了头。

「行吧，」他咕哝道，「我马上就下去。」

===Original===
He heard them fussing over the baby in the hall. Then he heard his elder brother’s voice.

“You surely don’t mean to say, mother,” Robert was saying with the crushing superiority of eighteen, “that you’re going to trust that child to﻿—William.”

“Well,” said William’s mother, “someone has to take him out. It’s such a lovely afternoon. I’m sure it’s very kind of William, on his half-holiday, too. And she’s got *such* a headache.”

“Well, of course,” said Robert in the voice of one who washes his hands of all further responsibility, “you know William as well as I do.”

“Oh, dear!” sighed William’s mother. “And everything so nicely settled, Robert, and you must come and find fault with it all. If you don’t want William to take him out, will you take him out yourself?”

===Chinese===
他听见他们在门厅里围着宝宝忙乱。接着，他听见了哥哥的声音。

「您该不会是想说，妈妈，」罗伯特正以十八岁少年那种压倒一切的优越感说着，「您居然打算把那孩子托付给——威廉。」

「话不能这么说，」威廉的母亲说，「总得有人带他出去呀。下午天气多好。威廉肯帮忙真是太好了，何况还是在他的半日假里。再说她头疼得*那么*厉害。」

「好吧，当然了，」罗伯特用一种从此撒手不管一切责任的口气说，「威廉是什么人，您跟我一样清楚。」

「哎呀，老天！」威廉的母亲叹了口气，「什么事都安排得妥妥当当的，罗伯特，你偏要跑来挑刺。你要是不想让威廉带他出去，那你自己带他出去好不好？」

===Original===
Robert retreated hastily to the dining-room and continued the conversation from a distance.

“I don’t want to take him out myself﻿—thanks very much, all the same! All I say is﻿—you know William as well as I do. I’m not finding fault with anything. I simply am stating a fact.”

Then William came downstairs.

“Here he is, dear, all ready for you, and you needn’t go far away﻿—just up and down the road, if you like, but stay out till teatime. He’s a dear little baby, isn’t he? And isn’t it a nice Willy-Billy den, to take it out a nice ta-ta, while it’s mummy goes bye-byes, den?”

William blushed for pure shame.

===Chinese===
罗伯特慌忙撤进饭厅，隔着一段距离继续这场谈话。

「我自己可不想带他出去——不过还是非常感谢！我只是说——威廉是什么人，您跟我一样清楚。我不是在挑什么刺。我只不过是在陈述一个事实。」

于是威廉下了楼。

「他来啦，亲爱的，一切都给你准备好啦。你不用走远——就在这条路上来回遛遛就行，不过要待到喝茶的时候再回来。他是个可心的小宝贝，是不是？小威威多好呀，推着车车带宝宝去散散步，宝宝的妈妈睡觉觉，嗯？」

威廉纯粹是羞红了脸。

===Original===
He pushed the pram down to the end of the road and round the corner. In comparison with William’s feelings, the feelings of some of the early martyrs must have been pure bliss. A nice way for an Outlaw to spend the afternoon! He dreaded to meet any of his brother-outlaws, yet, irresistibly and as a magnet, their meeting-place attracted him. He wheeled the pram off the road and down the country lane towards the field which held their sacred barn. He stopped at the stile that led into the field and gazed wistfully across to the barn in the distance. The infant sat and sucked its thumb and stared at him. Finally it began to converse.

“Blab﻿—blab﻿—blab﻿—blab﻿—blub﻿—blub﻿—blub!”

“Oh, you shut up!” said William crushingly.

Annoyed at the prolonged halt, it seized its pram cover, pulled it off its hooks, and threw it into the road. While William was picking it up, it threw the pillow on to his head. Then it chuckled. William began to conceive an active dislike of it. Suddenly the Great Idea came to him. His face cleared. He took a piece of string from his pocket and tied the pram carefully to the railings. Then, lifting the baby cautiously and gingerly out, he climbed the stile with it and set off across the fields towards the barn. He held the baby to his chest with both arms clasped tightly round its waist. Its feet dangled in the air. It occupied the time by kicking William in the stomach, pulling his hair, and putting its fingers in his eyes.

“It beats me,” panted William to himself, “what people see in babies! Scratchin’ an’ kickin’ and blindin’ folks and pullin’ their hair all out!”

===Chinese===
他推着婴儿车一直推到路的尽头，再拐过街角。与威廉此刻的心情相比，几位早期殉道者的心情想必都算得上纯粹的幸福了。一个亡命徒的下午居然能这么打发！他生怕撞见帮里任何一位弟兄，可他们的聚会地点又像磁石一般，教他身不由己地被吸引了过去。他把婴儿车推下大路，拐进乡间小道，朝他们那座神圣谷仓所在的田野走去。他在通向田里的栅栏阶梯前停下，怅然望着远处那座谷仓。小宝宝坐在车里吮着大拇指，盯着他瞧。终于，它开口攀谈起来。

「叭——叭——叭——叭——啵——啵——啵！」

「噢，你给我闭嘴！」威廉以毁灭性的口吻说。

被这没完没了的停留惹恼了，宝宝一把抓住车毯，从挂钩上扯下来，扔到路当中。威廉去捡的时候，它又把小枕头扣到他脑袋上。然后它咯咯直乐。威廉开始对它生出一种切切实实的厌恶。突然，伟大的灵感降临了。他脸上的阴云一扫而空。他从口袋里掏出一截绳子，把婴儿车仔仔细细地拴在栏杆上。然后他小心翼翼、轻手轻脚地把宝宝抱出来，抱着它翻过栅栏阶梯，动身穿过田野朝谷仓走去。他把宝宝搂在胸前，双臂紧紧箍住它的腰。它两只小脚悬在半空，一路忙着踢威廉的肚子、薅他的头发、把手指头往他眼睛里戳。

「真是想不通，」威廉气喘吁吁地自言自语，「人们到底看上娃娃哪一点了！又挠又踢，还把人眼睛戳瞎、头发薅光！」

===Original===
When he entered the barn he was greeted by a sudden silence.

“Look here!” began one outlaw in righteous indignation.

“It’s a kidnap,” said William, triumphantly. “We’ll get a ransom on it.”

They gazed at him in awed admiration. This was surely the cream of outlawry. He set the infant on the ground, where it toddled for a few steps and sat down suddenly and violently. It then stared fixedly at the tallest boy present and smiled seraphically.

“Dad﻿—dad﻿—dad﻿—dad﻿—dad!”

Douglas, the tallest boy, grinned sheepishly. “It thinks I’m its father,” he explained complacently to the company.

===Chinese===
他一进谷仓，迎接他的是一阵突如其来的寂静。

「喂，我说！」一个小亡命徒义愤填膺地开了腔。

「这是绑票，」威廉得意洋洋地说，「咱们能拿它换赎金。」

众弟兄以敬畏倾慕的目光凝望着他。这无疑是亡命徒事业的登峰造极之作了。他把宝宝放到地上，它摇摇摆摆走了几步，猛地一下坐了个屁股蹲儿。然后它死死盯住在场个子最高的那个男孩，露出天使般圣洁的微笑。

「爸——爸——爸——爸——爸！」

个子最高的道格拉斯不好意思地咧嘴笑了。「它以为我是它爸爸。」他洋洋自得地向众人解释。

===Original===
“Well,” said Henry, who was William’s rival for the leadership of the Outlaws, “What do we do first? That’s the question.”

“In books,” said the outlaw called Ginger, “they write a note to its people and say they want a ransom.”

“We won’t do that﻿—not just yet,” said William hastily.

“Well, it’s not much sense holdin’ somethin’ up to ransom and not tellin’ the folks that they’ve got to pay nor nothin’, is it?” said Ginger with the final air of a man whose logic is unassailable.

“N﻿—oo,” said William. “But﻿—” with a gleam of hope﻿—“who’s got a paper and pencil? I’m simply statin’ a fact. Who’s got a paper and pencil?”

===Chinese===
「那么，」亨利说——他是威廉争夺亡命徒帮领导权的对手，「咱们先干什么？这才是问题。」

「书里是这么写的，」那个叫金杰的小亡命徒说，「他们给它家里写张条子，说他们要一笔赎金。」

「咱们先不这么干——暂时先不。」威廉急忙说。

「那么，扣着东西要赎金，又不告诉人家他们得掏钱什么的，这也没多大道理，是不是？」金杰带着一种逻辑无懈可击的人的拍板口气说。

「不——哦，」威廉说，「不过——」他眼中闪过一线希望——「谁有纸和笔？我只是在陈述事实。谁有纸和笔？」

===Original===
No one spoke.

“Oh, yes!” went on William in triumph. “Go on! Write a note. Write a note without paper and pencil, and we’ll all watch. Huh!”

“Well,” said Ginger sulkily, “I don’t s’pose they had paper and pencils in outlaw days. They weren’t invented. They wrote on﻿—on﻿—on leaves or something,” he ended vaguely.

“Well, go on. Write on leaves,” said William still more triumphant. “We’re not stoppin’ you are we? I’m simply statin’ a fact. Write on leaves.”

They were interrupted by a yell of pain from Douglas. Flattered by the parental relations so promptly established by the baby, he had ventured to make its further acquaintance. With vague memories of his mother’s treatment of infants, he had inserted a finger in its mouth. The infant happened to possess four front teeth, two upper and two lower, and they closed like a vice upon Douglas’ finger. He was now examining the marks.

===Chinese===
没人吭声。

「噢，对了！」威廉得意地接着说，「写呀！写张条子呀。不用纸和笔写出张条子来，咱们大家都瞧着呢。哼！」

「哼，」金杰绷着脸说，「我看亡命徒那个年代也没有纸和笔。还没发明出来呢。他们都是写在——写在——写在树叶子上什么的。」他含含糊糊地收了尾。

「那好啊，写呀。往树叶子上写呀。」威廉越发得意了，「我们又没拦着你，是不是？我只是在陈述事实。往树叶子上写呀。」

一阵痛苦的惨叫打断了他们。道格拉斯被宝宝这么快就确立起来的亲子关系捧得飘飘然，竟大着胆子同它进一步亲近。他凭着对自己母亲哄娃娃办法的模糊记忆，把一根手指伸进了它嘴里。不巧这宝宝恰好长了四颗门牙，上两颗下两颗，像老虎钳一样咬住了道格拉斯的手指。他这会儿正在检视那些牙印。
===Original===
“Look! Right deep down! See it? Wotcher think of that! Nearly to the bone! Pretty savage baby you’ve brought along,” he said to William.

“I jolly well know that,” said William feelingly. “It’s your own fault for touching it. It’s all right if you leave it alone. Just don’t touch it, that’s all. Anyway, it’s mine, and I never said you could go fooling about with it, did I? It wouldn’t bite *me*, I bet!”

“Well, what about the ransom?” persisted Henry.

“Someone can go and tell its people and bring back the ransom,” suggested Ginger.

There was a short silence. Then Douglas took his injured finger from his mouth and asked pertinently:

“Who?”

===Chinese===
「瞧！咬得多深！看见没？你觉得这怎么样！都快见骨头了！你弄来的这个宝宝可真够凶的。」他对威廉说。

「这一点我太清楚了。」威廉言之凿凿地说，「谁叫你去碰它的。你不去惹它就没事。别碰它，就这么简单。再说，它是我的，我又没说过你可以拿它瞎胡闹，是不是？它就绝不会咬*我*，我敢打赌！」

「那赎金到底怎么办？」亨利不依不饶。

「可以派个人去告诉它家里人，再把赎金带回来。」金杰提议。

短短一阵沉默。然后道格拉斯把受伤的手指从嘴里拿出来，一针见血地问：

「派谁？」

===Original===
“William brought it,” suggested Henry.

“Yes, so I bet I’ve done my share.”

“Well, what’s anyone else goin’ to do, I’d like to know? Go round to every house in this old place and ask if they’ve had a baby taken off them and if they’d pay a ransom for it back? That’s sense, isn’t it? You know where you got it from, don’t you, and you can go and get its ransom.”

“I can, but I’m not goin’ to,” said William finally. “I’m simply statin’ a fact. I’m not goin’ to. And if anyone says I daren’t,” (glancing round pugnaciously) “I’ll fight ’em for it.”

No one said he daren’t. The fact was too patent to need stating. Henry hastily changed the subject.

===Chinese===
「威廉弄来的。」亨利提议。

「是啊，所以敢说我这份儿已经干完了。」

「那么，倒要请教，别人又该怎么干？」亨利反唇相讥，「挨家挨户去问：你们家丢没丢娃娃、肯不肯出赎金赎回去？这叫有脑子，是不是？你知道它是从哪儿弄来的，不是吗，那你就自己去把赎金要回来呀。」

「我能去，可我不去。」威廉最后说，「我只是在陈述事实。我就是不去。谁要是说我不敢去——」他虎视眈眈地环视一圈——「我就为这个跟他干一架。」

没有人说他不敢。事实明摆着，无须陈述。亨利赶紧换了话题。

===Original===
“Anyway, what have we brought for the feast?”

William produced his licorice water and half cake, Douglas two slices of raw ham and a dog biscuit, Ginger some popcorn and some cold boiled potatoes wrapped up in newspaper, Henry a cold apple dumpling and a small bottle of paraffin-oil.

“I knew the wood would be wet after the rain. It’s to make the fire burn. That’s sense, isn’t it?”

“Only one thing to cook,” said Ginger sadly, looking at the slices of ham.

===Chinese===
「先别管这个，咱们宴席上吃的东西都带了什么？」

威廉掏出了他的甘草水和半块蛋糕，道格拉斯拿出两片生火腿和一块狗饼干，金杰拿出一些爆米花和几个用报纸包着的冷土豆，亨利拿出一个凉苹果团子和一小瓶煤油。

「我就知道下过雨柴火准是湿的。这是用来让火烧得旺的。这才叫有脑子，是不是？」

「能煮的只有一样东西。」金杰望着那几片火腿，黯然神伤。

===Original===
“We can cook up the potatoes and the dumpling. They don’t look half enough cooked. Let’s put them on the floor here, and go out for adventures first. All different ways and back in a quarter of an hour.”

The Outlaws generally spent part of the afternoon dispersed in search of adventure. So far they had wooed the Goddess of Danger chiefly by trespassing on the ground of irascible farmers in hopes of a chase which were generally fulfilled.

They deposited their store on the ground in a corner of the barn, and with a glance at the “kidnap,” who was seated happily upon the floor engaged in chewing its hat-strings, they went out, carefully closing the door.

After a quarter of an hour Ginger and William arrived at the door simultaneously from opposite directions.

===Chinese===
「土豆和团子还可以再煮一煮。它们看上去半生不熟的。咱们先把它们搁在这地上，先出去探险。各走各的路，一刻钟后回来。」

亡命徒帮通常会把下午的一部分时间分散开来，各自寻访奇遇。到目前为止，他们向危险女神求爱的主要方式是擅闯脾气暴躁的农场主的地界，指望被追上一场——而这指望一般都能实现。

他们把全部家当堆在谷仓一个角落的地上，瞥了一眼那「肉票」——它正美滋滋地坐在地上，专心嚼着自己的帽带儿——然后出了门，仔细把门带上。

一刻钟后，金杰和威廉从相反的方向同时到了门口。

===Original===
“Any luck?”

“No.”

“Same here. Let’s start the old fire going.”

They opened the door and went in. The infant was sitting on the floor among the stores, or rather among what was left of the stores. There was paraffin-oil on its hair, face, arms, frock and feet. It was drenched in paraffin-oil. The empty bottle and its hat lay by its side. Mingled with the paraffin-oil all over its person was cold boiled potato. It was holding the apple-dumpling in its hand.

“Ball!” it announced ecstatically from behind its mask of potato and paraffin-oil.

===Chinese===
「有收获吗？」

「没有。」

「我也一样。咱们把老火生起来吧。」

他们推门进去。宝宝正坐在家当中间——更准确地说，是坐在家当的残骸中间。它的头发上、脸上、胳膊上、裙子上、脚上全是煤油。它从头到脚浇透了煤油。空瓶子和它的帽子躺在它身边。它浑身上下的煤油里还和着冷土豆泥。它手里攥着那个苹果团子。

「球球！」它隔着那张土豆加煤油的面具，狂喜地宣布。

===Original===
They stood in silence for a minute. Then, “Who’s going to make that fire burn now?” said Ginger, glaring at the empty bottle.

“Yes,” said William slowly, “an’ who’s goin’ to take that baby home? I’m simply statin’ a fact. Who’s goin’ to take that baby home?”

There was no doubt that when William condescended to adopt a phrase from any of his family’s vocabularies, he considerably overworked it.

“Well, it did it itself. It’s no one else’s fault, is it?”

“No, it’s not,” said William. “But that’s the sort of thing folks never see. Anyway, I’m goin’ to wash its face.”

===Chinese===
他们默默站了一分钟。然后，「现在这火可怎么生得着？」金杰瞪着那个空瓶子说。

「是啊，」威廉慢吞吞地说，「还有，谁把这宝宝送回家？我只是在陈述事实。谁把这宝宝送回家？」

毫无疑问，每当威廉肯屈尊从家里哪一位的词汇表里借来一句话，他总要把这句话用过头好几倍。

「得了，是它自己弄的。又不赖别人，是不是？」

「对，不赖。」威廉说，「可这种事大人们从来就是看不见的。反正，我要给它洗脸。」

===Original===
“What with?”

William took out his grimy handkerchief and advanced upon his prey. His bottle of licorice water was lying untouched in the corner. He took out the cork.

“Goin’ to wash it in that dirty stuff?”

“It’s made of water﻿—clean water﻿—I made it myself, so I bet I ought to know, oughtn’t I? That’s what folks wash in, isn’t it?﻿—clean water?”

“Yes,” bitterly, “and what are we goin’ to drink, I’d like to know? You’d think that baby had got enough of our stuff﻿—our potatoes and our apple-dumpling, an’ our oil﻿—without you goin’ an’ givin’ it our licorice water as well.”

William was passing his handkerchief, moistened with licorice water, over the surface of the baby’s face. The baby had caught a corner of it firmly between its teeth and refused to release it.

===Chinese===
「拿什么洗？」

威廉掏出他那条脏手帕，向他的猎物步步逼近。他那瓶甘草水还原封不动地躺在角落里。他拔出了瓶塞。

「打算拿那脏玩意儿给它洗？」

「它是水做的——干净水——我自己做的，所以我敢说我该清楚，是不是？人们洗脸用的不就是它吗——干净水？」

「是啊，」金杰恨恨地说，「可咱们喝什么，我倒要请问？照理说那宝宝把我们东西祸害得够多了——我们的土豆、我们的苹果团子、还有我们的油——哪还用得着你再把我们的甘草水也搭给它。」

威廉正用蘸湿了甘草水的手帕擦拭宝宝的脸。宝宝一口咬住手帕的一角，死也不肯松开。

===Original===
“If you’d got to take this baby home like this,” he said, “you wouldn’t be thinking much about drinking licorice water. I’m simply statin’﻿—”

“Oh, shut up saying that!” said Ginger in sudden exasperation. “I’m sick of it.”

At that moment the door was flung open and in walked slowly a large cow closely followed by Henry and Douglas.

Henry’s face was one triumphant beam. He felt that his prestige, eclipsed by William’s kidnapping coup, was restored.

“I’ve brought a cow,” he announced, “fetched it all the way from Farmer Litton’s field﻿—five fields off, too, an’ it took some fetching, too.”

===Chinese===
「要是得由你把这宝宝这样子送回家，」他说，「你就顾不上琢磨喝甘草水的事了。我只是在陈述——」

「噢，别再说那句了！」金杰突然火冒三丈，「我都听吐了。」

就在这时，门被猛地推开，一头大母牛慢悠悠地踱了进来，后面紧跟着亨利和道格拉斯。

亨利的脸上焕发着得胜的光辉。他觉得自己的威望——先前被威廉的绑票奇功压下去的那份威望——如今又恢复了。

「我弄来一头牛。」他宣布，「一路从利顿农场主的地里牵来的——隔着五块地呢，可费了老鼻子劲才牵来。」

===Original===
“Well, what for?” said William after a moment’s silence.

Henry gave a superior laugh.

“What for! You’ve not read much about outlaws, I guess. They always drove in cattle from the surroundin’ districks.”

“Well, what for?” said William again, giving a tug at his handkerchief, which the infant still refused to release.

“Well﻿—er﻿—well﻿—to kill an’ roast, I suppose,” said Henry lamely.

===Chinese===
「那么，干什么用？」威廉沉默了片刻，说。

亨利居高临下地笑了笑。

「干什么用！我看亡命徒的书你没读过几本。他们总是把周围各处的牲口都赶进来。」

「那么，干什么用？」威廉又问了一遍，使劲拽了拽他的手帕，宝宝还是不肯松口。

「这个——呃——这个——大概是杀了烤着吃吧。」亨利底气不足地说。

===Original===
“Well, go on,” said William. “Kill it an’ roast it. We’re not stoppin’ you, are we? Kill it an’ roast it﻿—an’ get hung for murder. I s’pose it’s murder to kill cows same as it is to kill people﻿—’cept for butchers.”

The cow advanced slowly and deprecatingly towards the “kidnap,” who promptly dropped the handkerchief and beamed with joy.

“Bow-wow!” it said excitedly.

“Anyway, let’s get on with the feast,” said Douglas.

“Feast!” echoed Ginger bitterly. “Feast! Not much feast left! That baby William brought’s used all the paraffin-oil and potatoes, and it’s squashed the apple-dumpling, and William’s washed its face in the licorice water.”

===Chinese===
「那好啊，动手呀。」威廉说，「杀了烤呀。我们又没拦着你，是不是？杀了烤呀——然后以谋杀罪给绞死。我想杀牛跟杀人一样都算谋杀——只有卖肉的例外。」

母牛迈着缓慢而客客气气的步子朝那「肉票」走去，肉票立刻丢下手帕，喜笑颜开。

「汪！汪！」它兴奋地叫道。

「别管了，咱们接着开宴吧。」道格拉斯说。

「开宴！」金杰恨声应道，「开宴！宴席剩下的可不多了！威廉弄来的那个宝宝把煤油和土豆全用光了，把苹果团子也压扁了，威廉还拿甘草水给它洗了脸。」
===Original===
Henry gazed at it dispassionately and judicially.

“Yes﻿—it looks like as if someone had washed it in licorice water﻿—and as if it had used up all the oil and potatoes. It doesn’t look like as if it would fetch much ransom. You seem to have pretty well mucked it up.”

“Oh, shut up about the baby,” said William picking up his damp and now prune-coloured handkerchief. “I’m just about sick of it. Come on with the fire.”

They made a little pile of twigs in the field and began the process of lighting it.

“I hope that cow won’t hurt the ‘kidnap,’ ” said Douglas suddenly. “Go and see, William; it’s your kidnap.”

===Chinese===
亨利不动声色、俨然法官似的打量着它。

「是啊——它瞧着像是让人拿甘草水洗过——也像是把油和土豆都用光了。它这副样子可卖不出多少赎金了。你们俩算是把它彻底糟蹋完了。」

「噢，宝宝的事少说两句吧。」威廉捡起他那条湿漉漉、如今变成深褐色的手帕，「我简直受够它了。走，生火去。」

他们在田里堆起一小堆树枝，开始点火。

「但愿那头牛别伤了『肉票』。」道格拉斯忽然说，「去看看吧，威廉；它是你的肉票。」

===Original===
“Well, an’ it’s Henry’s cow, and I’m sorry for that cow if it tries playin’ tricks on that baby.”

But he rose from his knees reluctantly, and threw open the barn door. The cow and the baby were still gazing admiringly at each other. From the cow’s mouth at the end of a long, sodden ribbon, hung the chewed remains of the baby’s hat. The baby was holding up the dog biscuit and crowed delightfully as the cow bent down its head and cautiously and gingerly smelt it. As William entered, the cow turned round and switched its tail against the baby’s head. At the piercing howl that followed, the whole band of outlaws entered the barn.

“What are you doing to the poor little thing?” said Douglas to William.

“It’s Henry’s cow,” said William despairingly. “It hit it. Oh, go on, shut up! Do shut up.”

The howls redoubled.

“You brought it,” said Henry accusingly, raising his voice to be heard above the baby’s fury and indignation. “Can’t you stop it? Not much sense taking babies about if you don’t know how to stop ’em crying!”

===Chinese===
「哼，那还是亨利的牛呢。那头牛要是敢对那宝宝耍花招，我才替它捏把汗呢。」

可他还是不情愿地从地上站起来，一把推开谷仓的门。牛和宝宝还在互相倾慕地对望。牛嘴上垂着一条湿漉漉的长带子，末端挂着宝宝那顶帽子的被嚼残骸。宝宝正举着那块狗饼干，欢喜得咿咿直叫，牛低下头，小心翼翼、轻手轻脚地嗅它。威廉一进去，牛就转过身，一甩尾巴扫在宝宝脑袋上。紧接着那声撕心裂肺的嚎啕一起，全体亡命徒都涌进了谷仓。

「你对那可怜的小东西干了什么？」道格拉斯质问威廉。

「那是亨利的牛。」威廉绝望地说，「是它打的它。噢，行了吧，闭嘴！你倒是闭嘴啊。」

嚎哭声翻了一倍。

「是你把它弄来的。」亨利控诉道，嗓门提高到盖过宝宝的狂怒与义愤的程度，「你就不能叫它停下来吗？带着娃娃到处跑，又不会哄它们别哭，那可没什么道理！」

===Original===
The baby was now purple in the face.

The Outlaws stood around and watched it helplessly.

“P’raps it’s hungry,” suggested Douglas.

He took up the half cake from the remains of the stores and held it out tentatively to the baby. The baby stopped crying suddenly.

“Dad﻿—dad﻿—dad﻿—dad﻿—dad,” it said tearfully.

Douglas blushed and grinned.

===Chinese===
宝宝这时脸都哭紫了。

亡命徒们围成一圈，束手无策地看着它。

「兴许是饿了。」道格拉斯提示道。

他从家当的残骸里拿起那半块蛋糕，试探着递到宝宝跟前。宝宝戛然止住了哭。

「爸——爸——爸——爸——爸。」它眼泪汪汪地说。

道格拉斯脸一红，咧嘴笑了。

===Original===
“Keeps on thinking I’m its father,” he said with conscious superiority. “Here, like some cake?”

The baby broke off a handful and conveyed it to its mouth.

“It’s eating it,” cried Douglas in shrill excitement. After thoroughly masticating it, however, the baby repented of its condescension and ejected the mouthful in several instalments.

William blushed for it.

“Oh, come on, let’s go and look at the fire,” he said weakly.

===Chinese===
「它老当我是它爸爸。」他带着自觉高人一等的口气说，「来，吃点儿蛋糕？」

宝宝掰下一大块，送进了嘴里。

「它吃啦！」道格拉斯尖着嗓子狂喜地大叫。可是它嚼了个透之后，又后悔起自己的屈尊俯就，把那一嘴东西分作好几口吐了出来。

威廉替它臊得慌。

「噢，走吧，咱们瞧瞧火去。」他有气无力地说。

===Original===
They left the barn and returned to the scene of the fire-lighting. The cow, still swinging the remains of the baby’s hat from its mouth, was standing with its front feet firmly planted on the remains of what had been a promising fire.

“Look!” cried William, in undisguised pleasure. “Look at Henry’s cow! Pretty nice sort of cow you’ve brought, Henry. Not much sense taking cows about if you can’t stop them puttin’ folks’ fires out.”

After a heated argument, the Outlaws turned their attention to the cow. The cow refused to be “shoo’d off.” It simply stood immovable and stared them out. Ginger approached cautiously and gave it a little push. It switched its tail into his eye and continued to munch the baby’s hat-string. Upon William’s approaching it lowered its head, and William retreated hastily. At last they set off to collect some fresh wood and light a fresh fire. Soon they were blissfully consuming two blackened slices of ham, the popcorn, and what was left of the cake.

After the “feast,” Ginger and William, as Wild Indians, attacked the barn, which was defended by Douglas and Henry. The “kidnap” crawled round inside on all fours, picking up any treasures it might come across *en route* and testing their effect on its palate.

Occasionally it carried on a conversation with its defenders, bringing with it a strong perfume of paraffin oil as it approached.

===Chinese===
他们离开谷仓，回到生火的现场。那头牛嘴里还甩荡着宝宝帽子的残骸，前腿牢牢踩在一堆原本大有前途的火的残骸上。

「瞧！」威廉毫不掩饰满心欢喜地大喊，「瞧亨利的牛！你弄来的这头牛可真不赖呀，亨利。带着牛到处跑，又管不住它把人家的火踩灭，那可没什么道理！」

一场唇枪舌剑之后，亡命徒们把注意力转向了那头牛。牛拒不接受「嘘走」。它干脆一动不动地站着，用瞪眼把他们瞪了回去。金杰小心翼翼地凑上去，轻轻推了它一把。它一甩尾巴抽在他眼睛上，继续嚼宝宝的帽带儿。威廉一靠近，它就低下头，威廉赶紧撤退。最后他们只好动身去捡些新柴，另生一堆新火。很快，他们就幸福无比地吃起了两片焦黑的火腿、爆米花，还有蛋糕剩下的一点儿。

「宴席」过后，金杰和威廉扮作野蛮印第安人进攻谷仓，谷仓由道格拉斯和亨利把守。那「肉票」在里头手脚并用地四处爬行，沿途捡起它碰见的任何宝贝，逐一检验它们对舌头的功效。

它时不时还同守军攀谈几句，每回走近，都随身带来一股浓烈的煤油香。

===Original===
“Blab﻿—blab﻿—blab﻿—blab﻿—blub﻿—blub﻿—Dad﻿—dad﻿—dad﻿—dad﻿—dad. Go﻿—o﻿—o﻿—o.”

William had insisted on a place on the attacking side.

“I couldn’t put any feelin’,” he explained, “into fightin’ for that baby.”

When they finally decided to set off homewards, William gazed hopelessly at his charge. Its appearance defies description. For many years afterwards William associated babies in his mind with paraffin-oil and potato.

“Just help me get the potato out of its hair,” he pleaded; “never mind the oil and the rest of it.”

===Chinese===
「叭——叭——叭——叭——啵——啵——啵——爸——爸——爸——爸——爸。乖——哦——哦——哦。」

威廉坚持要站到进攻的一方。

「要我替那宝宝打仗，」他解释说，「我可提不起一点儿劲儿来。」

等他们最后决定动身回家时，威廉绝望地望着自己看管的这个小东西。它的模样笔墨难以形容。此后许多年，威廉一想起娃娃，脑子里就浮现出煤油和土豆。

「帮我把土豆从它头发里弄出来就行，」他恳求道，「油什么的就随它去吧。」

===Original===
“My hat! doesn’t it smell funny!﻿—and doesn’t it look funny﻿—all oil and potato and bits of cake!” said Ginger.

“Oh! shut up about it,” said William irritably.

The cow followed them down to the stile and watched them sardonically as they climbed it.

“Bow-wow!” murmured the baby in affectionate farewell.

William looked wildly round for the pram, but﻿—the pram was gone﻿—only the piece of string dangled from the railings.

===Chinese===
「我的老天！它这味儿多冲呀！——这模样多逗呀——全是油、土豆，还有蛋糕渣儿！」金杰说。

「噢！这事别说了。」威廉烦躁地说。

那头牛跟着他们走到栅栏阶梯跟前，一脸讥诮地看着他们爬过去。

「汪！汪！」宝宝深情款款地低声道别。

威廉发疯似的四下张望找婴儿车，可是——婴儿车没了——栏杆上只晃荡着那截绳子。

===Original===
“Crumbs!” said William, “Talk about bad luck! I’m simply statin’ a fact. Talk about bad luck!”

At that minute the pram appeared, charging down the hill at full speed with a cargo of small boys. At the bottom of the hill it overturned into a ditch accompanied by its cargo. To judge from its appearance, it had passed the afternoon performing the operation.

“That’s my pram!” said William to the cargo, as it emerged, joyfully, from the ditch.

“Garn! S’ours! We found it.”

===Chinese===
「我的天！」威廉说，「这叫什么倒霉事儿！我只是在陈述事实。这叫什么倒霉事儿！」

就在这时，婴儿车出现了，满载着一车小男孩，全速冲下山坡。到了坡底，它连同那车货物一起翻进了沟里。从它的模样判断，这一下午它一直在从事这项事业。

「那是我的婴儿车！」威廉对着那车货物说——它们正欢天喜地地从沟里往外爬。

「呸！是我们的！是我们捡到的。」

===Original===
“Well, I left it there.”

“Come on! We’ll fight for it,” said Ginger, rolling up his sleeves in a businesslike manner. The other Outlaws followed his example. The pram’s cargo eyed them appraisingly.

“Oh, all right! Take your rotten old pram!” they said at last.

Douglas placed the baby in its seat and William thoughtfully put up the hood to shield his charge as far as possible from the curious gaze of the passersby. His charge was now chewing the pram cover and talking excitedly to itself. With a “heart steeled for any fate” William turned the corner into his own road. The baby’s mother was standing at his gate.

===Chinese===
「哼，是我搁那儿的。」

「来吧！我们为它打一架。」金杰说着，干脆利落地挽起袖子。其他亡命徒纷纷效仿。婴儿车的那车货物把他们上下打量了一番。

「噢，行了吧！拿走你们的破车吧！」他们最后说。

道格拉斯把宝宝放回座位里，威廉体贴地撑起车篷，尽量让自己看管的这位小东西避开路人好奇的目光。这小东西正在嚼车毯，兴奋地自言自语。威廉怀着一颗「钢铁般直面一切命运」的心，拐过弯进了自家的那条路。宝宝的母亲正站在他家大门口。

===Original===
“There you are!” she called. “I was getting quite anxious. Thank you *so* much, dear.”

**But that is what she said before she saw the baby!**

===Chinese===
「你可回来啦！」她招呼道，「我都快急死了。真是太*谢谢*你了，亲爱的。」

**可这是她在看见宝宝之前说的话！**

## V The Show / 五 展览会

===Original===

The Outlaws sat around the old barn, plunged in deep thought. Henry, the oldest member (aged 12¼) had said in a moment of inspiration:

“Let’s think of﻿—sumthin’ else to do﻿—sumthin’ quite fresh from what we’ve ever done before.”

And the Outlaws were thinking.

They had engaged in mortal combat with one another, they had cooked strange ingredients over a smoking and reluctant flame with a fine disregard of culinary conventions, they had tracked each other over the countryside with gait and complexions intended to represent those of the aborigines of South America, they had even turned their attention to kidnapping (without any striking success), and these occupations had palled.

===Chinese===

亡命徒帮围坐在旧谷仓边，陷入了深深的思索。年纪最长的成员亨利（年方12¼岁）在灵光乍现之际说了句：

“咱们想点——别的什么干干吧——跟咱们以前干过的都不一样的、全新的什么。”

于是亡命徒帮就思索起来。

他们彼此进行过殊死搏斗，他们在一堆冒烟又不情愿的火苗上煮过五花八门的东西、全然不把烹饪规矩放在眼里，他们顶着模拟南美洲原住民的步态和脸色互相追踪着跑遍乡野，他们甚至搞过绑架（毫无惊人战果），这些营生都已经叫他们腻烦了。

===Original===

In all its activities the Society of Outlaws (comprising four members) aimed at a simple, unostentatious mode of procedure. In their shrinking from the glare of publicity they showed an example of unaffected modesty that many other public societies might profitably emulate. The parents of the members were unaware of the very existence of the society. The ill-timed and tactless interference of parents had nipped in the bud many a cherished plan, and by bitter experience the Outlaws had learnt that secrecy was their only protection. Owing to the rules and restrictions of an unsympathetic world that orders school hours from 9 to 4 their meetings were confined to half-holidays and occasionally Sunday afternoons.

William, the ever ingenious, made the first suggestion.

“Let’s shoot things with bows an’ arrows same as real outlaws used to,” he said.

===Chinese===

亡命徒帮会社（共四名成员）的一切活动，都追求一种朴素、不事张扬的行事方式。他们畏避公众的目光，展现出一种毫不做作的谦逊，足为其他许多公共社团效法的楷模。成员们的父母对会社的存在一无所知。父母们那种不合时宜、不讲策略的干预，已经把多少心爱的计划扼杀在了萌芽里；惨痛的经验教会了亡命徒帮：保密是他们唯一的护身符。由于这个冷酷世界定下朝九晚四的上课时间，他们的集会只能限于半天假，偶尔外加星期天下午。

一贯足智多谋的威廉第一个开了口。

“咱们用弓箭射东西吧，跟真正的亡命徒从前干的一样。”他说。

===Original===

“What things?” and

“What bows an’ arrows?” said Henry and Ginger simultaneously.

“Oh, anything﻿—birds an’ cats an’ hens an’ things﻿—an’ buy bows an’ arrows. You can buy them in shops.”

“We can make them,” said Douglas, hopefully.

“Not like you can get them in shops. They’d shoot crooked or sumthin’ if we made them. They’ve got to be jus’ so to shoot straight. I saw some in Brook’s window, too, jus’ right﻿—jus’ same as real outlaws had.”

===Chinese===

“射什么东西？”还有

“什么弓箭？”亨利和金杰异口同声。

“哦，什么都行——鸟啊、猫啊、母鸡啊什么的——弓箭就去买。店里就能买到。”

“咱们可以自己做。”道格拉斯满怀希望地说。

“自己做的不如店里买的。自己做的射起来准歪什么的。要射得直，就得做得恰到好处。我在布鲁克家的橱窗里看见过，正合适——跟真正的亡命徒用的一模一样。”

===Original===

“How much?” said the outlaws breathlessly.

“Five shillings﻿—targets for learnin’ on before we begin shootin’ real things an’ all.”

“Five shillings!” breathed Douglas. He might as well have said five pounds. “We’ve not got five shillings. Henry’s not having any money since he broke their drawing-room window an’ Ginger only has 3d. a week an’ has to give collection an’ we’ve not paid for the guinea pig yet, the one that got into Ginger’s sister’s hat an’ she was so mad at, an’﻿—”

“Oh, never mind all that,” said William, scornfully. “We’ll jus’ get five shillings.”

===Chinese===

“多少钱？”亡命徒们屏住呼吸问。

“五先令——还得买练箭用的靶子，练熟了才开始射真家伙。”

“五先令！”道格拉斯倒抽一口凉气。他说五镑也不过如此。“咱们哪来五先令。亨利自打打碎了他们家客厅的窗玻璃就断了零花钱，金杰一星期只有三便士，还得交教堂奉献金，还有咱们那只豚鼠的钱还没付呢，就是钻进金杰姐姐帽子里的那只，她气得要命，还有——”

“哦，那些都别管，”威廉不屑地说，“咱们弄来五先令就是了。”

===Original===

“How?”

“Well,” uncertainly, “grownups can always get money when they want it.”

“How?” again.

William disliked being tied down to details.

“Oh﻿—bazaars an’ things,” impatiently.

===Chinese===

“怎么弄？”

“呃，”他没把握地说，“大人想要钱的时候，总归弄得到钱。”

“怎么弄？”又是一句。

威廉不喜欢被追问细节。

“哦——义卖会什么的。”他不耐烦了。

===Original===

“Bazaars!” exploded Henry. “Who’d come to a bazaar if we had one? Who would? Jus’ tell me that if you’re so clever! Who’d come to it? Besides, you’ve got to sell things at a bazaar, haven’t you? What’d we sell? We’ve got nothin’ to sell, have we? What’s the good of havin’ a bazaar with nothin’ to sell and no one to buy it? Jus’ tell me that!”

Henry always enjoyed scoring off William.

“Well﻿—shows an’ things,” said William desperately.

There was a moment’s silence, then Ginger repeated thoughtfully. “Shows!” and Douglas, whose eldest brother was home from college for his vacation, murmured self-consciously, “By Jove!”

===Chinese===

“义卖会！”亨利炸了。“咱们办义卖会谁会来？谁会来？你要是这么聪明，倒说说看！谁会来？再说，义卖会总得卖东西吧，是不是？咱们卖什么？咱们没东西可卖，对不对？办一场没东西可卖、也没人来买的义卖会有什么用？你倒说说看！”

亨利一向以驳倒威廉为乐。

“呃——展览什么的。”威廉急中生智。

一时无人作声，随后金杰若有所思地重复道：“展览！”而道格拉斯——他大哥正从大学放假回家——也心虚地低声叹道：“天哪！”

===Original===

“We *could* do a show,” said Ginger. “Get animals an’ things an’ charge money for lookin’ at them.”

“Who’d pay it?” said Henry, the doubter.

“Anyone would. You’d pay to see animals, wouldn’t you?﻿—real animals. People do at the Zoo, don’t they? Well, we’ll get some animals. That’s easy enough, isn’t it?”

A neighbouring church clock struck four and the meeting was adjourned.

“Well, we’ll have a show an’ get money and buy bows an’ arrows an’ shoot things,” summed up William, “an we’ll arrange the show next week.”

===Chinese===

“咱们*倒*是可以办个展览，”金杰说，“弄些动物什么的，收钱让人参观。”

“谁肯掏钱？”怀疑派亨利说。

“谁都肯。看动物你也肯掏钱的，对不对？——真动物。人们在动物园不就掏钱看吗？呃，咱们就去弄些动物来。这还不容易吗？”

邻近的教堂钟敲了四下，会议散了。

“好，咱们就办展览，弄到钱买弓箭射东西，”威廉总结道，“展览的事下星期再安排。”

===Original===

William returned home slowly and thoughtfully. He sat on his bed, his hands in his pockets, his brow drawn into a frown, his thoughts wandering in a dreamland of wonderful “shows” and rare exotic beasts.

Suddenly from the next room came a thin sound that gathered volume till it seemed to fill the house like the roaring of a lion, then died gradually away and was followed by silence. But only for a second. It began again﻿—a small whisper that grew louder and louder, became a raucous bellow, then faded slowly away to rise again after a moment’s silence. In the next room William’s mother’s Aunt Emily was taking her afternoon nap. Aunt Emily had come down a month ago for a week’s visit and had not yet referred to the date of her departure. William’s father was growing anxious. She was a stout, healthy lady, who spent all her time recovering from a slight illness she had had two years ago. Her life held two occupations, and only two. These were eating and sleeping. For William she possessed a subtle but irresistible fascination. Her stature, her appetite, her gloom, added to the fact that she utterly ignored him, attracted him strongly.

The tea bell rang and the sound of the snoring ceased abruptly. This entertainment over, William descended to the dining-room, where his father was addressing his mother with some heat.

===Chinese===

威廉慢悠悠、若有所思地回了家。他坐在床上，双手插兜，眉头拧成一个疙瘩，思绪徜徉在美妙的“展览”和珍禽异兽的梦境里。

突然，隔壁房间传来一个细细的声音，越聚越响，直到像狮子吼一般灌满整座房子，又渐渐消逝，随后是一片寂静。可只有一秒钟。它又开始了——一声低语，越来越响，变成嘶哑的咆哮，再慢慢淡下去，静默片刻后又重新升起。隔壁房间里，威廉母亲的姨妈埃米莉（Emily）正在睡午觉。埃米莉姨妈一个月前来做客，说好住一个星期，至今尚未提及归期。威廉的父亲日渐焦虑。她是位结结实实、十分健康的太太，把全部光阴都用来休养两年前得过的一场小病。她的人生只有两桩事业，拢共只有两桩：吃和睡。对威廉来说，她具有一种微妙而不可抗拒的魅力。她的块头、她的胃口、她的愁苦，外加她全然不把他放在眼里这一事实，都强烈地吸引着他。

茶点的铃响了，鼾声戛然而止。这场余兴节目结束了，威廉下楼来到饭厅，他父亲正气冲冲地对母亲说话。

===Original===

“Is she going to stay here forever, or only for a few years? I’d like to know, because﻿—”

Perceiving William, he stopped abruptly, and William’s mother murmured:

“It’s so nice to have her, dear.”

Then Aunt Emily entered.

===Chinese===

“她是打算在这儿住上一辈子，还是只住个几年？我想知道，因为——”

一见威廉进来，他猛地收住了话头，威廉的母亲低声说：

“家里有她多好呀，亲爱的。”

这时埃米莉姨妈进来了。

===Original===

“Have you slept well, Aunt?”

“Slept!” repeated Aunt Emily majestically. “I hardly expect to sleep in my state of health. A little rest is all I can expect.”

“Sorry you’re no better,” said William’s father sardonically.

“*Better?*” she repeated again indignantly. “It will be a long time before I’m better.”

She lowered her large, healthy frame into a chair, carefully selected a substantial piece of bread and butter and attacked it with vigour.

===Chinese===

“您睡得好吗，姨妈？”

“睡！”埃米莉姨妈庄严地重复了一遍，“就我这身子骨，我压根不指望睡好。能稍微歇一歇，我就知足了。”

“可惜您不见起色。”威廉的父亲讥诮地说。

“*起色？*”她又愤愤地重复了一遍，“要见起色，还早着呢。”

她把那副高大结实的身躯安顿进一把椅子，仔细挑了一厚片黄油面包，大举进攻。

===Original===

“I’m going to the post after tea,” said William’s mother. “Would you care to come with me?”

Aunt Emily took a large helping of jam.

“You hardly expect me to go out in the evening in my state of health, surely? It’s years since I went out after tea. And I was at the post office this morning. There were a lot of people there, but they served me first. I suppose they saw I looked ill.”

William’s father choked suddenly and apologised, but not humbly.

===Chinese===

“喝过茶我要去趟邮局，”威廉的母亲说，“您愿意一块儿去吗？”

埃米莉姨妈舀了满满一大勺果酱。

“您总不至于指望我就这身子骨晚上出门吧？我喝过茶以后出门，都多少年没的事了。再说今天早上我刚去过邮局。那儿人可多了，可他们头一个招呼我。我猜他们是看出来我气色不好。”

威廉的父亲猛地呛了一下，道了歉，但并不谦卑。

===Original===

“Though I must say,” went on Aunt Emily, “this place does suit me. I think after a few months here I should be a little stronger. Pass the jam, William.”

The glance that William’s father fixed upon her would have made a stronger woman quail, but Aunt Emily was scraping out the last remnants of jam and did not notice.

“I’m a bit overtired today, I think,” she went on. “I’m so apt to forget how weak I am and then I overdo it. I’m ready for the cake, William. I just sat out in the sun yesterday afternoon and sat a bit too long and overtired myself. I ought to write letters after tea, but I don’t think I have the strength. Another piece of cake, William. I’ll go upstairs to rest instead, I think. I hope you’ll keep the house quiet. It’s so rarely that I can get a bit of sleep.”

William’s father left the room abruptly. William sat on and watched, with fascinated eyes, the cake disappear, and finally followed the large, portly figure upstairs and sat down in his room to plan the “show” and incidentally listen, with a certain thrilled awe, for the sounds from next door.

===Chinese===

“不过我得说，”埃米莉姨妈接着说，“这地方倒真适合我。我想在这儿住上几个月，我兴许能强壮一点。把果酱递给我，威廉。”

威廉的父亲射向她的那一眼，足以让更强壮的女人发抖，可埃米莉姨妈正忙着刮果酱瓶里最后一点残渣，没有察觉。

“我今天有点过劳了，我看，”她继续说，“我就是容易忘了自己身子多虚，一忘就过了头。我可以吃蛋糕了，威廉。昨天下午我就那么在外头太阳底下坐了坐，坐得久了点，就过劳了。喝过茶我本该写写信的，可我看我没有那个力气。再来一块蛋糕，威廉。我想我还是上楼歇着去吧。希望你们让家里保持安静。我能睡上一小觉的时候可不多。”

威廉的父亲突然离席。威廉继续坐着，出神地看着蛋糕一点点消失，最后跟着那魁伟的身影上了楼，在自己房里坐下，筹划起“展览”来，顺便带着几分刺激的敬畏，谛听隔壁的动静。

===Original===

The place and time of the “show” presented no little difficulty. To hold it in the old barn would give away to the world the cherished secret of their meeting place. It was William who suggested his bedroom, to be entered, not by way of the front door and staircase, but by the less public way of the garden wall and scullery roof. Ever an optimist, he affirmed that no one would see or hear. The choice of a time was limited to Wednesday afternoon, Saturday afternoon, and Sunday. Sunday at first was ruled out as impossible. But there were difficulties about Wednesday afternoon and Saturday afternoon. On Wednesday afternoon Ginger and Douglas were unwilling and ungraceful pupils at a dancing class. On Saturday afternoon William’s father gardened and would command a view of the garden wall and scullery roof. On these afternoons also Cook and Emma, both of a suspicious turn of mind, would be at large. On Sunday Cook and Emma went out, William’s mother paid a regular weekly visit to an old friend and William’s father spent the afternoon on the sofa, dead to the world.

===Chinese===

“展览”的地点和时间是个不小的难题。在旧谷仓里办，就会把他们心爱的碰头地点这个宝贝秘密泄露给全世界。是威廉提议用他的卧室，入口不走大门和楼梯，而走比较避人的路线——花园的墙和厨房外屋顶。他一如既往地乐观，断言谁也不会看见、谁也听不见。时间的选择只有星期三下午、星期六下午和星期天。星期天起初被当作绝无可能，排除在外。可星期三下午和星期六下午也各有难处。星期三下午，金杰和道格拉斯要去上舞蹈课，是既不情愿又姿态难看的学生。星期六下午，威廉的父亲要侍弄花园，正好把花园的墙和厨房外屋顶尽收眼底。这几个下午，厨娘和埃玛这两个一肚子疑心的人物也都在家走动。到了星期天，厨娘和埃玛出门，威廉的母亲照例每周拜访一位老朋友，威廉的父亲则在沙发上度过整个下午，睡得不省人事。

===Original===

Moreover, as he pointed out to the Outlaws, the members of the Sunday School could be waylaid and induced to attend the show and they would probably be provided with money for collection. The more William thought over it, the more attractive became the idea of a Sunday afternoon in spite of superficial difficulties; therefore Sunday afternoon was finally chosen.

The day was fortunately a fine one, and William and the other Outlaws were at work early. William had asked his mother, with an expression of meekness and virtue that ought to have warned her of danger, if he might have “jus’ a few friends” in his room for the afternoon. His mother, glad that her husband should be spared his son’s restless company, gave willing permission.

By half-past two the exhibits were ready. In a cage by the window sat a white rat painted in faint alternate stripes of blue and pink.
