This was Douglas’ contribution, handpainted by himself in water colours. It wore a bewildered expression and occasionally licked its stripes and then obviously wished it hadn’t. Its cage bore a notice printed on cardboard:

===Chinese===

何况，正如他向亡命徒帮指出的，主日学校的学生可以在半路上拦住，哄来看展览，而且他们身上多半带着奉献金。威廉越想越觉得星期天下午这个主意有魅力，尽管表面上有种种难处；于是星期天下午最终当选。

那天天气幸好晴好，威廉和其他亡命徒一早就干开了。威廉问过母亲，能不能下午“就请几个朋友”到他房间里来——问时那副温良贤德的神情本该让她警觉到危险。他母亲乐得让丈夫免受儿子闹腾的搅扰，爽快地答应了。

两点半，展品全部就绪。窗边一只笼子里蹲着一只白鼠，身上涂着淡蓝粉相间的条纹。这是道格拉斯的展品，由他亲手用水彩上的色。它一脸茫然，时不时舔一下自己的条纹，然后明显地后悔了。笼子上贴着一张纸板写的告示：

===Original===

> Rat from China 

 Rats are all like 

 this in China

===Chinese===

> 中国来的老鼠

中国的老鼠

全都长这样

===Original===

Next came a cat belonging to William’s sister, Smuts by name, now imprisoned beneath a basket-chair. At the best of times Smuts was short-tempered, and all its life had cherished a bitter hatred of William. Now, enclosed by its enemy in a prison two feet square, its fury knew no bounds. It tore at the basket work, it flew wildly round and round, scratching, spitting, swearing. Its chair bore the simple and appropriate notice:

> Wild cat

===Chinese===

接下来是威廉姐姐的猫，名叫斯马茨，此刻被扣在一把藤椅底下。斯马茨即使在心情最好的时候也脾气火爆，一辈子都对威廉怀着一腔刻骨仇恨。如今被仇人关进一座两英尺见方的监狱，它的怒火无边无际。它撕咬藤条，疯狂地转圈抓挠、喷吐唾沫、骂骂咧咧。椅子上贴着一张简单贴切的告示：

> 野猫

===Original===

William watched it with honest pride and prayed fervently that its indignation would not abate during the afternoon.

Next came a giant composed of Douglas upon Ginger’s back, draped in two sheets tied tightly round Douglas’s neck. This was labelled:

> Genwin giant

===Chinese===

威廉怀着老实的自豪注视着它，热切祷告它这股怒气整个下午都不要消。

再接下来是一个巨人，由金杰驮着道格拉斯组成，两床床单紧紧拴在道格拉斯脖子上一披。展品标签写着：

> 正真巨人

===Original===

Ginger was already growing restive. His muffled voice was heard from the folds of the sheets informing the other Outlaws that it was a bit thick and he hadn’t known it would be like this or he wouldn’t have done it, and anyway he was going to change with Douglas half time or he’d chuck up the whole thing.

The next exhibit was a black fox fur of William’s mother’s, to which was fortunately attached a head and several feet, and which he had surreptitiously removed from her wardrobe. This had been tied up, stuffed with waste paper and wired by William till it was, in his eyes, remarkably lifelike. As the legs, even with the assistance of wire, refused to support the body and the head would only droop sadly to the ground, it was perforce exhibited in a recumbent attitude. It bore marks of sticky fingers, and of several side slips of the scissors when William was cutting the wire, but on the whole he was justly proud of it. It bore the striking but untruthful legend:﻿—

> Bear shot 

===Chinese===

金杰已经越来越坐不住了。只听见他从床单褶子里发出闷声闷气的抗议，跟其他亡命徒说这也太苦了，他早知道是这样就不干了，反正他跟道格拉斯必须对半换班，不然他就不干了。

下一件展品是威廉母亲的一条黑色狐皮围脖，幸好上面还连着脑袋和好几只脚，是他偷偷从她衣柜里弄出来的。这条围脖经威廉又是捆又是塞废纸又是上铁丝，在他眼里已经活灵活现到惊人的地步。由于那几条腿即使有铁丝帮忙也撑不起身子，脑袋只会悲哀地垂到地上，只好以卧姿展出。它身上带着黏糊糊的手指印，还有威廉剪铁丝时剪刀打滑留下的几道伤，但总的说来他有充分的理由为它骄傲。它身上挂着一条醒目但不诚实的字幅：——

> 被亡命徒帮在

===Original===

 by outlaws 

 in Rusher

===Chinese===

拉舍尔打死

的大熊

===Original===

Next came:

> Blue dog

This was Henry’s fox terrier, generally known as Chips. For Chips the world was very black. Henry’s master mind had scorned his paint box and his water colours. Henry had “borrowed” a blue bag and dabbed it liberally over Chips. Chips had, after the first wild frenzied struggle, offered no resistance. He now sat, a picture of black despair, turning every now and then a melancholy eye upon the still enraged Smuts. But for him cats and joy and life and fighting were no more. He was abject, shamed﻿—a blue dog.

===Chinese===

再接下来是：

> 蓝狗

这是亨利的狐梗，平时大家都叫它薯条。对薯条来说，世界一片漆黑。亨利的智囊头脑看不上他的颜料盒和水彩。亨利“借”来一只洗衣蓝袋（装靛蓝的漂洗袋），在薯条身上慷慨地到处拍打。起初那阵疯狂挣扎过后，薯条就不再反抗了。它现在坐在那里，活脱脱一幅绝望的画像，时不时朝还在盛怒的斯马茨投去忧郁的一瞥。对它来说，猫、欢乐、生活和打架都已经不复存在。它卑微、羞惭——一条蓝狗。

===Original===

William himself, as showman, was an imposing figure. He was robed in a red dressing-gown of his father’s that trailed on the ground behind him and over whose cords in front he stumbled ungracefully as he walked. He had cut a few strands from the fringe of a rug and glued them to his lips to represent moustaches. They fell in two straight lines over his mouth. On his head was a tinsel crown, once worn by his sister as Fairy Queen.

The show had been widely advertised and all the neighbouring children had been individually canvassed, but under strict orders of secrecy. The threats of what the Outlaws would do if their secret were disclosed had kept many a child awake at night.

William surveyed the room proudly.

===Chinese===

威廉本人作为展览主持人，是个气派的场面。他套着父亲的红色晨衣，下摆拖在身后的地上，走路时在前襟的腰带上绊得踉踉跄跄。他从地毯的流苏上剪下几绺，粘在嘴唇上充当胡子。它们成两道直线耷拉在嘴上。头上是一顶锡箔王冠，是他姐姐扮仙后时戴过的。

展览早就广而告之，左邻右舍的孩子挨个上门游说过了，但都严令保密。亡命徒帮放话，秘密若被泄露他们将如何如何，吓得许多孩子夜里睡不着觉。

威廉自豪地环视房间。

===Original===

“Not a bad show for a penny, I *should* say. I guess there aren’t many like it, anyway. Do shut up talkin’, Ginger. It’ll spoil it all, if folks hear the giant talking out of his stomach. It’s Douglas that’s got to do the giant’s *talking*. Anyone could see that. I say, they’re comin’! Look! They’re comin’! Along the wall!”

There was a thin line of children climbing along the wall in single file on all fours. They ascended the scullery roof and approached the window. These were the first arrivals who had called on their way to Sunday School.

Henry took their pennies and William cleared his throat and began:﻿—

===Chinese===

“一便士看这场展览，我*看*不亏。反正我猜这样的展览可不多。金杰，你闭嘴吧。要是让人听见巨人从肚子里说话，就全砸了。巨人开口*说话*得由道格拉斯来。谁都看得出来。我说，他们来了！看！来了！顺着墙过来了！”

只见一条细细的孩子长龙，正四肢着地、单列沿着墙头爬过来。他们爬上厨房外屋顶，朝窗户逼近。这是头一批观众，来主日学校的路上先来光顾。

亨利收下他们的便士，威廉清了清嗓子，开讲了：——

===Original===

“White rat from China, ladies an’ gentlemen, pink an’ blue striped. All rats is pink an’ blue striped in China. This is the only genwin China rat in England﻿—brought over from China special las’ week jus’ for the show. It lives on China bread an’ butter brought over special, too.”

“Wash it!” jeered an unbeliever. “Jus’ wash it an’ let’s see it then.”

“Wash it?” repeated the showman indignantly. “It’s gotter be washed. It’s washed every morning an’ night same as you or me. China rats have gotter be washed or they’d die right off. Washin’ ’em don’t make no difference to their stripes. Anyone knows that that knows anything about China rats, I guess.”

===Chinese===

“中国来的白鼠，女士们先生们，粉蓝条纹的。中国的老鼠全是粉蓝条纹的。这是英格兰唯一一只正真中国鼠——上星期专门从中国运来的，就为这场展览。它吃的是中国黄油面包，也是专门运来的。”

“洗洗它！”一个不信邪的起哄，“把它洗洗，咱们瞧瞧。”

“洗它？”主持人愤愤地重复，“它是得洗来着。它每天早晚都洗，跟你跟我一样。中国的老鼠非洗不可，不洗就当场死掉。洗它们对条纹毫无影响。凡是懂中国老鼠的人都懂这个，我看。”

===Original===

He laughed scornfully and turned to Smuts. Smuts had grown used to the basket chair and was settling down for a nap. William crouched down on all fours, ran his fingers along the basket-work, and, putting his face close to it, gave vent to a malicious howl. Smuts sprang at him, scratching and spitting.

“Wild cat,” said William triumphantly. “Look at it! Kill anyone if it got out! Spring at their throats, it would, an’ scratch their eyes out with its paws an’ bite their necks till its teeth met. If I jus’ moved away that chair it would spring out at you.” They moved hastily away from the chair, “and I bet some of you would be dead pretty quick. It could have anyone’s head right off with bitin’ and scratchin’. Right off﻿—separate from their bodies!”

There was an awestricken silence.

===Chinese===

他轻蔑地一笑，转向斯马茨。斯马茨已经习惯了那把藤椅，正打算睡个觉。威廉四肢着地蹲下去，手指顺着藤条摸过去，把脸贴近了，发出一声恶狠狠的嚎叫。斯马茨朝他猛扑过来，又抓又啐。

“野猫，”威廉得意地说，“都看看！放出来能弄死人！它会扑上人的喉咙，用爪子挠出人的眼珠，咬人的脖子直到牙牙相扣。我只要把那把椅子挪开，它就朝你们扑过来了。”孩子们慌忙从椅子边挪开，“我敢说你们有些人不出一会儿就没命了。它又是咬又是挠，能把谁的脑袋都整个咬下来。整个下来——跟身子分家！”

一片敬畏的寂静。

===Original===

Then:

“Garn! It’s Smuts. It’s your sister’s cat!”

William laughed as though vastly amused by this idea.

“Smuts!” he said, giving a surreptitious kick to the chair that infuriated its occupant still more. “I guess there wouldn’t be many of us left in this house if Smuts was like this.”

===Chinese===

然后：

“去你的！那是斯马茨。是你姐姐的猫！”

威廉哈哈大笑，仿佛这个主意有趣极了。

“斯马茨！”他说着，偷偷踢了那把椅子一脚，把里面的囚徒气得更凶了。“我看斯马茨要是真长这样，咱们这家里剩下的活人可不多。”

===Original===

They passed on to the giant.

“A giant,” said William, rearranging the tinsel crown, which was slightly too big for him. “Real giant. Look at it. As big as two of you put together. How d’you think he gets in at doors and things? Has to have everything made special. Look at him walk. Walk, Ginger.”

Ginger took two steps forward. Douglas clutched his shoulders and murmured anxiously, “By Jove!”

“Go on,” urged William scornfully, “That’s not walkin’.”

===Chinese===

他们接着看巨人。

“巨人，”威廉说着，正了正头上那顶对他来说略大了点的锡箔王冠，“如假包换的巨人。瞧瞧。有你们两个加起来那么大。你们猜他怎么进门什么的？样样都得定做。看他走路。走两步，金杰。”

金杰向前迈了两步。道格拉斯抓紧他的肩膀，焦急地低声说：“天哪！”

“接着走啊，”威廉不屑地催促，“那不叫走路。”

===Original===

The goaded Ginger’s voice came from the giant’s middle regions!

“If you go on talkin’ at me, I’ll drop him. I’m just about sick of it.”

“All right,” said William hastily.

“Anyway it’s a giant,” he went on to his audience. “A jolly fine giant.”

===Chinese===

被激怒的金杰的声音从巨人的中部传了出来！

“你要是再冲我嚷嚷，我就把他摔了。我真是受够了。”

“好好好。”威廉赶紧说。

“反正这是个巨人，”他接着对观众说，“一个满不错的巨人。”

===Original===

“It’s got Douglas’s face,” said one of his audience.

William was for a moment at a loss.

“Well,” he said at last, “giant’s got to have some sort of a face, hasn’t it? Can’t not have a face, can it?”

The Russian Bear, which had often been seen adorning the shoulders of William’s mother and was promptly recognised, was greeted with ribald jeers, but there was no doubt as to the success of the Blue Dog. Chips advanced deprecatingly, blue head drooping, and blue tail between blue legs, makingabject apologies for his horrible condition. But Henry had done his work well. They stood around in rapt admiration.

===Chinese===

“它长着道格拉斯的脸。”观众里有一个说。

威廉一时语塞。

“呃，”他终于说，“巨人总得有张脸吧，是不是？总不能没有脸吧，能吗？”

那条俄国熊皮——它常见披在威廉母亲的肩头，当场就被认了出来——招来一片起哄的怪笑，可蓝狗的成功毫无疑问。薯条低着头缓步上前，蓝色的脑袋耷拉着，蓝色的尾巴夹在蓝色的腿间，为它这副可怕的惨相一个劲儿赔不是。可亨利的活儿干得漂亮。孩子们围成一圈，看得如痴如醉。

===Original===

“Blue dog,” said the showman, walking forward proudly and stumbling violently over the cords of the dressing gown. “Blue dog,” he repeated, recovering his balance and removing the tinsel crown from his nose to his brow. “You never saw a blue dog before, did you? No, and you aren’t likely to see one again, neither. It was made blue special for this show. It’s the only blue dog in the world. Folks’ll be comin’ from all over the world to see this blue dog﻿—an’ thrown in in a penny show! If it was in the Zoo you’d have to pay a shilling to see it, I bet. It’s﻿—it’s jus’ luck for you it’s here. I guess the folks at the Zoo wish they’d got it. Tain’t many shows have blue dogs. Brown an’ black an’ white﻿—but not blue. Why, folks pay money jus’ to see shows of ornery dogs﻿—so you’re jus’ lucky to see a blue dog *an’* a dead bear from Russia *an’* a giant, *an’* a wild cat, *an’* a China rat for jus’ one penny.”

===Chinese===

“蓝狗，”主持人说着骄傲地走上前，被晨衣的腰带狠狠绊了一个趔趄。“蓝狗，”他重复道，一边恢复平衡，一边把锡箔王冠从鼻子上挪回头顶。“你们从前见过蓝狗吗，啊？没有，往后也不大可能再见着第二条了。它是专门为这场展览染蓝的。它是全世界唯一的蓝狗。各地的人都要赶来看这条蓝狗——还只收一便士！这要是在动物园，看它一眼你得掏一先令，我敢打赌。它——它跑到你们眼前来，纯粹是你们的福气。我猜动物园那些人眼馋着呢。有蓝狗的展览可不多。棕的、黑的、白的——就是没有蓝的。呃，人们看一场寻常狗展都得掏钱呢——所以你们花一便士就看到一条蓝狗，*外带*一头俄国死熊，*外带*一个巨人，*外带*一只野猫，*外带*一只中国鼠，可真是天大的运气。”

===Original===

After each speech William had to remove from his mouth the rug fringe which persisted in obeying the force of gravity rather than William’s idea of what a moustache should be.

“It’s jus’ paint. Henry’s gate’s being painted blue,” said one critic feebly, but on the whole the Outlaws had scored a distinct success in the blue dog.

Then, while they stood in silent admiration round the unhappy animal, came a sound from the next door, a gentle sound like the sighing of the wind through the trees. It rose and fell. It rose again and fell again. It increased in volume with each repetition, till at its height it sounded like a wild animal in pain.

===Chinese===

每讲完一段，威廉都得把那撮地毯流苏从嘴上摘下来——它固执地服从地心引力，而不服从威廉对胡子应有样式的见解。

“不过是染的。亨利家的大门正在刷蓝漆。”一位批评家有气无力地说，但总的说来，蓝狗让亡命徒帮打了一场响当当的胜仗。

接着，就在他们围着那条倒霉的狗静静赞叹的时候，隔壁传来一个声音，一个轻柔的声音，像风穿过树林的叹息。它起了又落，落了又起。每重复一遍，音量就大一分，到最高处听来像一头痛苦的野兽。

===Original===

“What’s that?” asked the audience breathlessly.

William was slightly uneasy. He was not sure whether this fresh development would add lustre or dishonour to his show.

“Yes,” he said darkly to gain time, “what is it? I guess you’d like to know what it is!”

“Garn! It’s jus’ snorin’.”

===Chinese===

“那是什么？”观众屏住呼吸问。

威廉有点不安。他拿不准这个新节目会给他的展览增光还是抹黑。

“是啊，”他故弄玄虚地拖延时间，“那是什么呢？我看你们很想知道那是什么吧！”

“去你的！不就是打呼噜嘛。”

===Original===

“Snorin’!” repeated William. “It’s not ornery snorin’, that isn’t. Jus’ listen, that’s all! You couldn’t snore like that, I bet. Huh!”

They listened spellbound to the gentle sound, growing louder and louder till at its loudest it brought rapt smiles to their faces, then ceasing abruptly, then silence. Then again the gentle sound that grew and grew.

William asked Henry in a stage whisper if they oughtn’t to charge extra for listening to it. The audience hastily explained that they weren’t listening, they “jus’ couldn’t help hearin’.”

A second batch of sightseers had arrived and were paying their entrance pennies, but the first batch refused to move. William, emboldened by success, opened the door and they crept out to the landing and listened with ears pressed to the magic door.

===Chinese===

“打呼噜！”威廉重复道，“那可不是寻常的呼噜，可不是。你们就听听，光听听就知道了！你们谁也打不出那样的呼噜，我敢打赌。哼！”

他们听得入了迷：那轻柔的声音越来越响，响到顶点时他们脸上绽开了如痴如醉的笑容，随后戛然而止，一片寂静。然后那轻柔的声音又起，越来越响。

威廉用舞台腔的耳语问亨利，听这个是不是该额外收费。观众赶紧声明他们没在听，他们“就是想不听也办不到”。

第二批看客到了，正在付门票便士，可第一批说什么也不肯挪窝。威廉被成功壮了胆，打开房门，大家蹑手蹑脚摸到楼梯口，把耳朵贴在那扇有魔力的门上听。

===Original===

Henry now did the honours of showman. William stood, majestic in his glorious apparel, deep in thought. Then to his face came the faint smile that inspiration brings to her votaries. He ordered the audience back into the showroom and shut the door. Then he took off his shoes and softly and with bated breath opened Aunt Emily’s door and peeped within. It was rather a close afternoon, and she lay on her bed on the top of her eiderdown. She had slipped off her dress skirt so as not to crush it, and she lay in her immense stature in a blouse and striped petticoat, while from her open mouth issued the fascinating sounds. In sleep Aunt Emily was not beautiful.

William thoughtfully propped up a cushion in the doorway and stood considering the situation.

===Chinese===

这会儿是亨利在尽主人之谊。威廉一身华服站在那里，威风凛凛，陷入了沉思。随后他脸上浮起灵感赐予其信徒的那抹浅笑。他把观众赶回展厅，关上门。然后他脱掉鞋子，屏住呼吸，轻轻推开埃米莉姨妈的房门，朝里窥探。那天下午相当闷热，她躺在床上的鸭绒被上面。为了不压皱裙子，她把裙身褪了下来，庞大的身躯穿着女式衬衫和条纹衬裙躺在那里，那引人入胜的声音正从她张开的嘴里发出来。睡着了的埃米莉姨妈可不漂亮。

威廉若有所思地在门口支起一个靠垫，站在那儿盘算形势。

===Original===

In a few minutes the showroom was filled with a silent, expectant crowd. In a corner near the door was a new notice:

> Place for taking 

===Chinese===

几分钟后，展厅里挤满了一群不出声、满心期待的观众。门边角落里多了一张新告示：

> 脱鞋子并宣式

===Original===

 off shoes and taking 

 oth of silence

===Chinese===

保持安静的

地方

===Original===

William, after administering the oath of silence to a select party in his most impressive manner led them shoeless and on tiptoe to the next room.

From Aunt Emily’s bed hung another notice:

===Chinese===

威廉以他最郑重其事的姿态，给精选的一小批观众主持完守静宣誓，便领着他们光着脚、踮着脚尖进了隔壁房间。

埃米莉姨妈的床上垂着另一张告示：

===Original===

> Fat wild woman 

 torkin natif 

 langwidge

===Chinese===

> 胖野女人

说的是原生

土话

===Original===

They stood in a hushed, delighted group around her bed. The sounds never ceased, never abated. William only allowed them two minutes in the room. They came out reluctantly, paid more money, joined the end of the queue and reentered. More and more children came to see the show, but the show now consisted solely in Aunt Emily.

The China rat had licked off all its stripes; Smuts was fast asleep; Ginger was sitting down on the seat of a chair and Douglas on the back of it, and Ginger had insisted at last on air and sight and had put his head out where the two sheets joined; the Russian Bear had fallen on to the floor and no one had picked it up; Chips lay in a disconsolate heap, a victim of acute melancholia﻿—and no one cared for any of these things. Newcomers passed by them hurriedly and stood shoeless in the queue outside Aunt Emily’s room eagerly awaiting their turn. Those who came out simply went to the end again to wait another turn. Many returned home for more money, for Aunt Emily was 1d extra and each visit after the first,½d. The Sunday School bell pealed forth its summons, but no one left the show. The vicar was depressed that evening. The attendance at Sunday School had been the worst on record. And still Aunt Emily slept and snored with a rapt, silent crowd around her. But William could never rest content. He possessed ambition that would have put many of his elders to shame. He cleared the room and reopened it after a few minutes, during which his clients waited in breathless suspense.

===Chinese===

他们围在她床边，一群屏息凝神、心花怒放的人。那声音不停不息、不衰不减。威廉只许他们在房里待两分钟。他们恋恋不舍地出来，再掏钱，排到队尾，再进去看。来看展览的孩子越来越多，可这展览如今只剩下埃米莉姨妈一项内容了。

中国鼠把自己身上的条纹舔了个精光；斯马茨睡熟了；金杰坐在椅子的座面上，道格拉斯坐在椅背上，金杰最后坚持要透透气、见见光，把脑袋从两床床单的接缝处伸了出来；俄国熊掉在了地上，没有人捡；薯条蔫成一堆趴着，成了急性忧郁症的牺牲品——而这一切都无人问津。新来的匆匆从它们旁边经过，光着脚在埃米莉姨妈房门外的队伍里排队，热切地等着轮到自己。出来的人径直又排到队尾，再等下一轮。许多人回家去取更多的钱，因为埃米莉姨妈要额外加收一便士，第一遍之后每看一遍再加半便士。主日学校的钟声当当传出它的召唤，可谁也没离开展览。牧师那天晚上很消沉。主日学校的出勤率破了有史以来最差的纪录。而埃米莉姨妈还在睡，还在打呼噜，身边围着一群屏息凝神的观众。可威廉从来不知满足。他怀有的雄心，足以叫许多长辈汗颜。他清了场，几分钟后重新开门——这几分钟里，他的主顾们屏住呼吸、悬着心等着。

===Original===

When they reentered there was a fresh exhibit. William’s keen eye had been searching out each detail of the room. On the table by her bed now stood a glass containing teeth, that William had discovered on the washstand, and a switch of hair and a toothless comb, that William had discovered on the dressing-table. These all bore notices:

> Fat wild 

===Chinese===

再进场时，多了一件新展品。威廉的锐眼早已把房里每个细节搜罗了一遍。她床边的小桌上，如今摆着一只盛假牙的玻璃杯——那是威廉在脸盆架上发现的——一束假发和一把缺齿的梳子——那是威廉在梳妆台上发现的。它们都带着告示：

> 胖野

===Original===

 woman’s 

 teeth

===Chinese===

女人的

牙

===Original===

> Fat wild 

 woman’s 

 hare

===Chinese===

> 胖野

女人的

头法

===Original===

> Fat wild 

 woman’s 

 kome

===Chinese===

> 胖野

女人的

疏子

===Original===

Were it not that the slightest noise meant instant expulsion from the show (some of their number had already suffered that bitter fate) there would have been no restraining the audience. As it was, they crept in, silent, expectant, thrilled, to watch and listen for the blissful two minutes. And Aunt Emily never failed them. Still she slept and snored. They borrowed money recklessly from each other. The poor sold their dearest treasures to the rich, and still they came again and again. And still Aunt Emily slept and snored. It would be interesting to know how long this would have gone on, had she not, on the top note of a peal that was a pure delight to her audience, awakened with a start and glanced around her. At first she thought that the cluster of small boys around her was a dream, especially as they turned and fled precipitately at once. Then she sat up and her eye fell upon the table by her bed, the notices, and finally upon the petrified horror-stricken showman. She sprang up and, seizing him by the shoulders, shook him till his teeth chattered, the tinsel crown fell down, encircling ears and nose, and one of his moustaches fell limply at his feet.

===Chinese===

若不是一点最轻微的响动就意味着立刻逐出展览（他们中已有几位领教过这苦涩的命运），观众早就按捺不住了。即便如此，他们还是蹑手蹑脚地进来，不出声，满心期待，激动万分，守着那幸福的两分钟看和听。埃米莉姨妈也从没让他们失望。她照睡不误，照打呼噜不误。他们无所顾忌地互相借钱。穷人把自己最心爱的宝贝卖给富人，还是一遍又一遍地来。埃米莉姨妈还是又睡又呼。要不是她在一串令观众纯然愉悦的呼噜的最高音上猛然惊醒、环顾四周，真想知道这样还能持续多久。起初她以为围在床边的那一小群男孩是个梦，尤其他们转身立刻逃了个精光。随后她坐起身，目光落在床边的桌子上、那些告示上，最后落在吓僵了的、魂飞魄散的主持人身上。她跳起来，揪住他的肩膀摇晃，摇得他牙齿咯咯打颤，锡箔王冠滑下来，套住了耳朵和鼻子，一边的胡子软塌塌地落在脚边。

===Original===

“You wicked boy!” she said as she shook him, “you *wicked*, *wicked*, *wicked* boy!”

He escaped from her grasp and fled to the showroom, where, in sheer self-defence, he moved a table and three chairs across the door. The room was empty except for Henry, the blue dog, and the still sleeping Smuts. All that was left of the giant was the crumpled sheets. Douglas had, with an awestricken “By Jove!” snatched up his rat as he fled. The last of their clients was seen scrambling along the top of the garden wall on all fours with all possible speed.

Mechanically William straightened his crown.

“She’s woke,” he said. “She’s mad wild.”

===Chinese===

“你这个坏孩子！”她一边摇一边说，“你这个*坏*、*坏*、*坏*透了的孩子！”

他从她手里挣脱，逃进展厅，纯粹出于自卫，把一张桌子和三把椅子横在门后。房间里空无一人，只剩亨利、蓝狗和还在睡的斯马茨。巨人只剩两床皱成一团的床单。道格拉斯逃走时惊叹一声“天哪！”，顺手捞走了他的老鼠。他们的最后一位主顾，正四肢着地以最快速度顺着花园墙头爬远。

威廉机械地正了正王冠。

“她醒了，”他说，“她火大了。”

===Original===

He listened apprehensively for angry footsteps descending the stairs and his father’s dread summons, but none came. Aunt Emily could be heard moving about in her room, but that was all. A wild hope came to him that, given a little time, she might forget the incident.

“Let’s count the money﻿—” said Henry at last.

They counted.

“Four an’ six!” screamed William. “Four an’ six! Jolly good, I *should* say! An’ it would only have been about two shillings without Aunt Emily, an’ I thought of her, didn’t I? I guess you can all be jolly grateful to me.”

===Chinese===

他提心吊胆地等着愤怒的脚步声下楼、等父亲那可怕的传唤，可什么也没来。只听得见埃米莉姨妈在房里走动，仅此而已。他心里升起一个大胆的希望：只要给她一点时间，她兴许会把这桩事忘了。

“咱们数数钱吧——”亨利终于说。

他们数了。

“四先令六便士！”威廉尖叫起来，“四先令六便士！我说，真不赖！要没有埃米莉姨妈，也就两先令上下，是我想到她的，对不对？我看你们都该好好感激我。”

===Original===

“All right,” said Henry unkindly. “I’m not envying you, am I? You’re welcome to it when she tells your father.”

And William’s proud spirits dropped.

Then came the opening of the fateful door and heavy steps descending the stairs.

William’s mother had returned from her weekly visit to her friend. She was placing her umbrella in the stand as Aunt Emily, hatted and coated and carrying a bag, descended. William’s father had just awakened from his peaceful Sunday afternoon slumber, and, hearing his wife, had come into the hall.

===Chinese===

“行啊，”亨利不厚道地说，“我又没眼馋你，对吧？等她告诉你父亲，有你受的。”

威廉那股骄傲劲儿泄了下去。

接着，那扇命运攸关的门开了，沉重的脚步声下了楼。

威廉的母亲拜访完朋友回来了。她正把伞插进伞架，埃米莉姨妈戴着帽子、穿着外套、拎着一只提包下了楼。威廉的父亲刚从星期天下午安稳的酣睡中醒来，听见妻子，走进了门厅。

===Original===

Aunt Emily fixed her eye upon him.

“Will you be good enough to procure a conveyance?” she said. “After the indignities to which I have been subjected in this house I refuse to remain in it a moment longer.”

Quivering with indignation she gave details of the indignities to which she had been subjected. William’s mother pleaded, apologised, coaxed. William’s father went quietly out to procure a conveyance. When he returned she was still talking in the hall.

“A crowd of vulgar little boys,” she was saying, “and horrible indecent placards all over the room.”

===Chinese===

埃米莉姨妈的目光钉住了他。

“劳您的驾，去给我叫辆车好吗？”她说，“在这座房子里蒙受了这样的羞辱之后，我一刻也不愿再多待了。”

她气得浑身发抖，历数了自己蒙受的种种羞辱。威廉的母亲又求情、又道歉、又哄劝。威廉的父亲悄悄出门去叫车。他回来时，她还在门厅里说着。

“一群粗俗的小男孩，”她正说着，“满屋子还都是不堪入目的牌子。”

===Original===

He carried her bag down to the cab.

“And me in my state of health,” she said as she followed him. From the cab she gave her parting shot.

“And if this horrible thing hadn’t happened, I might have stayed with you all the winter and perhaps part of the spring.”

William’s father wiped his brow with his handkerchief as the cab drove off.

===Chinese===

他把她的提包拎下去，放到马车上。

“就我这身子骨。”她跟在后面说。临上车，她放出了最后一炮。

“要不是出了这桩可怕的事，我本来可以在你们这儿住上一冬天，说不定还有一部分春天呢。”

马车驶走时，威廉的父亲用手帕抹了抹额头。

===Original===

“How dreadful!” said his wife, but she avoided meeting his eye. “It’s﻿—it’s *disgraceful* of William,” she went on with sudden spirit. “You must speak to him.”

“I will,” said his father determinedly. “William!” he shouted sternly from the hall.

William’s heart sank.

“She’s told,” he murmured, his last hope gone.

===Chinese===

“太可怕了！”他妻子说，但她躲开了他的目光。“威廉这——这*太不像话了*，”她突然来了精神，接着说，“你必须说说他。”

“我会的。”他父亲斩钉截铁。“威廉！”他从门厅厉声喊道。

威廉的心往下一沉。

“她告状了。”他喃喃地说，最后的希望破灭了。

===Original===

“You’d better go and get it over,” advised Henry.

“William!” repeated the voice still more fiercely.

Henry moved nearer the window, prepared for instant flight if the voice’s owner should follow it up the stairs.

“Go on,” he urged. “He’ll only come up for you.”

===Chinese===

“你还是下去挨这一顿吧。”亨利出主意。

“威廉！”那嗓门又喊了一遍，更凶了。

亨利朝窗户挪近了些，准备着那嗓门的主人一旦追上楼来就立刻逃命。

“去吧，”他催道，“他反正会上来抓你的。”

===Original===

William slowly removed the barricade and descended the stairs. He had remembered to take off the crown and dressing gown, but his one-sided moustache still hung limply over his mouth.

His father was standing in the hall.

“What’s that horrible thing on your face?” he began.

“Whiskers,” answered William laconically.

===Chinese===

威廉慢慢挪开桌椅的封锁，下了楼。他没忘记摘掉王冠、脱下晨衣，可那半边胡子还软塌塌地挂在嘴上。

他父亲站在门厅里。

“你脸上那个可怕的东西是什么？”他开口道。

“胡子。”威廉言简意赅。

===Original===

His father accepted the explanation.

“Is it true,” he went on, “that you actually took your friends into your aunt’s room without permission and hung vulgar placards around it?”

William glanced up into his father’s face and suddenly took hope. Mr. Brown was no actor.

“Yes,” he admitted.

===Chinese===

他父亲接受了这个解释。

“是不是真的，”他接着说，“你真的未经许可带你的朋友们进了你姨妈的房间，还在屋里挂满了粗俗的牌子？”

威廉抬眼看了看父亲的脸，突然生出了希望。布朗先生不是个会做戏的人。

“是真的。”他承认。

===Original===

“It’s disgraceful,” said Mr. Brown, “*disgraceful!* That’s all.”

But it was not quite all. Something hard and round slipped into William’s hand. He ran lightly upstairs.

“Hello!” said Henry, surprised. “That’s not taken long. What﻿—”

William opened his hand and showed something that shone upon his extended palm.

“Look!” he said. “Crumbs! Look!” It was a bright half-crown.

===Chinese===

“太不像话了，”布朗先生说，“*太不像话了*！没别的好说。”

可并不止于此。一样又硬又圆的东西溜进了威廉的手心。他脚步轻轻地上楼去了。

“喂！”亨利吃了一惊，“这么快就完事了？什么——”

威廉摊开手，掌心上一件亮闪闪的东西。

“看！”他说，“乖乖！你看！”那是一枚亮晶晶的半克朗银币。

## IX William and White Satin / 九 威廉与白缎子

===Original===
“I’d simply love to have a page,” murmured Miss Grant wistfully. “A wedding seems so﻿—second-rate without a page.”

Mrs. Brown, her aunt and hostess, looked across the tea-table at her younger son, who was devouring iced cake with that disregard for consequences which is the mark of youth.

“There’s William,” she said doubtfully. Then, “You’ve had quite enough cake, William.”

Miss Grant studied William’s countenance, which at that moment expressed intense virtue persecuted beyond all bearing.

===Chinese===
「我真是太想有个小侍童了，」格兰特小姐满怀向往地低语道，「没有小侍童的婚礼，总显得那么——次一等。」

布朗太太——她是格兰特小姐的姨母兼女主人——隔着茶桌望向自己的小儿子，他正大口吞吃糖霜蛋糕，那副不顾后果的架势正是青春的标志。

「威廉不是现成的嘛。」她迟疑地说。接着又道：「你的蛋糕吃得够多了，威廉。」

格兰特小姐端详着威廉的面孔，那张脸此刻表达的是一种备受迫害、忍无可忍的强烈美德。

===Original===
“*Enough!*” he repeated. “I’ve had hardly any yet. I was only jus’ beginning to have some when you looked at me. It’s a plain cake. It won’t do me any harm. I wu’nt eat it if it’d do me any harm. Sugar’s *good* for you. Animals eat it to keep healthy. *Horses* eat it an’ it don’t do ’em any *harm*, an’ poll parrots an’ things eat it an’ it don’t do ’em any﻿—”

“Oh, don’t argue, William,” said his mother wearily.

William’s gift of eloquence was known and feared in his family circle.

===Chinese===
「*够多了*！」他重复道，「我还没怎么吃呢。你刚才看我的时候，我才刚开始吃。这是块素蛋糕。它对我没一点儿坏处。要是它对我有坏处我就不吃了。糖对人*好*着呢。动物们都吃糖保健。*马*就吃糖，糖对它们一点儿*坏处*也没有，鹦鹉啥的也吃糖，糖对它们也没有——」

「哦，别跟我犟，威廉。」他的母亲疲惫地说。

威廉的雄辩天才是全家上下闻名而且闻风丧胆的。

===Original===
Then Miss Grant brought out the result of her study of his countenance.

“He’s got such a﻿—*modern* face!” she said. “There’s something essentially medieval and romantic about the idea of a page.”

Mrs. Brown (from whose house the wedding was to take place) looked worried.

“There’s nothing medieval or romantic about William,” she said.

===Chinese===
接着，格兰特小姐宣布了她端详的结论。

「他这张脸长得多——多*现代*呀！」她说，「小侍童这个主意，骨子里就有一种中世纪的浪漫。」

布朗太太（婚礼就要从她家操办）面露忧色。

「威廉身上可没有一点儿中世纪或者浪漫的东西。」她说。

===Original===
“Well,”﻿—Miss Grant’s intellectual face lit up﻿—“what about his cousin Dorita. They’re about the same age, aren’t they? Both eleven. Well, the *two* of them in white satin with bunches of holly. Don’t you think? Would you mind having her to stay for the ceremony?” (Miss Grant always referred to her wedding as “the ceremony.”) “If you don’t have his hair cut for a bit, he mightn’t look so bad?”

William had retired to the garden with his three bosom friends﻿—Ginger, Henry, and Douglas﻿—where he was playing his latest game of mountaineering. A plank had been placed against the garden wall, and up this scrambled the three, roped together and wearing feathers in their caps. William was wearing an old golf cap of his mother’s, and mentally pictured himself as an impressive and heroic figure. Before they reached the top they invariably lost their foothold, rolled down the plank and fell in a confused and bruised heap at the bottom. The bruises in no way detracted from the charm of the game. To William the fascination of any game consisted mainly in the danger to life and limb involved. The game had been suggested by an old alpenstock which had been thoughtlessly presented to William by a friend of Mr. Brown’s. The paint of the staircase and upstairs corridor had been completely ruined before the family knew of the gift, and the alpenstock had been confiscated for a week, then restored on the condition that it was not to be brought into the house. The result was the game of mountaineering up the plank. They carried the alpenstock in turns, but William had two turns running to mark the fact that he was its proud possessor.

Mrs. Brown approached William on the subject of his prospective role of page with a certain apprehension. The normal attitude of William’s family towards William was one of apprehension.

===Chinese===
「那么，」——格兰特小姐那张才女的脸亮了起来——「他表妹多丽塔怎么样？他们俩差不多大，是不是？都十一岁。让*他们俩*穿上白缎子，捧上一束束冬青。你说呢？让她来住到典礼结束，你不介意吧？」（格兰特小姐总把她的婚礼称作「典礼」。）「要是先别给他剪头发，他说不定没那么糟？」

威廉已经带着他的三个心腹好友——金杰、亨利和道格拉斯——退到花园里去了，正在玩他最新发明的登山游戏。一块厚木板斜搭在花园墙上，三个用绳子拴在一起、帽子上插着羽毛的小家伙就顺着木板往上爬。威廉戴着他母亲的一顶旧高尔夫球帽，心里把自己想象成一个威风凛凛的英雄形象。他们还没爬到顶就必定失足，顺着木板滚下来，摔在底下一堆青一块紫一团的小身子里。这些乌青一点儿不减游戏的魅力。对威廉来说，任何游戏的迷人之处主要就在那种危及性命和手脚的危险上。这游戏是一位布朗先生的朋友没过脑子送给威廉的一根旧登山杖引出来的。等全家人知道这份礼物时，楼梯和楼上走廊的漆已经被刮了个彻底干净，登山杖被没收了一个星期，后来归还的条件是不许再拿进屋。结果就有了沿着木板登山的游戏。他们轮流扛登山杖，而威廉连扛两轮，以标明他才是它骄傲的主人。

布朗太太怀着几分忐忑，向威廉提起他即将出任小侍童的话题。威廉的家人对威廉的一贯态度，本来就是忐忑。

===Original===
“Would you like to go to Cousin Sybil’s wedding?” she said.

“No, I wu’nt,” said William without hesitation.

“Wouldn’t you like to go dressed up?” she said.

“Red Injun?” said William with a gleam of hope.

===Chinese===
「你愿意去参加西比尔表姐的婚礼吗？」她说。

「不，我才不愿。」威廉毫不犹豫地说。

「你不愿意穿得漂漂亮亮地去吗？」她说。

「扮印第安人？」威廉带着一线希望说。

===Original===
“Er﻿—no, not exactly.”

“Pirate?”

“Not quite.”

“I’d go as a Red Injun, or I’d go as a Pirate,” he said firmly, “but I wu’nt go as anything else.”

“A page,” said Miss Grant’s clear, melodious voice, “is a medieval and romantic idea, William. There’s the glamour of chivalry about it that should appeal strongly to a boy of your age.”

===Chinese===
「呃——不，不完全是。」

「扮海盗？」

「也不太是。」

「扮印第安人我去，扮海盗我也去，」他斩钉截铁地说，「可扮成别的，我死也不去。」

「小侍童呀，」格兰特小姐清脆悦耳的嗓音说道，「是个中世纪式的浪漫主意，威廉。它身上有骑士精神的光彩，对你这个年纪的男孩应该有强烈的吸引力。」

===Original===
William turned his inscrutable countenance upon her and gave her a cold glare.

They discussed his costume in private.

“I’ve got a pair of lovely white silk stockings,” said his mother. “They’d do for tights, and Ethel has got a satin petticoat that’s just beginning to go in one place. I should think we could make some sort of costume from that, don’t you? We’ll buy some more white satin and get some patterns.”

“No, I won’t wear Ethel’s ole clothes,” said William smouldering. “You all jus’ want to make me look ridiclus. You don’t care how ridiclus I look. I shall be ridiclus all the rest of my life goin’ about in Ethel’s ole clothes. I jus’ won’t do it. I jus’ won’t go to any ole weddin’. No, I *don’t* want to see Cousin Sybil married, an’ I jus’ *won’t* be made look ridiclus in Ethel’s ole clothes.”

===Chinese===
威廉把那张高深莫测的脸转向她，冷冷瞪了她一眼。

他们私下里讨论了他的行头。

「我有一双挺好的白丝袜，」他的母亲说，「可以当紧身裤袜用。埃塞尔有条缎子衬裙，正好有一处开始磨坏了。我想咱们能用它拼出一套行头来，你说呢？咱们再买些白缎子，买几张纸样。」

「不行，我不穿埃塞尔的破旧衣服。」威廉闷着一肚子火说，「你们就是想让我出洋相。我出多大的洋相你们都不在乎。往后一辈子我都得穿着埃塞尔的破旧衣服到处出洋相。我就是不干。我就是不去参加什么破婚礼。不，我*就是不想*看西比尔表姐出嫁，我就是*不*穿着埃塞尔的破旧衣服出洋相。」

===Original===
They reasoned and coaxed and threatened, but in vain. Finally William yielded to parental authority and went about his world with an air of a martyr doomed to the stake. Even the game of mountaineering had lost its charm and the alpenstock lay neglected against the garden wall. The attitude of his select circle of friends was not encouraging.

“Yah! *Page!* Who’s goin’ to be a *page*? Oh, crumbs. A page all dressed up in white. *Dear* little Willie. Won’t he look swe-e-e-et?”

Life became very full. It was passed chiefly in the avenging of insults. William cherished a secret hope that the result of this would be to leave him disfigured for life and so unable to attend the wedding. However, except for a large lump on his forehead, he was none the worse. He eyed the lump thoughtfully in his looking-glass and decided that with a little encouragement it might render his public appearance in an affair of romance an impossibility. But the pain which resulted from one heroic effort at banging it against the wall caused him to abandon the plan.

===Chinese===
他们又讲道理又哄又吓，全都没用。最后威廉屈从于父母的权威，带着一副被判了火刑的殉道者的神情在自己的天地里走动。连登山游戏都失了魅力，登山杖被冷落在花园墙边。他那个精选朋友圈的态度更不给力。

「哈！*小侍童*！谁要当*小侍童*呀？哦，天哪。穿一身白的小侍童。*亲爱*的小威威。他是不是甜——得——腻——人呀？」

日子变得非常充实，主要都用来报仇雪耻了。威廉暗暗希望，这样报仇的结果会让他落下终身破相，从而去不成婚礼。然而，除了额头上一个大包，他毫发无损。他对着镜子若有所思地端详那个包，断定只要稍加鼓动，它也许就能让他无法在一桩浪漫大事上公开露面了。可是拿它往墙上英勇地撞了一回之后，随之而来的疼痛让他放弃了这个计划。

===Original===
Dorita arrived the next week, and with her her small brother, Michael, aged three. Dorita was slim and graceful, with a pale little oval face and dark curling hair.

Miss Grant received her on the doorstep.

“Well, my little maid of honour?” she said in her flute-like tones. “Welcome! We’re going to be such friends﻿—you and me and William﻿—the bride” (she blushed and bridled becomingly) “and her little page and her little maid of honour. William’s a boy, and he’s just a *leetle* bit thoughtless and doesn’t realise the romance of it all. I’m sure you will. I see it in your dear little face. We’ll have some lovely talks together.” Her eyes fell upon Michael and narrowed suddenly. “He’d look sweet, too, in white satin, wouldn’t he?” turning to Mrs. Brown. “He could walk between them.﻿ ﻿… We could buy some more white satin.﻿ ﻿…”

When they had gone the maid of honour turned dark, long-lashed, demure eyes upon William.

===Chinese===
第二个星期，多丽塔到了，跟她一起来的还有她三岁的小弟弟迈克尔。多丽塔苗条优雅，小小的鹅蛋脸略显苍白，一头深色鬈发。

格兰特小姐在门口台阶上迎接她。

「哟，我的小女傧相？」她用长笛般的声调说，「欢迎！我们要成为顶好的朋友——你、我，还有威廉——新娘，」（她红着脸，娇态可掬地一昂头）「和她的小侍童、她的小女傧相。威廉是个男孩子，就是有那么一*咪咪*点粗心，意识不到这一切的浪漫。我肯定你会懂的。我从你亲爱的小脸上看得出来。咱们会有许多美妙的谈心。」她的目光落到迈克尔身上，突然眯了起来。「他穿上白缎子也会很好看的，是不是？」她转向布朗太太，「他可以走在他们俩中间。……咱们可以再买些白缎子。……」

他们走后，小女傧相把她那双睫毛长长、端庄娴静的黑眼睛转向威廉。

===Original===
“Soft mug, that,” she said in clear refined tones, nodding in the direction of the door through which the tall figure of Miss Grant had just disappeared.

William was vaguely cheered by her attitude.

“Are you keen on this piffling wedding affair?” she went on carelessly, “ ’cause I jolly well tell you I’m not.”

William felt that he had found a kindred spirit. He unbent so far as to take her to the stable and show her a field-mouse he had caught and was keeping in a cardboard box.

===Chinese===
「十足一个软面瓜，那位。」她用清晰文雅的腔调说，朝格兰特小姐高挑的身影刚刚消失的那扇门点了点头。

她这个态度让威廉隐隐地振奋起来。

「你对这桩鸡毛蒜皮的破婚礼来劲吗？」她漫不经心地继续说，「因为我得明明白白告诉你，我一点儿也不来劲。」

威廉觉得自己找到了一个志同道合的灵魂。他屈尊降贵到肯带她去马厩，看一只他抓来养在纸盒里的田鼠。

===Original===
“I’m teachin’ it to dance,” he confided, “an’ it oughter fetch a jolly lot of money when it can dance proper. Dancin’ mice do, you know. They show ’em on the stage, and people on the stage get pounds an’ pounds every night, so I bet mice do, too﻿—at least the folks the mice belong to what dance on the stage. I’m teachin’ it to dance by holdin’ a biscuit over its head and movin’ it about. It bit me twice yesterday.” He proudly displayed his mutilated finger. “I only caught it yesterday. It oughter learn all right today,” he added hopefully.

Her intense disappointment, when the only trace of the field-mouse that could be found was the cardboard box with a hole gnawed at one corner, drew William’s heart to her still more.

He avoided Henry, Douglas and Ginger. Henry, Douglas and Ginger had sworn to be at the church door to watch William descend from the carriage in the glory of his white satin apparel, and William felt that friendship could not stand the strain.

===Chinese===
「我在教它跳舞，」他推心置腹地说，「等它学像了，准能换回好一大笔钱。跳舞的老鼠都这样，你知道吧。人家在舞台上表演它们，舞台上的人一晚上挣好多好多镑，所以我敢说老鼠也挣——至少老鼠的主人是这么挣的。我教它跳舞的办法是拿一块饼干举在它头顶上晃来晃去。它昨天咬了我两口。」他骄傲地亮出那只伤痕累累的手指。「我昨天才抓到它。它今天保准能学会。」他满怀希望地补充道。

当田鼠唯一找得到的踪迹只剩那只被啃掉一角的纸盒时，她那份痛心失望，让威廉的心跟她贴得更近了。

他躲着亨利、道格拉斯和金杰。亨利、道格拉斯和金杰已经发誓要到教堂门口，亲眼观看威廉一身白缎子行头的荣光里走下马车，而威廉觉得，友谊经不起这种考验。

===Original===
He sat with Dorita on the cold and perilous perch of the garden wall and discussed Cousin Sybil and the wedding. Dorita’s language delighted and fascinated William.

“She’s a soppy old loony,” she would remark sweetly, shaking her dark curls. “The soppiest old loony you’d see in any old place on *this* old earth, you betcher life! She’s made of sop. I wouldn’t be found dead in a ditch with her﻿—wouldn’t touch her with the butt-end of a bargepole. She’s an assified cow, she is. Humph!”

“Those children are a *leetle* disappointing as regards character﻿—to a child lover like myself,” confided Miss Grant to her intellectual fiancé. “I’ve tried to sound their depths, but there are no depths to sound. There is none of the mystery, the glamour, the ‘clouds of glory’ about them. They are so﻿—so material.”

The day of the ordeal drew nearer and nearer, and William’s spirits sank lower and lower. His life seemed to stretch before him﻿—youth, manhood, and old age﻿—dreary and desolate, filled only with humiliation and shame. His prestige and reputation would be blasted forever. He would no longer be William﻿—the Red Indian, the pirate, the daredevil. He would simply be the Boy Who Went to a Wedding Dressed in White Satin. Evidently there would be a surging crowd of small boys at the church door. Every boy for miles round who knew William even by sight had volunteered the information that he would be there. William was to ride with Dorita and Michael in the bride’s carriage. In imagination he already descended from the carriage and heard the chorus of jeers. His cheeks grew hot at the thought. His life for years afterwards would consist solely in the avenging of insults. He followed the figure of the blushing bride-to-be with a baleful glare. In his worst moments he contemplated murder. The violence of his outburst when his mother mildly suggested a wedding present to the bride from her page and maid of honour horrified her.

===Chinese===
他和多丽塔并肩坐在花园墙头那又冷又险的地方，谈论西比尔表姐和那场婚礼。多丽塔的谈吐让威廉听得心花怒放、如痴如醉。

「她是个腻乎乎的老傻蛋。」她会甜甜地发表高见，晃着深色的鬈发，「在*咱们*这个老地球上随便哪个老地方，你都找不出比她更腻乎的老傻蛋了，拿性命打赌！她整个人都是泡烂的面包做的。跟她一起死在沟里我都嫌丢人——拿驳船篙子的粗屁股那头碰她一下我都不干。她是一头蠢驴附体的母牛，她就是。哼！」

「那两个孩子，就品性而言，让我这个热爱儿童的人有*一咪咪*点失望。」格兰特小姐向她那位知识分子未婚夫吐露道，「我试着探一探他们心灵的深浅，可他们根本没有深浅可探。他们身上没有神秘，没有光彩，没有那种『荣光的云霞』。他们是那么的——那么的物质。」

受难的日子一天天逼近，威廉的情绪一天天低落。他的一生仿佛在眼前铺展开来——少年、成年、老年——凄凄惶惶，只有耻辱和羞愧填满。他的威望和名声将永远扫地。他不再是威廉——那个印第安人、那个海盗、那个亡命徒。他只会变成「那个穿白缎子去参加婚礼的男孩」。教堂门口显然会聚起汹涌的一大群小男孩。方圆几英里内凡是见过威廉的男孩，个个都放出话来说他一定到场。威廉要跟多丽塔和迈克尔一起坐新娘的马车。他已经在想象中走下马车，听到了齐声的哄笑。一想到这个他就脸颊发烫。往后好些年，他的生活将只剩报仇雪耻一件事。他恶狠狠地盯着那位准新娘来来往往的背影。最难熬的时候他甚至盘算过谋杀。当母亲温和地提议，小侍童和小女傧相该给新娘送一份结婚礼物时，他爆发的那股戾气把她吓坏了。

===Original===
“I’m bein’ made look ridiclus all the rest of my life,” he ended. “I’m not givin’ her no present. I know what I’d *like* to give her,” he added darkly.

“Yes, and I *do*, too.”

Mrs. Brown forebore to question further.

The day of the wedding dawned coldly bright and sunny. William’s expressions of agony and complaints of various startling symptoms of serious illnesses were ignored by his experienced family circle.

===Chinese===
「我这辈子剩下的大好年华都得出洋相了。」他这样收尾，「我什么礼物也不给她。我知道我*倒想*送她点什么。」他阴沉沉地补了一句。

「是啊，而且我*也*知道。」

布朗太太忍住没有再问下去。

婚礼那天破晓，天色清冷明亮，阳光灿烂。威廉表达的种种痛苦，以及对各种骇人重症症状的诉苦，全被他见多识广的一家人置之不理。

===Original===
Michael was dressed first of the three in his minute white satin suit and sent down into the morning-room to play quietly. Then an unwilling William was captured from the darkest recess of the stable and dragged pale and protesting to the slaughter.

“Yes, an’ I’ll *die* pretty soon, prob’ly,” he said pathetically, “and then p’r’aps you’ll be a bit sorry, an’ I shan’t care.”

In Michael there survived two of the instincts of primitive man, the instinct of foraging for food and that of concealing it from his enemies when found. Earlier in the day he had paid a visit to the kitchen and found it empty. Upon the table lay a pound of butter and a large bag of oranges. These he had promptly confiscated and, with a fear of interruption born of experience, he had retired with them under the table in the morning-room. Before he could begin his feast he had been called upstairs to be dressed for the ceremony. On his return (immaculate in white satin) he found to his joy that his treasure trove had not been discovered. He began on the butter first. What he could not eat he smeared over his face and curly hair. Then he felt a sudden compunction and tried to remove all traces of the crime by rubbing his face and hair violently with a woolly mat. Then he sat down on the Chesterfield and began the oranges. They were very yellow and juicy and rather overripe. He crammed them into his mouth with both little fat hands at once. He was well aware, even at his tender years, that life’s sweetest joys come soonest to an end. Orange juice mingled with wool fluff and butter on his small round face. It trickled down his cheeks and fell on to his white lace collar. His mouth and the region round it were completely yellow. He had emptied the oranges out of the bag all around him on the seat. He was sitting in a pool of juice. His suit was covered with it, mingled with pips and skin, and still he ate on.

His first interruption was William and Dorita, who came slowly downstairs holding hands in silent sympathy, two gleaming figures in white satin. They walked to the end of the room. They also had been sent to the morning-room with orders to “play quietly” until summoned.

===Chinese===
三个孩子里迈克尔头一个穿上他那套小小的白缎子衣服，被打发到晨间起居室里安安静静地玩。接着，一百个不情愿的威廉从马厩最黑的角落里被逮了出来，脸色苍白、一路抗议地被拖去受刑。

「是啊，说不定我很快*就*要死了，」他悲悲切切地说，「到时候你们没准儿会有点儿难过，可我才不管呢。」

迈克尔身上保留着原始人的两种本能：四处觅食的本能，和找到食物后把它藏起来不让敌人看见的本能。那天早些时候，他光顾了一趟厨房，发现里面空无一人。桌上放着一磅黄油和一大袋橙子。他立刻将其统统没收，凭经验深知随时会被人打断，便带着战利品躲进了晨间起居室的桌子底下。还没来得及开宴，他就被叫上楼去穿典礼的衣服了。等他回来时（一身白缎子，无可挑剔），他欣喜地发现那批宝藏原封未动。他先从黄油下手。吃不下的就抹在脸上和鬈发上。接着他忽然一阵心虚，便抓过一块羊毛垫子使劲搓脸搓头发，想把罪证统统消灭。然后他往切斯特菲尔德长沙发上一坐，开始对付橙子。那些橙子黄澄澄、水汪汪的，熟得过了头。他用两只胖乎乎的小手同时往嘴里塞。哪怕在这么稚嫩的年纪，他也深知人生最甜的欢乐总是结束得最快。橙汁混着羊毛绒和黄油，糊在他那张圆圆的小脸上，顺着脸颊往下淌，滴到白色蕾丝领子上。他的嘴和嘴边一圈全是黄的。他把橙子从袋子里掏空，摆得四周座位上到处都是。他坐在一汪橙汁里。他的衣服上糊满了橙汁，混着橙子核和橙子皮，而他还在吃个不停。

头一个打断他的是威廉和多丽塔。两人手拉着手缓缓下楼，无声地互相怜悯着，是两团亮闪闪的白缎子身影。他们走向屋子那一头。他们也是被打发到晨间起居室、奉命「安安静静地玩」等叫唤的。

===Original===
“*Play?*” William had echoed coldly. “I don’t feel much like *playing*.”

They stared at Michael, openmouthed and speechless. Lumps of butter and bits of wool stuck in his curls and adhered to the upper portion of his face. They had been washed away from the lower portion of it by orange juice. His suit was almost covered with it. Behind he was saturated with it.

“*Crumbs!*” said William at last.

“*You’ll* catch it,” remarked his sister.

===Chinese===
「*玩*？」威廉当时冷冷地应了一句，「我可没什么心思*玩*。」

他们张着嘴，一言不发地盯着迈克尔。一块块黄油、一撮撮羊毛粘在他的鬈发里，贴在他脸的上半部；下半部已经被橙汁冲刷过了。他的衣服几乎整个糊满了橙汁，后身更是湿了个透。

「*天哪！*」威廉终于开口了。

「*你*可要挨收拾了。」他家这位小姐妹说道。

===Original===
Michael retreated hastily from the scene of his misdeeds.

“Mickyth good now,” he lisped deprecatingly.

They looked at the seat he had left﻿—a pool of crushed orange fragments and juice. Then they looked at each other.

“*He’ll* not be able to go,” said Dorita slowly.

===Chinese===
迈克尔慌忙从作案现场撤退。

「米基希现在乖了。」他口齿不清地讨饶道。

他们看着他刚离开的座位——一汪捏烂的橙子渣和橙汁。然后他们互相看了看。

「*他*是去不成了。」多丽塔慢慢地说。

===Original===
Again they looked at the empty orange-covered Chesterfield and again they looked at each other.

“Heth kite good now,” said Michael hopefully.

Then the maid of honour, aware that cold deliberation often kills the most glorious impulses, seized William’s hand.

“Sit down. *Quick!*” she whispered sharply.

===Chinese===
他们又看了看那空了下来的、糊满橙子的切斯特菲尔德长沙发，又互相看了看。

「他希现在可乖可乖了。」迈克尔满怀希望地说。

这时，小女傧相一把抓住威廉的手——她深知，冷冰冰的深思熟虑常常会扼杀最光辉的冲动。

「坐下。*快！*」她尖声低语道。

===Original===
Without a word they sat down. They sat till they felt the cold moisture penetrate to their skins. Then William heaved a deep sigh.

“*We* can’t go now,” he said.

Through the open door they saw a little group coming﻿—Miss Grant in shining white, followed by William’s mother, arrayed in her brightest and best, and William’s father, whose expression revealed a certain weariness mingled with a relief that the whole thing would soon be over.

“Here’s the old sardine all togged up,” whispered Dorita.

===Chinese===
他们一言不发地坐下了。他们一直坐到冰凉的湿意渗进皮肤。然后威廉长叹一声。

「这下*咱们*也去不成了。」他说。

透过敞开的门，他们望见一小队人马走来——格兰特小姐一身雪亮白衣，后面跟着威廉的母亲，穿戴上是一派最鲜亮最体面的行头，再后面是威廉的父亲，脸上的表情透着几分疲惫，又混着一种「这一切很快就要结束」的如释重负。

「瞧这条老沙丁鱼，全副行头武装齐啦。」多丽塔悄声道。

===Original===
“William! Dorita! Michael!” they called.

Slowly William, Dorita and Michael obeyed the summons.

When Miss Grant’s eyes fell upon the strange object that was Michael, she gave a loud scream.

“*Michael!* Oh, the *dreadful* child!”

===Chinese===
「威廉！多丽塔！迈克尔！」他们喊道。

威廉、多丽塔和迈克尔慢吞吞地应召而去。

格兰特小姐的目光一落到迈克尔这个奇怪物体上，就发出一声尖叫。

「*迈克尔*！哦，这个*可怕*的孩子！」

===Original===
She clasped the centre of the door and looked as though about to swoon.

Michael began to sob.

“*Poor* Micky,” he said through his tears. “He feelth tho thick.”

They removed him hastily.

===Chinese===
她抱住门框正中，看上去就要晕倒了。

迈克尔抽抽搭搭哭了起来。

「*可怜*的米基希。」他噙着泪说，「他觉得肚肚好难受。」

众人急忙把他架走了。

===Original===
“Never mind, dear,” said Mrs. Brown soothingly, “the other two look sweet.”

But Mr. Brown had wandered further into the room and thus obtained a sudden and startling view of the page and maid of honour from behind.

“What? Where?” he began explosively.

William and Dorita turned to him instinctively, thus providing Mrs. Brown and the bride with the spectacle that had so disturbed him.

===Chinese===
「别介意，亲爱的，」布朗太太安慰道，「另外两个看着挺俊的。」

可布朗先生已经踱进了屋子深处，于是一下子看见了小侍童和小女傧相的背面，骇了一大跳。

「什么？哪儿？」他炸雷般开了腔。

威廉和多丽塔本能地朝他转过身来，于是让布朗太太和新娘也饱览了那幅刚才搅得他心神不定的景象。

===Original===
The bride gave a second scream﻿—shriller and wilder than the first.

“Oh, what have they done? Oh, the *wretched* children! And just when I wanted to feel *calm*. Just when all depends on my feeling *calm*. Just when﻿—”

“We was walkin’ round the room an’ we sat down on the Chesterfield and there was this stuff on it an’ it came on our clothes,” explained William stonily and monotonously and all in one breath.

“*Why* did you sit down,” said his mother.

===Chinese===
新娘发出了第二声尖叫——比头一声更尖利、更狂乱。

「哦，他们干了什么呀？哦，这俩*可恶*的孩子！偏偏在我想保持*平静*的时候。偏偏在一切都取决于我的*平静*的时候。偏偏在——」

「我们本来在屋里走来走去，然后我们在切斯特菲尔德长沙发上坐下了，那上头本来就有这些玩意儿，它就蹭到我们衣服上了。」威廉石像般面无表情、平铺直叙、一口气全说了。

「你们*为什么*要坐下。」他的母亲说。

===Original===
“We was walkin’ round an’ we jus’ felt tired and we sat down on the Chesterfield and there was this stuff on it an’ it came on﻿—”

“Oh, *stop*! Didn’t you *see* it there?”

William considered.

“Well, we was jus’ walking round the room,” he said, “an’ we jus’ felt tired and we sat﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我们本来在走来走去，就是觉得累了，然后我们就在切斯特菲尔德沙发上坐下了，那上头本来就有这些玩意儿，它就蹭到——」

「哦，*别说了*！你们*没看见*它在上面吗？」

威廉想了想。

「是这样，我们就是在屋里走来走去，」他说，「就是觉得累了，然后我们就坐——」

===Original===
“*Stop* saying that.”

“Couldn’t we make *cloaks*?” wailed the bride, “to hang down and cover them all up behind. It wouldn’t take long﻿—”

Mr. Brown took out his watch.

“The carriage has been waiting a quarter of an hour already,” he said firmly. “We’ve no time to spare. Come along, my dear. We’ll continue the investigation after the service. You can’t go, of course, you must stay at home now,” he ended, turning a stern eye upon William. There was an unconscious note of envy in his voice.

===Chinese===
「*别*再说那个了。」

「咱们能不能做两件*斗篷*？」新娘哀声道，「垂下来把他们后头全遮住。用不了多久的——」

布朗先生掏出怀表。

「马车已经等了一刻钟了。」他语气坚决地说，「我们一分钟也不能耽搁了。走吧，亲爱的。调查嘛，等仪式完了再继续。你当然去不成了，你现在必须待在家里。」他末了向威廉投去严厉的一眼，收了尾。他的声音里有种他自己都没察觉的羡慕。

===Original===
“And I did so *want* to have a page,” said Miss Grant plaintively as she turned away.

Joy and hope returned to William with a bound. As the sound of wheels was heard down the drive he turned head over heels several times on the lawn, then caught sight of his long-neglected alpenstock leaning against a wall.

“Come on,” he shouted joyfully. “I’ll teach you a game I made up. It’s mountaineerin’.”

She watched him place a plank against the wall and begin his perilous ascent.

===Chinese===
「我本来是那么*想要*一个小侍童的。」格兰特小姐转过身去，哀哀地说。

喜悦和希望一蹦一跳地回到了威廉身上。车道尽头传来车轮声时，他在草坪上连翻了几个跟头，然后一眼瞥见他那根被冷落已久的登山杖正靠在墙边。

「来呀，」他欢天喜地地喊道，「我教你一个我发明的游戏。叫登山。」

她看着他往墙上搭起木板，开始他那步步惊心的攀登。

===Original===
“You’re a mug,” she said in her clear, sweet voice. “I know a mountaineering game worth ten of that old thing.”

And it says much for the character and moral force of the maid-of-honour that William meekly put himself in the position of pupil.

It must be explained at this point that the domestics of the Brown household were busy arranging refreshments in a marquee in the garden. The front hall was quite empty.

In about a quarter of an hour the game of mountaineering was in full swing. On the lowest steps of the staircase reposed the mattress from William’s father’s and mother’s bed, above it the mattress from Miss Grant’s bed, above that the mattress from William’s bed, and on the top, the mattress from Dorita’s bed. In all the bedrooms the bedclothes lay in disarray on the floor. A few nails driven through the ends of the mattresses into the stairs secured the stability of the “mountain.” Still wearing their robes of ceremony, they scrambled up in stockinged feet, every now and then losing foothold and rolling down to the pile of pillows and bolsters (taken indiscriminately from all the beds) which was arranged at the foot of the staircase. Their mirth was riotous and uproarious. They used the alpenstock in turns. It was a great help. They could get a firm hold on the mattresses with the point of the alpenstock. William stood at the top of the mountain, hot and panting, his alpenstock in his hand, and paused for breath. He was well aware that retribution was not far off﻿—was in the neighbouring church, to be quite exact, and would return in a carriage within the next few minutes. He was aware that an explanation of the yellow stain was yet to be demanded. He was aware that this was not a use to which the family mattresses could legitimately be put. But he cared for none of these things. In his mind’s eye he only saw a crowd of small boys assembled outside a church door with eager eyes fixed on a carriage from which descended﻿—Miss Grant, Mrs. Brown, and Mr. Brown. His life stretched before him bright and rose-coloured. A smile of triumph curved his lips.

===Chinese===
「你是个傻瓜蛋。」她用清脆甜美的嗓音说，「我知道一个登山游戏，比那老玩意儿强十倍。」

而小女傧相的人格和道德感化力有多强，从一件事上就可见一斑：威廉乖乖地把自己摆到了学生的位置上。

这里必须说明一下：布朗家的仆人们正忙着在花园的大帐篷里摆茶点，前厅里空无一人。

大约一刻钟后，登山游戏已玩得热火朝天。楼梯最底下的台阶上躺的是威廉父母床上的床垫，上面一层是格兰特小姐床上的床垫，再上面是威廉床上的，最顶上的是多丽塔床上的。所有卧室里的被褥都乱七八糟地摊在地板上。几枚钉子穿过床垫头钉进楼梯，固定住这座「山」的稳定性。两人还穿着典礼的行头，只穿着袜子往上爬，不时失足，滚到楼梯脚那一大堆枕头和长枕上（不分彼此地从所有床上搬来的）。他们的欢笑又野又喧。登山杖轮流着用，它可帮了大忙，用杖尖扎住床垫就能抓得很牢。威廉站在山顶上，热气腾腾、气喘吁吁，手里拄着登山杖，停下来歇口气。他心里清楚，报应已经不远——说准确些，就在隔壁的教堂里，而且几分钟内就会坐着马车回来。他清楚那摊黄渍的来历还有得追究。他清楚家里的床垫不是派这种用场的正路。可他什么都不在乎。他心眼里只看见一群小男孩聚在教堂门外，热切的眼睛盯着一辆马车，从车上走下来的是——格兰特小姐、布朗太太和布朗先生。他的一生在眼前铺展开来，明亮而玫瑰色。一抹得意的微笑弯上他的嘴角。

===Original===
“Yah! Who waited at a church for someone what never came? Yah!”

“I hope you didn’t get a bad cold waitin’ for me on Wednesday at the church door.”

“Some folks is easy had. I bet you all believed I was coming on Wednesday.”

Such sentences floated idly through his mind.

===Chinese===
「哈！谁在教堂门口白等了个根本没来的人呀？哈！」

「我希望你们星期三在教堂门口等我，没冻出重感冒吧。」

「有些人就是好糊弄。我敢打赌，你们全都信了我星期三会去。」

这样的句子在他脑子里悠悠地飘来飘去。

===Original===
“I say, my turn for that stick with the spike.”

William handed it to her in silence.

“I say,” she repeated, “what do you think of this marriage business?”

“Dunno,” said William laconically.

===Chinese===
「我说，该轮到我拿那根带尖儿的杖了。」

威廉默默地把杖递给了她。

「我说，」她重复了一遍，「你觉得结婚这档子事怎么样？」

「不知道。」威廉言简意赅地说。

===Original===
“If I’d got to marry,” went on the maid of honour, “I’d as soon marry *you* as anyone.”

“I wu’nt mind,” said the page gallantly. “But,” he added hastily, “in ornery clothes.”

“Oh, yes,” she lost her foothold and rolled down to the pile of pillows. From them came her voice muffled, but clear as ever. “You betcher life. In ornery clothes.”

===Chinese===
「要是我非得结婚的话，」小女傧相继续说，「嫁*你*和嫁别人我都无所谓。」

「我不介意。」小侍童豪爽地说。「不过，」他又赶紧补上一句，「得穿平常衣裳。」

「哦，那是。」她失了脚，滚到了那堆枕头上。枕头堆里传来她的声音，闷闷的，却一如既往地清脆。「拿性命打赌。穿平常衣裳。」

## III William Below Stairs / 三 威廉屈居楼下

===Original===

William was feeling embittered with life in general. He was passing through one of his not infrequent periods of unpopularity. The climax had come with the gift of sixpence bestowed on him by a timid aunt, who hoped thus to purchase his goodwill. With the sixpence he had bought a balloon adorned with the legs and head of a duck fashioned in cardboard. This could be blown up to its fullest extent and then left to subside. It took several minutes to subside, and during those minutes it emitted a long-drawn-out and high-pitched groan. The advantage of this was obvious. William could blow it up to its fullest extent in private and leave it to subside in public concealed beneath his coat. While this was going on William looked round as though in bewildered astonishment. He inflated it before he went to breakfast. He then held it firmly and secretly so as to keep it inflated till he was sitting at the table. Then he let it subside. His mother knocked over a cup of coffee, and his father cut himself with the bread knife. Ethel, his elder sister, indulged in a mild form of nervous breakdown. William sat with a face of startled innocence. But nothing enraged his family so much as William’s expression of innocence. They fell upon him, and he defended himself as well as he could. Yes, he was holding the balloon under the table. Well, he’d blown it up some time ago. He couldn’t keep it blown up forever. He had to let the air out some time. He couldn’t help it making a noise when the air went out. It was the way it was made. He hadn’t made it. He set off to school with an air of injured innocence﻿—and the balloon. Observing an elderly and irascible-looking gentleman in front of him, he went a few steps down a back street, blew up his balloon and held it tightly under his coat. Then, when abreast of the old gentleman, he let it off. The old gentleman gave a leap into the air and glared fiercely around. He glanced at the small virtuous-looking schoolboy with obviously no instrument of torture at his lips, and then concentrated his glare of fury and suspicion on the upper windows. William hastened on to the next pedestrian. He had quite a happy walk to school.

===Chinese===

威廉觉得生活整个儿都对不起他。他正处在一段不时会光顾他的“不得人心期”里。高潮是一位胆小怕事的姨妈送了他一枚六便士，指望借此收买他的好感。他用这六便士买了一只气球，气球上装着纸板做的鸭头和鸭脚。把它吹到最鼓，再放着让它慢慢瘪下去，得花好几分钟才瘪得完，而这几分钟里它会发出一种拖长的高音呻吟。这么做的好处是显而易见的。威廉可以背地里把它吹到最鼓，再藏在外套底下，当众让它瘪下去。瘪的时候，威廉就四处张望，装出一副又茫然又吃惊的样子。早饭前他把它吹鼓了。随后他紧紧地、偷偷地按住它，让它一直鼓着，直到自己在餐桌前坐定。然后他放手让它瘪。他妈妈碰翻了一杯咖啡，他爸爸被面包刀割了手，他姐姐埃塞尔则轻度精神崩溃了一回。威廉坐在那里，一脸惊愕的无辜。可没有什么比威廉的无辜表情更能激怒他的家人了。他们朝他扑过去，他竭尽所能地为自己辩护。是的，气球是他在桌子底下按着。呃，他早就把它吹鼓了。他总不能让它永远鼓着吧。总得有个时候把气放出去。气出去的时候它要响，他有什么法子。它生来就是这样的。又不是他造的。他带着一副受了委屈的无辜神情——外加那只气球——动身去上学。他瞧见前面有位上了年纪、样子火爆的老先生，便折进一条后街走了几步，把气球吹鼓，紧紧捂在外套底下。然后，走到与老先生并排时，他放了手。老先生腾空一跳，凶巴巴地四下张望。他看了看那个一脸乖相、嘴边明明没有任何刑具的小学生，便把满腔怒火和疑心都集中到了楼上的窗户上。威廉赶紧奔向下一位行人。他一路走到学校，快活得很。

===Original===

School was at first equally successful. William opened his desk, hastily inflated his balloon, closed his desk, then gazed round with his practised expression of horrified astonishment at what followed. He drove the French master to distraction.

“Step out ’oo makes the noise,” he screamed.

No one stepped out, and the noise continued at intervals.

The mathematics master finally discovered and confiscated the balloon.

===Chinese===

学校里一开始也同样顺利。威廉打开课桌，匆匆把气球吹鼓，关上课桌，然后用他练熟了的、对接下来那阵响声又惊又骇的表情环顾四周。他把法语老师折腾得快要发疯。

“谁在弄那个响声，给我站出来！”他尖声叫道。

没有人站出来，那响声还断断续续地响个不停。

数学老师最终发现了气球，把它没收了。

===Original===

“I hope,” said the father at lunch, “that they’ve taken away that infernal machine of yours.”

William replied sadly that they had. He added that some people didn’t seem to think it was stealing to take other people’s things.

“Then we may look forward to a little peace this evening?” said the father politely. “Not that it matters to me, as I’m going out to dinner. The only thing that relieves the tedium of going out to dinner is the fact that for a short time one has a rest from William.”

William acknowledged the compliment by a scowl and a mysterious muttered remark to the effect that some people were always at him.

===Chinese===

“我希望，”吃午饭时父亲说，“他们已经把你那台恶魔机器给收走了。”

威廉伤心地回答说收走了。他还补了一句：有些人拿了别人的东西，好像压根儿不觉得这叫偷。

“那么今晚咱们可以指望清静一会儿了？”父亲客气地说。“倒不关我的事，反正我要出去吃晚饭。出去吃晚饭这件事唯一的可取之处，就是有那么一小会儿，可以暂时不见威廉。”

威廉用一记怒目和一句神秘兮兮的嘀咕领受了这份恭维，大意是说有些人老跟他过不去。

===Original===

During preparation in afternoon school he read a storybook kindly lent him by his next-door neighbour. It was not because he had no work to do that William read a storybook in preparation. It was a mark of defiance to the world in general. It was also a very interesting storybook. It opened with the hero as a small boy misunderstood and ill-treated by everyone around him. Then he ran away. He went to sea, and in a few years made an immense fortune in the goldfields. He returned in the last chapter and forgave his family and presented them with a noble mansion and several shiploads of gold. The idea impressed William﻿—all except the end part. He thought he’d prefer to have the noble mansion himself and pay rare visits to his family, during which he would listen to their humble apologies, and perhaps give them a nugget or two, but not very much﻿—certainly not much to Ethel. He wasn’t sure whether he’d ever really forgive them. He’d have rooms full of squeaky balloons and trumpets in his house anyway, and he’d keep caterpillars and white rats all over the place too﻿—things they made such a fuss about in their old house﻿—and he’d always go about in dirty boots, and he’d never brush his hair or wash, and he’d keep dozens of motorcars, and he wouldn’t let Ethel go out in any of them. He was roused from this enthralling daydream by the discovery and confiscation of his storybook by the master in charge, and the subsequent fury of its owner. In order adequately to express his annoyance, he dropped a little ball of blotting-paper soaked in ink down William’s back. William, on attempting retaliation, was sentenced to stay in half an hour after school. He returned gloomily to his history book (upside down) and his misanthropic view of life. He compared himself bitterly with the hero of the storybook and decided not to waste another moment of his life in uncongenial surroundings. He made a firm determination to run away as soon as he was released from school.

===Chinese===

下午自习课上，他读起了隔壁邻居好心借给他的一本故事书。威廉在自习课上读故事书，倒不是因为他没有功课可做。这是他对整个世界的一种挑衅姿态。再说，那也确实是本非常好看的故事书。开场是主人公小时候受尽周围所有人的误解和虐待。后来他离家出走了。他上了海船，不出几年就在金矿区发了大财。最后一章里他回来了，原谅了全家人，还送给他们一座堂皇的宅邸和好几船金子。这个故事打动了威廉——除了结尾那部分。他想他更愿意自己留着那座堂皇的宅邸，偶尔回家看看家里人，听听他们低声下气的道歉，兴许赏他们一两块金块，但不多——给埃塞尔的肯定不多。他拿不准自己到底会不会真原谅他们。反正他家里要摆满会吱吱叫的气球和喇叭，还要满屋子养毛毛虫和白老鼠——都是他们在老房子里大惊小怪的东西——他要成天穿着脏靴子到处走，永远不梳头不洗脸，他要养上几十辆汽车，一辆也不让埃塞尔坐。他正沉浸在这迷人的白日梦里，故事书被督课的老师发现并没收了，书主人随后大发雷霆，把他从梦里惊醒。为了充分表达自己的恼火，书主人把一个浸透了墨水的吸墨纸团顺着威廉的后背塞了下去。威廉试图报复，结果被判放学后留校半小时。他闷闷地回到他的历史书（拿倒了）和他那套愤世嫉俗的人生观上。他把自己跟故事书里的主人公苦苦比较了一番，决定在这格格不入的环境里再浪费一分钟人生也不值得。他下定决心，一放学就离家出走。

===Original===

---

He walked briskly down the road away from the village. In his pocket reposed the balloon. He had made the cheering discovery that the mathematics master had left it on his desk, so he had joyfully taken it again into his possession. He thought he might reach the coast before night, and get to the goldfields before next week. He didn’t suppose it took long to make a fortune there. He might be back before next Christmas and﻿—crumbs! he’d jolly well make people sit up. He wouldn’t go to school, for one thing, and he’d be jolly careful who he gave nuggets to for another. He’d give nuggets to the butcher’s boy and the postman, and the man who came to tune the piano, and the chimney-sweep. He wouldn’t give any to any of his family, or any of the masters at the school. He’d just serve people out the way they served him. He just would. The road to the coast seemed rather long, and he was growing rather tired. He walked in a ditch for a change, and then scraped through a hedge and took a shortcut across a ploughed field. Dusk was falling fast, and even William’s buoyant spirits began to flag. The fortune part was all very well, but in the meantime he was cold and tired and hungry. He hadn’t yet reached the coast, much less the goldfields. Something must be done. He remembered that the boy in the story had “begged his way” to the coast. William determined to beg his. But at present there seemed nothing to beg it from, except a hawthorn hedge and a scarecrow in the field behind it. He wandered on disconsolately deciding to begin his career as a beggar at the first sign of human habitation.

===Chinese===

---

他脚步轻快地沿着大路走出了村子。口袋里躺着那只气球。他有一个令人振奋的发现：数学老师把气球落在了讲台上，于是他欢天喜地地重新据为己有。他想着天黑前也许能走到海边，下星期之前就能到金矿区。他并不觉得在那儿发财要花多久。说不定明年圣诞节前就能回来，到那时候——乖乖！他保管让大伙儿大吃一惊。首先，他再也不上学了；其次，金块给谁他也要好好挑挑。他要给肉铺的小伙计、邮递员、来调钢琴的师傅和扫烟囱的工人发金块。家里的人一个也不给，学校里的老师一个也不给。他就要照别人待他的样子回敬别人。他就是要这样。去海边的路显得相当长，他也越来越累了。他换换花样，在沟里走了一段，又钻过一道树篱，抄近路横穿了一片犁过的田。暮色很快合拢下来，连威廉那么旺盛的精气神也开始蔫了。发财什么的固然好，可眼下他又冷又累又饿。他还没走到海边呢，金矿区就更别提了。总得想个办法。他记起故事书里那个男孩是一路“讨饭”讨到海边的。威廉决定也一路讨过去。可眼下一眼望去没什么可讨的，只有一道山楂树篱和树篱后面田里的一个稻草人。他垂头丧气地继续往前走，决定一见到人烟就开始他的乞丐生涯。

===Original===

At last he discovered a pair of iron gates through the dusk and, assuming an expression of patient suffering calculated to melt a heart of stone, walked up the drive. At the front door he smoothed down his hair (he had lost his cap on the way), pulled up his stockings, and rang the bell. After an interval a stout gentleman in the garb of a butler opened the door and glared ferociously up and down William.

“Please﻿—” began William plaintively.

The stout gentleman interrupted.

“If you’re the new Boots,” he said majestically, “go round to the back door. If you’re not, go away.”

He then shut the door in William’s face. William, on the top step, considered the question for a few minutes. It was dark and cold, with every prospect of becoming darker and colder. He decided to be the new Boots. He found his way round to the back door and knocked firmly. It was opened by a large woman in a print dress and apron.

===Chinese===

终于，他在暮色里发现了一对铁门，便摆出一副足以熔化铁石心肠的、含辛忍苦的表情，走上车道。到了正门前，他抹平头发（帽子半路丢了），拉直长袜，按响了门铃。过了一会儿，一位身穿管家制服的胖绅士开了门，恶狠狠地把威廉从头到脚打量了一番。

“劳驾——”威廉可怜巴巴地开口。

胖绅士打断了他。

“你要是来上工的擦靴工，”他威严地说，“就绕到后门去。要不是，就走开。”

说罢他就当着威廉的面把门关上了。威廉站在台阶顶上，把这个问题掂量了几分钟。天又黑又冷，而且眼看着还要更黑、更冷。他决定当那个新来的擦靴工。他摸到后门，笃笃地敲了门。开门的是个穿印花布裙子、系围裙的高大女人。

===Original===

“What y’ want?” she said aggressively.

“He said,” said William firmly, “to come round if I was the new Boots.”

The woman surveyed him in grim disapproval.

“You bin round to the front?” she said. “Nerve!”

Her disapproval increased to suspicion.

===Chinese===

“你要干吗？”她气势汹汹地说。

“他说的，”威廉斩钉截铁，“我要是新来的擦靴工，就绕到后面来。”

那女人沉着脸、一脸不赞成地把他打量了一遍。

“你到前门去过了？”她说，“好大的胆子！”

她的不赞成升级成了怀疑。

===Original===

“Where’s your things?” she said.

“Comin’,” said William without a moment’s hesitation.

“Too tired to bring ’em with you?” she said sarcastically. “All right. Come in!”

William came in gratefully. It was a large, warm, clean kitchen. A small kitchen-maid was peeling potatoes at a sink, and a housemaid in black, with a frilled cap and apron, was powdering her nose before a glass on the wall. They both turned to stare at William.

“ ’Ere’s the new Boots,” announced Cook, “ ’is valet’s bringin’ ’is things later.”

===Chinese===

“你的行李呢？”她说。

“随后到。”威廉毫不迟疑地说。

“累得连行李都带不动了？”她挖苦道，“行吧。进来！”

威廉感激地进了门。这是一间宽敞、暖和、干净的厨房。一个小厨娘正在水池边削土豆，一个穿黑衣服、戴褶边帽系褶边围裙的女仆正对着墙上的一面镜子扑粉。两人都转过身来盯着威廉瞧。

“新来的擦靴工到，”厨娘宣布，“他的贴身用人回头把他的行李送来。”

===Original===

The housemaid looked up William from his muddy boots to his untidy hair, then down William from his untidy hair to his muddy boots.

“Imperdent-lookin’ child,” she commented haughtily, returning to her task.

William decided inwardly that she was to have no share at all in the nuggets.

The kitchen-maid giggled and winked at William, with obviously friendly intent. William mentally promised her half a shipload of nuggets.

“Now, then, Smutty,” said the housemaid without turning round, “none of your sauce!”

===Chinese===

女仆把威廉从他的泥靴子打量到他的乱头发，又把威廉从他的乱头发打量到他的泥靴子。

“一副没规矩的样子。”她傲慢地评了一句，回头接着干她的事。

威廉在心里认定，金块一块也不分给她。

小厨娘咯咯笑着朝威廉挤了挤眼，眼见得是一片好意。威廉在心里许了她半船金块。

“我说，小黑炭，”女仆头也不回地说，“少跟我贫嘴！”

===Original===

“ ’Ad your tea?” said the cook to William. William’s spirits rose.

“No,” he said plaintively.

“All right. Sit down at the table.”

William’s spirits soared sky high.

He sat at the table and the cook put a large plate of bread and butter before him.

===Chinese===

“吃过茶点了吗？”厨娘问威廉。威廉的精神为之一振。

“没有。”他可怜巴巴地说。

“行。坐到桌边去吧。”

威廉的精神直冲云霄。

他在桌边坐下，厨娘在他面前摆了一大盘黄油面包。

===Original===

William set to work at once. The housemaid regarded him scornfully.

“Learnt ’is way of eatin’ at the Zoo,” she said pityingly.

The kitchen-maid giggled again and gave William another wink. William had given himself up to wholehearted epicurean enjoying of his bread and butter and took no notice of them. At this moment the butler entered.

He subjected the quite unmoved William to another long survey.

“When next you come a-hentering of this ’ouse, my boy,” he said, “kindly remember that the front door is reserved for gentry an’ the back for brats.”

===Chinese===

威廉立刻埋头大干起来。女仆轻蔑地看着他。

“他那吃相是在动物园学的。”她怜悯地说。

小厨娘又咯咯笑起来，又朝威廉挤了挤眼。威廉正全心全意、津津有味地享用他的黄油面包，没搭理她们。这时管家进来了。

他把毫不动摇的威廉又上上下下打量了半天。

“下回你再上这宅子来串门，小伙子，”他说，“请务必记住，前门是留给上等人走的，后门是留给小崽子走的。”

===Original===

William merely looked at him coldly over a hunk of bread and butter. Mentally he knocked him off the list of nugget-receivers.

The butler looked sadly round the room.

“They’re all the same,” he lamented. “Eat, eat, eat. Nothin’ but eat. Eat all day an’ eat all night. ’E’s not bin in the ’ouse two minutes an’ ’e’s at it. Eat! eat! eat! ’E’ll ’ave all the buttons bust off his uniform in a week like wot the larst one ’ad. Like eatin’ better than workin’, don’t you?” he said sarcastically to William.

“Yes, I do, too,” said William with firm conviction.

The kitchen-maid giggled again, and the housemaid gave a sigh expressive of scorn and weariness as she drew a thin pencil over her eyebrows.

===Chinese===

威廉只冷冷地隔着一大块黄油面包看了他一眼。心里已经把他从金块受赠名单上勾掉了。

管家悲哀地环视了一圈厨房。

“全都是一个样，”他哀叹，“吃，吃，吃。除了吃没别的。白天吃，黑夜吃。他进这宅子还不到两分钟，就吃上了。吃！吃！吃！不出一个星期，他制服上的扣子就得全绷掉，跟上一个一样。好吃懒做，是不是？”他冲着威廉讥讽地说。

“对，我就是好吃懒做。”威廉说得斩钉截铁。

小厨娘又咯咯笑起来，女仆一面用细铅笔描眉，一面发出一声兼表轻蔑与厌倦的叹息。

===Original===

“Well, if you’ve quite finished, my lord,” said the butler in ponderous irony, “I’ll show you to your room.”

William indicated that he had quite finished, and was led up to a very small bedroom. Over a chair lay a page’s uniform with the conventional row of brass buttons down the front of the coat.

“Togs,” explained the butler briefly. “Your togs. Fix ’em on quick as you can. There’s company to dinner tonight.”

William fixed them on.

“You’re smaller than wot the last one was,” said the butler critically. “They ’ang a bit loose. Never mind. With a week or two of stuffin’ you’ll ’ave most probable bust ’em, so it’s as well to ’ang loose first. Now, come on. ’Oo’s bringing over your things?”

===Chinese===

“那么，您用完了吗，我的大人？”管家用沉甸甸的讽刺口吻说，“我带您去您的房间。”

威廉表示自己用完了，随后被领上楼，进了一间很小的卧室。椅子上搭着一套侍童制服，上衣前襟钉着老规矩的一排铜扣。

“行头，”管家简短地解释，“你的行头。赶紧穿上。今晚府上有客人来吃饭。”

威廉把行头穿上了。

“你比上一个矮小，”管家挑剔地说，“衣服挂着有点松。不碍事。塞上他一两个礼拜，保不齐你就把它们绷开了，所以先松点穿着也好。走，现在动身。谁给你送行李过来？”

===Original===

“E﻿—a friend,” explained William.

“I suppose it *is* a bit too much to expeck you to carry your own parcels,” went on the butler, “in these ’ere days. Bloomin’ Bolshevist, I speck, aren’t you?”

William condescended to explain himself.

“I’m a gold-digger,” he said.

“Criky!” said the butler.

===Chinese===

“呃——一位朋友。”威廉解释。

“我看如今这年头，指望你自己拎行李，确实是有点过分，”管家接着说，“要我说，你就是个不折不扣的布尔什维克，对不对？”

威廉屈尊解释了自己的身份。

“我是个淘金客。”他说。

“了不得！”管家说。

===Original===

William was led down again to the kitchen.

The butler threw open a door that led to a small pantry.

“This ’ere is where you work, and this ’ere,” pointing to a large kitchen, “is where you live. You ’ave not,” he ended haughtily “the hentry into the servants’ ’all.”

“Crumbs!” said William.

“You might has well begin at once,” went on the butler, “there’s all this lunch’s knives to clean. ’Ere’s a hapron, ’ere’s the knife-board an’ ’ere’s the knife-powder.”

===Chinese===

威廉又被领下楼回到厨房。

管家推开一扇通向小配膳室的门。

“这儿是你干活的地方，这儿，”他指着大厨房，“是你住的地方。你们这种身份的，”他傲然说道，“是无权踏进仆役餐厅的。”

“乖乖！”威廉说。

“你不如这就动手，”管家接着说，“午饭的刀全在这儿等着擦呢。这是围裙，这是刀板，这是刀粉。”

===Original===

He shut the bewildered William into the small pantry and turned to the cook.

“What do you think of ’im?” he said.

“ ’E looks,” said the cook gloomily, “the sort of boy we’ll ’ave trouble with.”

“Not much clarse,” said the housemaid, arranging her frilled apron. “It surprises me ’ow any creature like a boy can grow into an experienced, sensible, broad-minded man like you, Mr. Biggs.”

Mr. Biggs simpered and straightened his necktie.

===Chinese===

他把一脸茫然的威廉关进小配膳室，转身对厨娘说：

“你觉得他怎么样？”

“他看着，”厨娘愁眉苦脸地说，“就是那种咱们要跟着遭殃的孩子。”

“没什么档次，”女仆一面整理褶边围裙一面说，“真叫我奇怪，男孩这样的生物，怎么就能长成您这样一位有阅历、有见识、心胸开阔的男人，比格斯先生。”

比格斯先生得意地一抿嘴，扶正了领结。

===Original===

“Well,” he admitted, “as a boy, of course, I wasn’t like ’im.”

Here the pantry-door opened and William’s face, plentifully adorned with knife-powder came round.

“I’ve done some of the knives,” he said, “shall I be doin’ something else and finish the others afterwards?”

“ ’Ow many ’ave you done?” said Mr. Biggs.

“One or two,” said William vaguely, then with a concession to accuracy, “well, two. But I’m feeling tired of doin’ knives.”

===Chinese===

“呃，”他承认，“小时候，我当然跟他不一样。”

这时配膳室的门开了，威廉那张糊满刀粉的脸探了出来。

“几把刀我已经擦完了，”他说，“能不能先干点别的，回头再把剩下的擦完？”

“擦了几把了？”比格斯先生说。

“一两把，”威廉含含糊糊地说，随即又照顾了一下准确性，“呃，两把。不过我擦刀擦腻了。”

===Original===

The kitchen-maid emitted a scream of delight and the cook heaved a deep sigh.

The butler advanced slowly and majestically towards William’s tousled head, which was still craned around the pantry door.

“You’ll finish them knives, my boy,” he said, “or﻿—”

William considered the weight and size of Mr. Biggs.

“All right,” he said pacifically. “I’ll finish the knives.”

===Chinese===

小厨娘发出一声欢喜的尖叫，厨娘重重地叹了口气。

管家迈着缓慢而威严的步子，朝还从配膳室门后探出的威廉那颗乱蓬蓬的脑袋逼近。

“那几把刀你是要擦完的，小伙子，”他说，“不然——”

威廉掂量了一下比格斯先生的分量和块头。

“行，”他息事宁人地说，“我把刀擦完就是了。”

===Original===

He disappeared, closing the pantry door behind him.

“ ’E’s goin’ to be a trile,” said the cook, “an’ no mistake.”

“Trile’s ’ardly the word,” said Mr. Biggs.

“Haffliction,” supplied the housemaid.

“That’s more like it,” said Mr. Biggs.

===Chinese===

他缩了回去，随手关上了配膳室的门。

“他准是个祸害，”厨娘说，“错不了。”

“祸害还嫌轻了，”比格斯先生说。

“简直是天罚。”女仆补充。

“这还差不多。”比格斯先生说。

===Original===

Here William’s head appeared again.

“Wot time’s supper?” he said.

He retired precipitately at a hysterical shriek from the kitchen-maid and a roar of fury from the butler.

“You’d better go an’ do your potatoes in the pantry,” said the cook to the kitchenmaid, “and let’s ’ave a bit of peace in ’ere and see ’e’s doin’ of ’is work all right.”

The kitchenmaid departed joyfully to the pantry.

===Chinese===

威廉的脑袋又冒了出来。

“晚饭几点开？”他说。

小厨娘一声歇斯底里的尖叫、管家一声狂怒的咆哮，吓得他一溜烟缩了回去。

“你还是上配膳室削你的土豆去吧，”厨娘对小厨娘说，“让这儿清静一会儿，也看着点儿他，别叫他偷懒。”

小厨娘欢天喜地到配膳室去了。

===Original===

William was sitting by the table, idly toying with a knife. He had experimented upon the knife powder by mixing it with water, and the little brown pies that were the result lay in a row on the mantelpiece. He had also tasted it, as the dark stains upon his lips testified. His hair was standing straight up on his head as it always did when life was strenuous. He began the conversation.

“You’d be surprised,” he said, “if you knew what I really was.”

===Chinese===

威廉正坐在桌边，闲闲地摆弄着一把刀。他已经拿刀粉做过实验——和水混在一起，成果是几个棕色的小馅饼，一字排开搁在壁炉架上。他还尝过味道，嘴唇上的黑印子就是证据。他的头发笔直地竖在头顶上，每逢日子过得轰轰烈烈，他的头发总是这样。他先开的口。

“你要是知道我的真实身份，”他说，“准会大吃一惊。”

===Original===

She giggled.

“Go on!” she said. “What are you?”

“I’m a gold-digger,” he said. “I’ve got shiploads an’ shiploads of gold. At least, I will have soon. I’m not goin’ to give *him*,” pointing towards the door, “any, nor any of them in there.”

“Wot about me?” said the kitchenmaid, winking at the cat as the only third person to be let into the joke.

“You,” said William graciously, “shall have a whole lot of nuggets. Look here.” With a princely flourish he took up a knife and cut off three buttons from the middle of his coat and gave them to her. “You keep those and they’ll be kind of tokens. See? When I come home rich you show me the buttons an’ I’ll remember and give you the nuggets. See? I’ll maybe marry you,” he promised, “if I’ve not married anyone else.”

===Chinese===

她咯咯直笑。

“快说！”她说，“你是干什么的？”

“我是个淘金客，”他说，“我有满满几船几船的金子。起码，很快就有了。我一块也不打算给*他*，”他朝门那边指了指，“那里面的人也一块别想。”

“那我呢？”小厨娘说，一边朝猫挤了挤眼——那是唯一可以分享这个玩笑的第三者。

“你，”威廉雍容大度地说，“可以得到一大堆金块。瞧这儿。”他帝王气派地一挥手，拿起一把刀，从外衣前襟正中割下三颗扣子，递给了她。“你收好这些，就当是信物。明白吗？等我发财回来，你拿出扣子给我看，我就想起来，把金块给你。明白吗？兴许我还会娶你，”他许诺道，“要是那之前没娶别人的话。”

===Original===

The kitchenmaid put her head round the pantry door.

“ ’E’s loony,” she said. “It’s lovely listening to ’im talkin’.”

Further conversation was prevented by the ringing of the front-door bell and the arrival of the “company.”

Mr. Biggs and the housemaid departed to do the honours. The kitchenmaid ran to help with the dishing up, and William was left sitting on the pantry table, idly making patterns in knife powder with his finger.

===Chinese===

小厨娘把头探出配膳室的门。

“他是个小疯子，”她说，“听他讲话真有意思。”

进一步的谈话被前门门铃的响声和“客人”的到来打断了。

比格斯先生和女仆出去履行迎宾之责。小厨娘跑去帮忙上菜，威廉独自留在配膳室，坐在桌上，闲闲地用手指在刀粉里画花纹。

===Original===

“Wot was ’e doin’?” said the cook to the kitchenmaid.

“Nothin’﻿—’cept talkin’,” said the kitchenmaid. “ ’E’s a cure, *’e* is,” she added.

“If you’ve finished the knives,” called out the cook, “there’s some boots and shoes on the floor to be done. Brushes an’ blacking on the shelf.”

William arose with alacrity. He thought boots would be more interesting than knives. He carefully concealed the pile of uncleaned knives behind the knife-box and began on the shoes.

===Chinese===

“他在干什么呢？”厨娘问小厨娘。

“没干什么——光说话，”小厨娘说，“他可是个*活宝*。”

“你要是刀擦完了，”厨娘喊道，“地上还有几双靴子鞋要擦。刷子和鞋油在架子上。”

威廉一跃而起。他觉得靴子应该比刀有意思。他把没擦的那堆刀仔细藏在刀箱后面，动起了鞋的念头。

===Original===

The butler returned.

“Soup ready?” he said. “The company’s just goin’ into the dining-room﻿—a pal of the master’s. Decent-lookin’ bloke,” he added patronisingly.

William, in his pantry, had covered a brush very thickly with blacking, and was putting it in heavy layers on the boots and shoes. A large part of it adhered to his own hands. The butler looked in at him.

“Wot’s ’appened to your buttons?” he said sternly.

“Come off,” said William.

===Chinese===

管家回来了。

“汤好了吗？”他说，“客人这就进餐厅了——是老爷的一位朋友。看着是个体面人。”他屈尊补了一句。

威廉在他的配膳室里，往一把刷子上厚厚地蘸满了鞋油，正把它一层层往靴子和鞋上厚厚地涂。一大半都沾到了他自己手上。管家探进头来。

“你的扣子怎么回事？”他严厉地说。

“掉了。”威廉说。

===Original===

“Bust off,” corrected the butler. “I said so soon as I saw you. I said you’d ’ave eat your buttons bust off in a week. Well, you’ve eat ’em bust off in ten minutes.”

“Eatin’ an’ destroyin’ of ’is clothes,” he said gloomily, returning to the kitchen. “It’s all boys ever do﻿—eatin’ an’ destroyin’ of their clothes.”

He went out with the soup and William was left with the boots. He was getting tired of boots. He’d covered them all thickly with blacking, and he didn’t know what to do next. Then suddenly he remembered his balloon in his pocket upstairs. It might serve to vary the monotony of life. He slipped quietly upstairs for it, and then returned to his boots.

Soon Mr. Biggs and the housemaid returned with the empty soup-plates. Then through the kitchen resounded a high-pitched squeal, dying away slowly and shrilly.

The housemaid screamed.

===Chinese===

“绷掉的，”管家纠正，“我一见你就说了。我说过不出一星期，你准能把扣子吃绷掉。好啊，你十分钟就吃绷掉了。”

“就知道吃，就知道糟蹋衣裳，”他灰溜溜地回厨房去，一路念叨，“男孩们干的就是这个——吃，糟蹋衣裳。”

他端着汤出去了，剩下威廉和靴子在一起。他对靴子也渐渐腻了。他已经把它们全都厚厚涂满了鞋油，接下来不知道该干什么。忽然，他想起楼上口袋里的气球。它或许可以给生活换换花样。他悄悄溜上楼去取，然后回到靴子跟前。

不多时，比格斯先生和女仆端着空汤盘回来了。紧接着，一阵尖利的高啸响彻厨房，又尖又长，慢慢消逝。

女仆尖叫起来。

===Original===

“Lawks!” said the cook, “someone’s atorchurin’ of the poor cat to death. It’ll be that blessed boy.”

The butler advanced manfully and opened the pantry door. William stood holding in one hand an inflated balloon with the cardboard head and legs of a duck.

The butler approached him.

“If you let off that there thing once more, you little varmint,” he said, “I’ll﻿—”

Threateningly he had advanced his large expanse of countenance very close to William’s. Acting upon a sudden uncontrollable impulse William took up the brush thickly smeared with blacking and pushed back Mr. Biggs’s face with it.

===Chinese===

“老天爷！”厨娘说，“准是谁在折磨那只可怜的猫，要把它弄死了。八成就是那个小祖宗干的好事。”

管家挺身而出，打开了配膳室的门。威廉站在里面，一手按着一只吹鼓的、带着纸板鸭头鸭脚的气球。

管家朝他逼近。

“你要是再放一回那玩意儿，你个小坏种，”他说，“我就——”

他把那张大脸威胁性地凑到了威廉鼻子跟前。威廉在一阵无法遏制的冲动之下，抄起那把蘸满鞋油的刷子，一下抹在了比格斯先生的脸上，把他推了回去。

===Original===

There was a moment’s silence of sheer horror, then Mr. Biggs hurled himself furiously upon William.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

有那么一瞬间，是纯粹的、透骨的恐怖的寂静。随后，比格斯先生狂怒地向威廉猛扑过去。……

===Original===

---

In the dining-room sat the master and mistress of the house and their guest.

“Did the new Boots arrive?” said the master to his wife.

“Yes,” she said.

“Any good?” he said.

“He doesn’t seem to have impressed Biggs very favourably,” she said, “but they never do.”

===Chinese===

---

餐厅里坐着这宅子的老爷、太太和他们的客人。

“新来的擦靴工到了吗？”老爷问太太。

“到了。”她说。

“还凑合吗？”他说。

“看样子没给比格斯留下什么好印象，”她说，“不过他们从来也不会。”

===Original===

“The human boy,” said the guest, “is given us as a discipline. I possess one. Though he is my own son, I find it difficult to describe the atmosphere of peace and relief that pervades the house when he is out of it.”

“I’d like to meet your son,” said the host.

“You probably will, sooner or later,” said the guest gloomily. “Everyone in the neighbourhood meets him sooner or later. He does not hide his light under a bushel. Personally, I prefer people who haven’t met him. They can’t judge me by him.”

At this moment the butler came in with a note.

“No answer,” he said, and departed with his slow dignity.

===Chinese===

“人类中的男孩，”客人说，“是上天派来磨炼我们的。我就有一个。虽说他是我亲生的儿子，可连我也很难描摹，他不在家的时候，这宅子里弥漫的那种和平与解脱的气氛。”

“我倒想见见令郎。”男主人说。

“迟早会见着的，”客人阴郁地说，“这一带的人迟早都会见着他。他这个人是不肯把才干藏着掖着的。就我个人而言，我更喜欢那些还没见过他的人——他们起码没法拿他来评判我。”

这时管家拿着一张字条进来了。

“不必回话。”他说，随即带着他缓慢的威严退了出去。

===Original===

“Excuse me,” said the lady as she opened it, “it’s from my sister. ‘I hope,’ she read, ‘that you aren’t inconvenienced much by the non-arrival of the Boots I engaged for you. He’s got “flu.” ’ But he’s come,” she said wonderingly.

There came the sound of an angry shout, a distant scream and the clattering of heavy running footsteps﻿ ﻿… growing nearer.﻿ ﻿…

“A revolution, I expect,” said the guest wearily. “The Reds are upon us.”

===Chinese===

“失陪一下，”太太一面拆字条一面说，“是我妹妹写来的。‘但愿那个没到差的擦靴工，’她念道，‘没给你们添太多麻烦。他得了“流感”。’可他这不是来了吗。”她纳闷地说。

传来一声怒吼、一声远远的尖叫和沉重的奔跑脚步声……越来越近。……

“要我说，是闹革命了，”客人疲惫地说，“赤党打过来了。”

===Original===

At that moment the door was burst open and in rushed a boy with a blacking brush in one hand and an inflated balloon in the other. He was much dishevelled, with three buttons off the front of his uniform, and his face streaked with knife powder and blacking. Behind him ran a fat butler, his face purple with fury beneath a large smear of blacking. The boy rushed round the table, slipped on the polished floor, clutched desperately at the neck of the guest, bringing both guest and chair down upon the floor beside him. In a sudden silence of utter paralysed horror, guest and boy sat on the floor and stared at each other. Then the boy’s nerveless hand relaxed its hold upon the balloon, which had somehow or other survived the vicissitudes of the flight, and a shrill squeak rang through the silence of the room.

===Chinese===
