“You can have lots for that,” he said huskily. “They’ve gone cheap. They’ve gone ever so cheap. You can take all the boxful for that,” he went on recklessly. He pressed the box into her reluctant hands. “An’﻿—what else would you like? You jus’ tell me that. Tell me what else you’d like?”

“Please, I haven’t any more money,” gasped a small, bewildered voice.

“*Money* don’t matter,” said William. “Things is cheap today. Things is awful cheap today. *Awful* cheap! You can have﻿—anythin’ you like for that fourpence. Anythin’ you like.”

“ ’Cause it’s New Year’s Day?” said the vision, with a gleam of understanding.

===Chinese===
「这些钱能买好多呢。」他嗓音沙哑地说，「它们降价了。降得可厉害了。这些钱能把一整盒都拿走。」他不管不顾地往下说，把盒子硬塞进她那不太情愿的手里。「还——还想要点啥？你就跟我说。跟我说你还想要啥？」

「劳驾，我没有更多的钱了。」一个小小的、发懵的声音喘着气说。

「*钱*不算事儿，」威廉说，「今天东西便宜。今天东西便宜得吓人。*吓人*地便宜！这四便士——你随便挑啥都行。随便啥。」

「因为今天是元旦？」小天仙说，眼里闪过一丝了然。

===Original===
“Yes,” said William, “ ’cause it’s that.”

“Is it your shop?”

“Yes,” said William with an air of importance. “It’s all my shop.”

She gazed at him in admiration and envy.

===Chinese===
「对，」威廉说，「就因为这个。」

「这是你的店吗？」

「对，」威廉一脸郑重其事，「整个店都是我的。」

她又羡慕又妒忌地凝视着他。

===Original===
“I’d love to have a sweet-shop,” she said wistfully.

“Well, you take anythin’ you like,” said William generously.

She collected as much as she could carry and started towards the door. “*Sank* you! Sank you ever so!” she said gratefully.

William stood leaning against the door in the easy attitude of the good-natured, all-providing male.

===Chinese===
「我要是有家糖果店该多好。」她向往地说。

「那你就随便拿啥都行。」威廉慷慨地说。

她把拿得动的都收拢齐，朝门口走去。「*些些*你！些些你太多了！」她感激地说。

威廉倚门而立，摆出一副好脾气、有求必应的大男人的松弛姿态。

===Original===
“It’s all right,” he said with an indulgent smile. “Quite all right. Quite all right.” Then, with an inspiration born of memories of his father earlier in the day. “Not at all. Don’t menshun it. Not at all. Quite all right.”

He stopped, simply for lack of further expressions, and bowed with would-be gracefulness as she went through the doorway.

As she passed the window she was rewarded by a spreading effusive smile in a flushed face.

She stopped and kissed her hand.

===Chinese===
「不客气，」他带着宽容的微笑说，「完全不用。完全不用。」接着，凭借当天早上对父亲的记忆来了灵感：「哪儿的话。甭提啦。哪儿的话。完全不用。」

他停下来纯粹是因为词穷了，在她跨出门槛时煞有介事地鞠了一躬。

她走过橱窗时，收获了一张红扑扑的脸上绽开的、热情四溢的笑容。

她停下脚步，吻了吻自己的手。

===Original===
William blinked with pure emotion.

He continued his smile long after its recipient had disappeared. Then absentmindedly he crammed his mouth with a handful of Mixed Dew Drops and sat down behind the counter.

As he crunched Mixed Dew Drops he indulged in a day dream in which he rescued the little girl in the white fur coat from robbers and pirates and a burning house. He was just leaping nimbly from the roof of the burning house, holding the little girl in the white fur coat in his arms, when he caught sight of two of his friends flattening their noses at the window. He rose from his seat and went to the door.

“ ’Ullo, Ginger! ’Ullo, Henry!” he said with an unsuccessful effort to appear void of self-consciousness.

===Chinese===
威廉激动得直眨眼。

笑容的接受者早已消失，他还继续笑了好久。然后他心不在焉地往嘴里塞了一大把「什锦露珠糖」，在柜台后面坐下。

他一边嘎嘣嘎嘣嚼着什锦露珠糖，一边沉溺在白日梦里：他从强盗、海盗和一栋着火的房子手里，救出了那个穿白毛皮大衣的小女孩。他正抱着穿白毛皮大衣的小女孩，从着火房子的屋顶上灵巧地一跃而下，忽然瞥见他的两个朋友正把鼻子压扁在橱窗上。他从座位上起身，走向门口。

「你好啊，金杰！你好啊，亨利！」他说，努力想显得毫不心虚，却没成功。

===Original===
They gazed at him in wonder.

“I’ve gotta shop,” he went on casually. “Come on in an’ look at it.”

They peeped round the doorway cautiously and, reassured by the sight of William obviously in sole possession, they entered, openmouthed. They gazed at the boxes and bottles of sweets. Aladdin’s Cave was nothing to this.

“Howd’ you get it, William?” gasped Ginger.

===Chinese===
他们惊异地打量着他。

「我弄到一家店。」他漫不经心地继续说，「进来瞧瞧吧。」

他们小心翼翼地从门框后探进头来，一见威廉显然独揽全店，放了心，张着嘴走进去。他们盯着那一盒盒一瓶瓶的糖果。跟这个比，阿拉丁的宝洞都不算什么了。

「你哪儿弄来的，威廉？」金杰倒抽一口气。

===Original===
“Someone gave it me,” said William. “I took one of them things to be p’lite an’ someone gave it me. Go on,” he said kindly. “Jus’ help yourselves. Not at all. Jus’ help yourselves an’ don’t menshun it.”

They needed no second bidding. With the unerring instinct of childhood (not unsupported by experience) that at any minute their Eden might be invaded by the avenging angel in the shape of a grownup, they made full use of their time. They went from box to box, putting handfuls of sweets and chocolates into their mouths. They said nothing, simply because speech was, under the circumstances, a physical impossibility. Showing a foresight for the future, worthy of the noble ant itself, so often held up as a model to childhood, they filled pockets in the intervals of cramming their mouths.

A close observer might have noticed that William now ate little. William himself had been conscious for some time of a curious and inexplicable feeling of coldness towards the tempting dainties around him. He was, however, loth to give in to the weakness, and every now and then he nonchalantly put into his mouth a Toasted Square or a Fruity Bit.

===Chinese===
「别人送的呗。」威廉说，「我立了个什么有礼貌的决心，就有人送了我一家店。来吧，」他和蔼地说，「随便吃。别客气。随便吃，甭提啦。」

他们无需请第二遍。凭着童年时代那种分毫不差的本能（并非没有经验支撑）——他们的伊甸园随时可能被一位大人模样的复仇天使闯入——他们把时间利用到了极致。他们从一盒吃到另一盒，一把把的糖和巧克力往嘴里送。他们一言不发，纯粹是因为在那种情形下，说话在物理上已不可能。他们还展现出堪与高尚的蚂蚁本身媲美的未雨绸缪——蚂蚁可没少被树为儿童的榜样——在往嘴里猛塞的间隙，又把口袋装满。

细心的观察者也许会注意到，威廉现在吃得很少。威廉自己也早就察觉，他对四周这些诱人的美味精品怀着一种奇怪而莫名的不感兴趣。然而他不肯向这个弱点低头，时不时还得若无其事地往嘴里丢一颗「烤方块」或「果味糖」。

===Original===
It happened that a loutish boy of about fourteen was passing the shop. At the sight of three small boys rapidly consuming the contents, he became interested.

“What yer doin’ of?” he said indignantly, standing in the doorway.

“You get out of my shop,” said William valiantly.

“*Yer* shop?” said the boy. “Yer bloomin’ well pinchin’ things out o’ someone else’s shop, *I* can see. ’Ere, gimme some of them.”

===Chinese===
碰巧有个十四岁上下的野小子路过店门口。一见三个小男孩正风卷残云地消灭店里的存货，他来了兴致。

「你们干啥呢？」他义愤填膺地堵在门口说。

「你给我滚出我的店。」威廉勇猛地说。

「*你的*店？」那小子说，「你明明是在别人店里顺手牵羊，*我*可看得一清二楚。来，分我点儿。」

===Original===
“You get *out*!” said William.

“Get out *yerself*!” said the other.

“If I’d not took one to be p’lite,” said William threateningly, “I’d knock you down.”

“Yer would, would yer?” said the other, beginning to roll up his sleeves.

“Yes, an’ I would, too. You get out.” Seizing the nearest bottle, which happened to contain Acid Drops, he began to fire them at his opponent’s head. One hit him in the eye. He retired into the street. William, now afire for battle, followed him, still hurling Acid Drops with all his might. A crowd of boys collected together. Some gathered Acid Drops from the gutter, others joined the scrimmage. William, Henry, and Ginger carried on a noble fight against heavy odds.

===Chinese===
「你给我*出去*！」威廉说。

「*你*才出去！」对方说。

「要不是我立了个有礼貌的决心，」威廉威胁道，「我一拳撂倒你。」

「你敢，你敢吗？」对方说着，开始挽袖子。

「敢，而且说干就干。你出去。」他抓起手边最近的一只瓶子——里面装的正好是酸味糖——开始朝对手的脑袋开火。一颗命中了他的眼睛。他退到街上。威廉此刻战意熊熊，追了出去，还在拼命猛掷酸味糖。一群男孩聚拢过来。有的从阴沟里捡酸味糖，有的直接加入混战。威廉、亨利和金杰以寡敌众，打了一场高贵的仗。

===Original===
It was only the sight of the proprietor of the shop coming briskly down the sidewalk that put an end to the battle. The street boys made off (with what spoils they could gather) in one direction and Ginger and Henry in another. William, clasping an empty Acid Drop bottle to his bosom, was left to face Mr. Moss.

Mr. Moss entered and looked round with an air of bewilderment.

“Where’s Bill?” he said.

“He’s ill,” said William. “He couldn’t come. I’ve been keepin’ shop for you. I’ve done the best I could.” He looked round the rifled shop and hastened to propitiate the owner as far as possible. “I’ve got some money for you,” he added soothingly, pointing to the four pennies that represented his morning’s takings. “It’s not much,” he went on with some truth, looking again at the rows of emptied boxes and half-emptied bottles and the debris that is always and everywhere the inevitable result of a battle. But Mr. Moss hardly seemed to notice it.

===Chinese===
直到店主本人迈着轻快的步子沿人行道走来，战斗才告终结。街上那帮小子朝一个方向跑了（能捞的战利品都捞上了），金杰和亨利朝另一个方向跑了。威廉怀里搂着一只空空的酸味糖瓶，留下来独自面对莫斯先生。

莫斯先生走进店门，一脸茫然地环顾四周。

「比尔呢？」他说。

「他病了，」威廉说，「来不了。我一直替您看着店呢。我尽了最大努力。」他环顾这间被洗劫过的店，赶紧竭力安抚店主。「我给您挣了点钱，」他安抚道，指了指代表他一上午营业额的那四枚便士。「不算多，」他接着说，这话有几分属实——他又看了一眼那一排排空了的盒子、半空的糖瓶，以及那片任何地方、任何战斗都免不了的狼藉。可莫斯先生似乎根本没注意这些。

===Original===
“Thanks, William,” he said almost humbly. “William, she’s took me. She’s goin’ ter marry me. Isn’t it grand? After all these years!”

“I’m afraid there’s a bit of a mess,” said William, returning to the more important matter.

Mr. Moss waved aside his apologies.

“It doesn’t matter, William,” he said. “Nothing matters today. She’s took me at last. I’m goin’ to shut shop this afternoon and go over to her again. Thanks for staying, William.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢你，威廉。」他近乎谦卑地说，「威廉，她要我了。她要嫁给我了。了不起吧？熬了这么多年啊！」

「恐怕这儿有点乱。」威廉说着，把话题拉回更要紧的事情上。

莫斯先生把他的道歉挥到了一边。

「不要紧的，威廉，」他说，「今天什么都不要紧。她终于要我了。今天下午我打算关店，再到她那边去一趟。谢谢你替我看店，威廉。」

===Original===
“Not at all. Don’t menshun it,” said William nobly. Then, “I think I’ve had enough of that bein’ p’lite. Will one mornin’ do for this year, d’you think?”

“Er﻿—yes. Well, I’ll shut up. Don’t you stay, William. You’ll want to be getting home for lunch.”

Lunch? Quite definitely William decided that he did not want any lunch. The very thought of lunch brought with it a feeling of active physical discomfort which was much more than mere absence of hunger. He decided to go home as quickly as possible, though not to lunch.

“Goo’-bye,” he said.

===Chinese===
「不客气。甭提啦。」威廉大度地说。接着：「我想那个有礼貌，我已经当够了。一个上午就抵今年这一回，您看行不？」

「呃——行。这样吧，我打烊了。你别待着了，威廉。你该回家吃午饭了。」

午饭？威廉斩钉截铁地认定，他不要任何午饭了。一想到午饭，一种实实在在的生理不适便随之而来，远远不止是没有胃口那么简单。他决定尽快回家——尽管不是为了午饭。

「再——见。」他说。

===Original===
“Goodbye,” said Mr. Moss.

“I’m afraid you’ll find some things gone,” said William faintly; “some boys was in.”

“That’s all right, William,” said Mr. Moss, roused again from his rosy dreams. “That’s quite all right.”

But it was not “quite all right” with William. Reader, if you had been left, at the age of eleven, in sole charge of a sweet shop for a whole morning, would it have been “all right” with you? I trow not. But we will not follow William through the humiliating hours of the afternoon. We will leave him as, pale and unsteady, but as yet master of the situation, he wends his homeward way.

===Chinese===
「再见。」莫斯先生说。

「恐怕您会发现少了一些东西，」威廉有气无力地说，「进来过几个男孩。」

「没事儿的，威廉，」莫斯先生说，又从他的玫瑰色美梦里回过神来，「完全没事儿。」

可对威廉来说，这绝非「没事儿」。读者啊，倘若你十一岁那年，被单独留在一家糖果店里看管一整个上午，你还能「没事儿」吗？我是断乎不信的。不过，那个丢人现眼的下午的几个小时，我们就不跟着威廉一起熬了。我们就此别过他吧——他脸色发白、脚下虚浮，但暂且还是局势的主人，正一步一挪地往家走。

## II William the Intruder / 二 闯入者威廉

===Original===
“She’s different from everybody else in the world,” stammered Robert ecstatically. “You simply couldn’t describe her. No one could!”

His mother continued to darn his socks and made no comment.

Only William, his young brother, showed interest.

“*How’s* she different from anyone else?” he demanded. “Is she blind or lame or sumthin’?”

===Chinese===
「她和世上任何人都不同，」罗伯特如痴如醉地结结巴巴道，「你简直没法形容她。谁也没法！」

他的母亲继续给他补袜子，什么也没说。

只有他弟弟威廉显得很感兴趣。

「她*怎么*个跟别人不同法？」他质问道，「她是瞎了还是瘸了还是咋的？」

===Original===
Robert turned on him with exasperation.

“Oh, go and play at trains!” he said. “A child like you can’t understand anything.”

William retired with dignity to the window and listened, with interest unabated, to the rest of the conversation.

===Chinese===
罗伯特又气又恼地冲他转过身来。

「哦，去玩你的小火车吧！」他说，「你这种小毛孩子什么也不懂。」

威廉不失尊严地退到窗边，兴致丝毫不减地把余下的谈话听了个全。

===Original===
“Yes, but who is she, dear?” said their mother. “Robert, I can’t *think* how you get these big holes in your heels!”

Robert ran his hands wildly through his hair.

“I’ve *told* you who she is, Mother,” he said. “I’ve been talking about her ever since I came into the room.”

“Yes, I know, dear, but you haven’t mentioned her name or anything about her.”

“Well,” Robert spoke with an air of superhuman patience, “she’s a Miss Cannon and she’s staying with the Clives and I met her out with Mrs. Clive this morning and she introduced me and she’s the most beautiful girl I’ve ever seen and she﻿—”

===Chinese===
「是啊，可她是谁呀，亲爱的？」他们的母亲说，「罗伯特，我真*想*不通你袜后跟这些大洞是怎么弄出来的！」

罗伯特发狂似的用双手抓挠头发。

「我*跟您说过*她是谁了，妈，」他说，「我一进这屋子就一直在讲她的事。」

「是的，我知道，亲爱的，可你没提过她的名字，也没说过她的任何情况。」

「唉，」罗伯特用一种超乎常人的耐心说道，「她是位卡农小姐，眼下住在克莱夫家，今天早上她跟克莱夫太太一起出门时我遇见了她们，克莱夫太太给我们做了介绍，她是我见过的最美的姑娘，她——」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mrs. Brown hastily, “you told me all that.”

“Well,” went on the infatuated Robert, “we must have her to tea. I know I can’t marry yet﻿—not while I’m still at college﻿—but I could get to know her. Not that I suppose she’d look at me. She’s miles above me﻿—miles above anyone. She’s the most beautiful girl I’ve ever seen. You can’t imagine her. You wouldn’t believe me if I described her. No one could describe her. She﻿—”

Mrs. Brown interrupted him with haste.

“I’ll ask Mrs. Clive to bring her over one afternoon. I’ve no more of this blue wool, Robert. I wish you didn’t have your socks such different colours. I shall have to use mauve. It’s right on the heel; it won’t show.”

===Chinese===
「是了，」布朗太太连忙说，「这些你都跟我说过了。」

「这么说吧，」痴情的罗伯特继续道，「我们必须请她来喝茶。我知道我还不能结婚——我还在念大学呢——可我可以先跟她认识认识。倒不是我指望她会看得上我。她比我高出不知多少——比谁都高出不知多少。她是我见过的最美的姑娘。你们想象不出她什么样。我形容给你们听你们也不会信的。谁也没法形容她。她——」

布朗太太赶紧打断了他。

「我会请克莱夫太太哪天下午带她过来。我这点蓝毛线用完了，罗伯特。我真希望你的袜子颜色别这么杂七杂八。我只好用淡紫色的线了。正好补在袜后跟上；看不出来的。」

===Original===
Robert gave a gasp of horror.

“You *can’t*, Mother. How do you know it won’t show? And even if it didn’t show, the thought of it﻿—! It’s﻿—it’s a crisis of my life now I’ve met her. I can’t go about feeling ridiculous.”

“I say,” said William open-mouthed. “Are you spoony on her?”

“William, don’t use such vulgar expressions,” said Mrs. Brown. “Robert just feels a friendly interest in her, don’t you, Robert?”

“ ‘A friendly interest’!” groaned Robert in despair. “No one ever *tries* to understand what I feel. After all I’ve told you about her and that she’s the most beautiful girl I’ve ever seen and miles above me and above anyone and you think I feel a ‘friendly interest’ in her. It’s﻿—it’s the one great passion of my life! It’s﻿—”

===Chinese===
罗伯特惊恐地倒抽了一口冷气。

「您*千万不能*这么干，妈。您怎么知道看不出来？就算看不出来，一想到袜后跟是紫线的——！这——这可是我遇见她之后人生的紧要关头。我不能一脸可笑地到处走动。」

「我说，」威廉张着嘴说，「你是不是迷上她啦？」

「威廉，别用这种粗俗字眼，」布朗太太说，「罗伯特对她只是怀有一种朋友式的兴趣，对不对，罗伯特？」

「『朋友式的兴趣』！」罗伯特绝望地呻吟道，「从来就没有人*肯*试着理解我的感情。我把她的情况都跟你们说了，说了她是我见过的最美的姑娘，说我比她差了不知多少、比谁都差，可你们竟然以为我对她怀的是『朋友式的兴趣』。这——这是我此生唯一伟大的激情！这是——」

===Original===
“Well,” put in Mrs. Brown mildly, “I’ll ring up Mrs. Clive and ask if she’s doing anything tomorrow afternoon.”

Robert’s tragic young face lit up, then he stood wrapt in thought, and a cloud of anxiety overcast it.

“Ellen can press the trousers of my brown suit tonight, can’t she? And, Mother, could you get me some socks and a tie before tomorrow? Blue, I think﻿—a bright blue, you know, not too bright, but not so as you don’t notice them. I wish the laundry was a decent one. You know, a man’s collar ought to *shine* when it’s new on. They never put a shine on to them. I’d better have some new ones for tomorrow. It’s so important, how one looks. She﻿—people *judge* you on how you look. They﻿—”

===Chinese===
「好了，」布朗太太温和地插话道，「我这就给克莱夫太太打电话，问问她明天下午有没有空。」

罗伯特那张悲剧性的年轻面孔一下子亮了，随即他站在那儿陷入沉思，一片焦虑的阴云又蒙上脸来。

「埃伦今晚能把我那套棕色西服的裤子熨一熨，对吧？还有，妈，您能在明天之前给我弄几双袜子和一条领带吗？要蓝色的，我想——鲜蓝色，您知道，别太鲜，可也别鲜得让人注意不到。我真希望那家洗衣店像点样。您知道，男人的领子刚上脖的时候应该*发亮*才是。他们从来不给领子上亮。我明天最好换几副新领子。一个人外表如何太重要了。她——人们就是*凭*外表评判你的。他们——」

===Original===
Mrs. Brown laid her work aside.

“I’ll go and ring up Mrs. Clive now,” she said.

When she returned, William had gone and Robert was standing by the window, his face pale with suspense, and a Napoleonic frown on his brow.

“Mrs. Clive can’t come,” announced Mrs. Brown in her comfortable voice, “but Miss Cannon will come alone. It appears she’s met Ethel before. So you needn’t worry any more, dear.”

===Chinese===
布朗太太把手里的活计放到一边。

「我现在就去给克莱夫太太打电话。」她说。

她回来时，威廉已经走了，罗伯特正站在窗边，脸因悬念而发白，眉间锁着一副拿破仑式的怒容。

「克莱夫太太来不了，」布朗太太用她那令人宽心的嗓音宣布道，「不过卡农小姐会一个人来。看来她从前就认识埃塞尔。所以你不用再担心了，亲爱的。」

===Original===
Robert gave a sardonic laugh.

“*Worry!*” he said, “There’s plenty to worry about still. What about William?”

“Well, what about him?”

“Well, can’t he go away somewhere tomorrow? Things never go right when William’s there. You know they don’t.”

“The poor boy must have tea with us, dear. He’ll be very good, I’m sure. Ethel will be home then and she’ll help. I’ll tell William not to worry you. I’m sure he’ll be good.”

===Chinese===
罗伯特讥诮地笑了一声。

「*担心*！」他说，「要担心的事还多着呢。威廉怎么办？」

「嗯，威廉怎么了？」

「唉，就不能让他明天上哪儿待着去吗？只要有威廉在场，事情就没一件顺的。您是知道的。」

「可怜的孩子总得跟我们一起喝茶呀，亲爱的。我保证他会乖乖的。到时候埃塞尔在家，她会帮着照应。我会嘱咐威廉不许搅扰你。我保证他会乖乖的。」

---

===Original===
William had received specific instructions. He was not to come into the house till the tea-bell rang, and he was to go out and play in the garden again directly after tea. He was perfectly willing to obey them. He was thrilled by the thought of Robert in the role of the lovelorn hero. He took the situation quite seriously.

He was in the garden when the visitor came up the drive. He had been told not to obtrude himself upon her notice, so he crept up silently and peered at her through the rhododendron bushes. The proceeding also happened to suit his character of the moment, which was that of a Red Indian chief.

Miss Cannon was certainly pretty. She had brown hair, brown eyes, and dimples that came and went in her rosy cheeks. She was dressed in white and carried a parasol. She walked up the drive, looking neither to right nor left, till a slight movement in the bushes arrested her attention. She turned quickly and saw a small boy’s face, smeared black with burnt cork and framed in hens’ feathers tied on with tape. The dimples peeped out.

“Hail, O great chief!” she said.

===Chinese===
威廉接到了明确的指令：喝茶的铃响之前不许进屋，喝完茶立刻回花园去玩。他完全乐意服从。一想到罗伯特要充当害相思病的主角，他就兴奋不已。他把这桩事看得极其郑重。

客人沿着车道走上来时，他正在花园里。他被告知不得在她跟前招摇露面，于是悄悄爬上前去，透过杜鹃花丛窥视她。这一举动恰好也合他此刻的身份——一名印第安酋长。

卡农小姐确实漂亮。她有一头棕发、一双棕色眼睛，红润的脸颊上一对酒窝时隐时现。她一身白裙，手执阳伞，沿着车道款款走来，目不斜视，直到花丛里一阵轻微的响动攫住了她的注意力。她迅速转身，看见一张小男孩的脸——脸用烧焦的软木涂得漆黑，四周框着用带子扎上去的鸡毛。酒窝浮现了出来。

「万福，伟大的酋长！」她说。

===Original===
William gazed at her open-mouthed. Such intelligence on the part of a grownup was unusual.

“Chief Red Hand,” he supplied with a fierce scowl.

She bowed low, brown eyes alight with merriment.

“And what death awaits the poor white face who has fallen defenceless into his hand?”

“You better come quiet to my wigwam an’ see,” said Red Hand darkly.

===Chinese===
威廉张着嘴呆望她。大人能有这样的悟性，实属罕见。

「红手酋长。」他凶狠狠地皱着眉补充道。

她深深一躬，棕色眼睛里闪着笑意。

「不知这位不幸落入他手中、无力自卫的白脸人，等着他的是个什么死法？」

「你最好乖乖跟我到我的棚屋去看看。」红手阴沉沉地说。

===Original===
She threw a glance to the bend in the drive behind which lay the house and with a low laugh followed him through the bushes. From one point the drawing-room window could be seen, and there the anxious Robert stood, pale with anxiety, stiff and upright in his newly-creased trousers (well turned up to show the new blue socks), his soulful eyes fixed steadfastly on the bend in the drive round which the beloved should come. Every now and then his nervous hand wandered up to touch the new tie and gleaming new collar, which was rather too high and too tight for comfort, but which the shopkeeper had informed his harassed customer was the “latest and most correct shape.”

Meanwhile the beloved had reached William’s “dugout.” William had made this himself of branches cut down from the trees and spent many happy hours in it with one or other of his friends.

“Here is the wigwam, Paleface,” he said in a sepulchral voice. “Stand here while I decide with Snake Face and the other chiefs what’s goin’ to be done to you. There’s Snake Face an’ the others,” he added in his natural voice, pointing to a small cluster of shrubs.

===Chinese===
她朝车道拐弯处瞥了一眼——宅子就藏在那后头——随即轻笑一声，跟着他钻进了花丛。从某个位置能望见客厅的窗户，焦灼的罗伯特正站在那里，急得脸色发白，穿着熨出崭新裤线的裤子僵硬笔挺地立着（裤脚好好翻卷着，好露出崭新的蓝袜子），那双深情的眼睛死死盯着车道拐弯处——他的心上人就该从那里出现。他不时紧张地抬手去摸新领带和崭新发亮的领子。那领子又高又紧，戴着并不舒服，可店主已向他这位备受折磨的顾客保证过，这是「最新、最正统的式样」。

与此同时，心上人已经到了威廉的「地洞」。这是威廉自己用树上砍下来的枝条搭成的，他常和这个或那个伙伴在里面度过许多快活时光。

「棚屋到了，白脸人。」他用坟墓里般的嗓音说道，「你站在这儿等着，我去跟蛇脸和别的酋长商量商量怎么处置你。蛇脸他们就在那儿。」他用平时的嗓音补充道，指了指一小丛灌木。

===Original===
Approaching these, he stood and talked fiercely and unintelligibly for a few minutes, turning his scowling corked face and pointing his finger at her every now and then, as, apparently, he described his capture.

Then he approached her again.

“That was Red Indian what I was talkin’ then,” he explained in his ordinary voice, then sinking it to its low, roaring note and scowling more ferociously than ever, “Snake Face says the Paleface must be scalped and cooked and eat!”

He took out a penknife and opened it as though to perform the operation, then continued, “But me and the others say that if you’ll be a squaw an’ cook for us we’ll let you go alive.”

===Chinese===
他朝那丛灌木走去，站在那儿凶悍而含混不清地说了几分钟，不时转着他那张涂了软木、怒容满面的脸，手指着她，显然是在描述自己怎样俘获了她。

然后他又走回她跟前。

「刚才我说的是印第安话。」他用平常的嗓音解释道，随即又压低成瓮声瓮气的咆哮，脸色比先前更凶，「蛇脸说，这白脸人得剥头皮、下锅、吃掉！」

他掏出一把小折刀，把它打开，仿佛马上就要动刑，接着又续道：「不过我跟其他酋长说好了，要是你肯当婆娘给我们做饭，我们就放你一条活命。」

===Original===
Miss Cannon dropped on to her knees.

“Most humble and grateful thanks, great Red Hand,” she said. “I will with pleasure be your squaw.”

“I’ve gotter fire round here,” said William proudly, leading her to the back of the wigwam, where a small wood fire smouldered spiritlessly, choked by a large tin full of a dark liquid.

“That, O Squaw,” said Red Hand with a dramatic gesture, “is a Paleface we caught las’ night!”

===Chinese===
卡农小姐双膝跪倒。

「万分谦卑、感激不尽，伟大的红手，」她说，「我心甘情愿做您的婆娘。」

「俺这儿周围生着火呢。」威廉骄傲地说，领着她绕到棚屋后头。那儿一小堆柴火有气无力地闷烧着，火上坐着一只装满深色液体的大铁皮罐，把火都压得喘不上气来。

「那个，噢，婆娘，」红手煞有介事地一挥手，「是俺们昨晚抓到的一个白脸人！」

===Original===
The squaw clasped her hands together.

“Oh, how *lovely*!” she said. “Is he cooking?”

Red Hand nodded. Then,

“I’ll get you some feathers,” he said obligingly. “You oughter have feathers, too.”

===Chinese===
婆娘把双手紧紧握在一起。

「噢，多*妙*哇！」她说，「他正炖着呢？」

红手点了点头。接着——

「我去给你弄些羽毛来。」他热心地说，「你也该插上羽毛。」

===Original===
He retired into the depth of the wigwam and returned with a handful of hen feathers. Miss Cannon took off her big shady hat and stuck the feathers into her fluffy brown hair with a laugh.

“This is jolly!” she said. “I love Red Indians!”

“I’ve got some cork you can have to do your face, too,” went on William with reckless generosity. “It soon burns in the fire.”

She threw a glance towards the chimneys of the house that could be seen through the trees and shook her pretty head regretfully.

===Chinese===
他退进棚屋深处，捧着一把鸡毛回来了。卡农小姐摘下那顶宽大的遮阳帽，笑着把羽毛插进她蓬松的棕发里。

「真带劲！」她说，「我最喜欢印第安人了！」

「我还有块软木，也给你涂脸用。」威廉大手大脚得没了边，继续说道，「搁火里一会儿就烧好了。」

她朝树梢间望得见的宅子烟囱瞥了一眼，惋惜地摇了摇漂亮的脑袋。

===Original===
“I’m afraid I’d better not,” she said sadly.

“Well,” he said, “now I’ll go huntin’ and you stir the Paleface and we’ll eat him when I come back. Now, I’ll be off. You watch me track.”

He opened his clasp-knife with a bloodthirsty flourish and, casting sinister glances round him, crept upon his hands and knees into the bushes. He circled about, well within his squaw’s vision, obviously bent upon impressing her. She stirred the mixture in the tin with a twig and threw him every now and then the admiring glances he so evidently desired.

Soon he returned, carrying over his shoulder a doormat which he threw down at her feet.

“A venison, O squaw,” he said in a lordly voice. “Let it be cooked. I’ve had it out all morning,” he added in his ordinary tones; “they’ve not missed it yet.”

===Chinese===
「恐怕我最好还是别涂了。」她黯然说道。

「那好，」他说，「现在我去打猎，你来搅白脸人，等我回来咱们就吃他。我这就动身。你瞧好我怎么追踪。」

他杀气腾腾地一甩手打开折刀，朝四周阴森森地扫了几眼，手脚并用地爬进灌木丛。他在完全处于婆娘视野之内的范围里绕来绕去，显然一门心思要给她留下深刻印象。她用一根小树枝搅着罐子里的东西，不时朝他投去他显然渴慕的倾慕目光。

不多时他回来了，肩上扛着一块门垫，扔在她脚边。

「一块鹿肉，噢，婆娘。」他颐指气使地说，「拿去做了吧。我把它扛出来一早上了，」他用平常的声调补充道，「他们还没发觉少了呢。」

===Original===
He fetched from the “wigwam” two small jagged tins and, taking the larger tin off the fire, poured some into each.

“Now,” he said, “here’s some Paleface for you, squaw.”

“Oh,” she said, “I’m sure he’s awfully good, but﻿—”

“You needn’t be frightened of it,” said William protectively. “It’s jolly good, I can tell you.” He picked up the paper cover of a packet of soup from behind the trees. “It’s jus’ that and water and it’s jolly good!”

===Chinese===
他从「棚屋」里取来两只豁着口的小铁皮罐，把那只大罐从火上端下来，往每只罐里倒了些。

「来，」他说，「这是给你的一份白脸人，婆娘。」

「噢，」她说，「我敢说他味道一定好极了，可是——」

「你用不着怕它。」威廉一副护着她的口气，「我告诉你吧，好喝得很。」他从树后头捡起一张汤料的包装纸。「就是这玩意儿兑上水，好喝得很！」

===Original===
“How lovely! Do they let you﻿—?”

“They don’t let me,” he broke in hastily, “but there’s heaps in the larder and they don’t notice one every now an’ then. Go on!” encouragingly, “I don’t mind you having it! Honest, I don’t! I’ll get some more soon.”

Bravely she raised the tin to her lips and took a sip.

“Gorgeous!” she said, shutting her eyes. Then she drained the tin.

William’s face shone with pride and happiness. But it clouded over as the sound of a bell rang out from the house.

===Chinese===
「多好哇！他们肯让你——？」

「他们不让，」他急忙插嘴道，「可食品柜里多得很，隔三差五少一包他们发觉不了。喝吧！」他鼓励着，「我不在乎你喝！真的，不骗你！回头我再弄一些来。」

她勇敢地举起铁皮罐凑到唇边，抿了一小口。

「美极了！」她说着闭上了眼睛。接着她把一罐喝了个底朝天。

威廉的脸因骄傲和幸福而容光焕发。可宅子里传出一阵铃声，他的脸随即阴沉下来。

===Original===
“Crumbs! That’s tea!”

Hastily Miss Cannon took the feathers from her hair and put on her hat.

“You don’t keep a looking-glass in your wigwam I suppose?” she said.

“N-no,” admitted William. “But I’ll get one for next time you come. I’ll get one from Ethel’s room.”

“Won’t she mind?”

===Chinese===
「糟了！喝茶了！」

卡农小姐急忙从头发里取下羽毛，戴上帽子。

「我想你们棚屋里是不备镜子的吧？」她说。

「不——不备，」威廉承认道，「不过你下次来我就能弄一面了。我从埃塞尔房里弄。」

「她不会不高兴吗？」

===Original===
“She won’t know,” said William simply.

Miss Cannon smoothed down her dress.

“I’m horribly late. What will they think of me? It was awful of me to come with you. I’m always doing awful things. That’s a secret between you and me.” She gave William a smile that dazzled him. “Now come in and we’ll confess.”

“I can’t,” said William. “I’ve got to wash an’ come down tidy. I promised I would. It’s a special day. Because of Robert, you know. Well *you* know. Because of﻿—Robert!”

He looked up at her mystified face with a significant nod.

===Chinese===
「她不会知道的。」威廉说得干脆。

卡农小姐抚平了裙子。

「我迟到得吓死人。他们会怎么想我？我跟你跑了这么久，真是太不像话了。我净干不像话的事。这是你我之间的秘密。」她朝威廉嫣然一笑，把他晃得眼花缭乱。「走，咱们进去坦白去。」

「我不行，」威廉说，「我得洗洗干净、整整齐齐地下去。我答应了会的。今天是个特别的日子。因为罗伯特，你懂的。呃，*你*懂的。因为——罗伯特！」

他抬头望着她那张困惑的脸，意味深长地点了点头。

===Original===
Robert was frantic. He had run his hands through his hair so often that it stood around his head like a spiked halo.

“We *can’t* begin without her,” he said. “She’ll think we’re awful. It will﻿—put her off me forever. She’s not used to being treated like that. She’s the sort of girl people don’t begin without. She’s the most beautiful girl I’ve ever met in all my life and you﻿—my own mother﻿—treat her like this. You may be ruining my life. You’ve no idea what this means to me. If you’d seen her you’d feel more sympathy. I simply can’t describe her﻿—I﻿—”

“I said four o’clock, Robert,” said Mrs. Brown firmly, “and it’s after half-past. Ethel, tell Emma she can ring the bell and bring in tea.”

The perspiration stood out on Robert’s brow.

“It’s﻿—the downfall of all my hopes,” he said hoarsely.

===Chinese===
罗伯特都快疯了。他一遍又一遍地用双手抓挠头发，头发根根倒竖，围着脑袋支棱成一圈刺猬似的光环。

「她没到咱们*不能*开席，」他说，「她会觉得我们糟透了。这会——让她永远对我没了兴致。她不习惯受这种对待。她是那种人家非等她到了才开席的姑娘。她是我这辈子遇见过的最美的姑娘，而您——我的亲妈——竟这样待她。您这可能是在毁我的一生。您根本不知道这对我意味着什么。您要是见过她，就会多几分同情。我实在没法形容她——我——」

「我说的是四点钟，罗伯特，」布朗太太语气坚决，「现在已经过了半点。埃塞尔，去告诉埃玛，可以摇铃上茶了。」

罗伯特额头上沁出了汗珠。

「这——是我全部希望的覆灭。」他声音沙哑地说。

===Original===
Then, a few minutes after the echoes of the tea-bell died away, the front door bell rang sharply. Robert stroked his hair down with wild, unrestrained movements of his hands, and summoned a tortured smile to his lips.

Miss Cannon appeared upon the threshold, bewitching and demure.

“Aren’t I perfectly disgraceful?” she said with her low laugh. “To tell the truth, I met your little boy in the drive and I’ve been with him some time. He’s a perfect little dear, isn’t he?”

Her brown eyes rested on Robert. Robert moistened his lips and smiled the tortured smile, but was beyond speech.

“Yes, I know Ethel and I met your son﻿—*yesterday*, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
接着，茶铃声的余音消散几分钟后，前门门铃骤然响起。罗伯特双手狂乱而不加节制地把头发往下抹，朝唇边挤出一个受刑般的微笑。

卡农小姐出现在门口，妩媚动人又端庄娴静。

「我是不是糟糕透顶？」她轻声笑着说，「不瞒你们说，我在车道上碰见了你们家小家伙，陪他待了好一阵子。他真是个可爱的小宝贝，是不是？」

她那双棕色眼睛落在罗伯特身上。罗伯特舔了舔嘴唇，挤出一个受刑般的微笑，却再说不出话来。

「是了，我认识埃塞尔，我还见过您家少爷——是*昨天*来着吧？」

===Original===
Robert murmured unintelligibly, raising one hand to the too tight collar, and then bowed vaguely in her direction.

Then they went in to tea.

William, his hair well brushed, the cork partially washed from his face, and the feathers removed, arrived a few minutes later. Conversation was carried on chiefly by Miss Cannon and Ethel. Robert racked his brain for some striking remark, something that would raise him in her esteem far above the ranks of the ordinary young man, but nothing came. Whenever her brown eyes rested on him, however, he summoned the mirthless smile to his lips and raised a hand to relieve the strain of the imprisoning collar. Desperately he felt the precious moments passing and his passion yet unrevealed, except by his eyes, whose message he was afraid she had not read.

===Chinese===
罗伯特含混不清地咕哝了一句，抬手碰了碰那副勒得太紧的领子，随即朝她的方向茫然地鞠了一躬。

然后他们进去喝茶。

几分钟后威廉来了，头发梳得整整齐齐，脸上的软木黑灰洗掉了一部分，羽毛也拔干净了。谈话主要由卡农小姐和埃塞尔撑着。罗伯特绞尽脑汁想找一句惊天动地的话，一句能让她对他的敬重远远超出平庸青年行列的话，可什么也想不出来。然而每当她的棕色眼睛落在他身上，他就把那个不带笑意的微笑召唤到唇边，抬手松一松那囚笼般领子的勒劲。他绝望地感到宝贵的时光正在流逝，而他的激情尚未表白——只除了他的眼睛，只是他生怕她没有读懂眼睛里的话。

===Original===
As they rose from tea, William turned to his mother, with an anxious sibilant whisper,

“Ought *I* to have put on my best suit *too*?”

The demure lights danced in Miss Cannon’s eyes and the look the perspiring Robert sent him would have crushed a less bold spirit.

William had quite forgotten the orders he had received to retire from the scene directly after tea. He was impervious to all hints. He followed in the train of the all-conquering Miss Cannon to the drawing-room and sat on the sofa with Robert who had taken his seat next his beloved.

===Chinese===
大家起身离席时，威廉转向母亲，焦灼地咝咝低语：

「是不是*我*也*该*穿上我那套最好的衣服？」

端庄狡黠的光在卡农小姐眼里跳舞，而汗涔涔的罗伯特投向他的那一眼，早能把一个胆子小些的灵魂碾得粉碎。

威廉早把喝完茶立刻退场的命令忘得一干二净。他对一切暗示都无动于衷。他跟在无往不胜的卡农小姐的队伍后面进了客厅，跟罗伯特一起坐上沙发——罗伯特已在他心上人身边落了座。

===Original===
“Are you﻿—er﻿—fond of reading, Miss Cannon?” began Robert with a painful effort.

“I﻿—*wrote* a tale once,” said William boastfully, leaning over Robert before she could answer. “It was a jolly good one. I showed it to some people. I’ll show it to you if you like. It began with a pirate on a raft an’ he’d stole some jewel’ry and the king the jewels belonged to was coming after him on a steamer and jus’ when he was comin’ up to him he jumped into the water and took the jewls with him an’ a fish eat the jewls and the king caught it an’,” he paused for breath.

“I’d love to read it!” said Miss Cannon.

===Chinese===
「您——呃——喜欢读书吗，卡农小姐？」罗伯特费了九牛二虎之力才开腔。

「我——可是*写过*一个故事的呢。」还没等她回答，威廉就越过罗伯特抢着自夸，「写得可带劲了。我拿给好些人看过。你要愿意我就拿给你看。开头是个海盗待在木筏上，他偷了些珠宝，珠宝的主人国王坐着一艘轮船追他，眼看就要追上了，他就跳进水里，把珠宝带在身上，一条鱼把珠宝吞了，国王把鱼逮住了，还有——」他停下来喘气。

「我太想读一读了！」卡农小姐说。

===Original===
Robert turned sideways, and resting an arm on his knee to exclude the persistent William, spoke in a husky voice.

“What is your favourite flower, Miss Cannon?”

William’s small head was craned round Robert’s arm.

“I’ve gotter garden. I’ve got Virginia Stock grow’n all over it. It grows up in no time. An’ must’erd ’n cress grows in no time, too. I like things what grow quick, don’t you? You get tired of waiting for the other sorts, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
罗伯特侧过身去，一只手臂拄在膝盖上，好隔开纠缠不清的威廉，哑着嗓子开口说话。

「您最喜欢什么花，卡农小姐？」

威廉的小脑袋从罗伯特的手臂旁探了出来。

「我有个花园。我的弗吉尼亚雪轮开得满园都是。一眨眼就长起来了。芥菜苗和水芹苗也是一眨眼就长起来。我喜欢长得快的东西，你喜欢不？等那些长得慢的，等得人都烦了，是不是？」

===Original===
Robert rose desperately.

“Would you care to see the garden and greenhouses, Miss Cannon?” he said.

“I’d love to,” said Miss Cannon.

With a threatening glare at William, Robert led the way to the garden. And William, all innocent animation, followed.

===Chinese===
罗伯特不顾一切地站起身来。

「您愿意参观一下花园和暖房吗，卡农小姐？」他说。

「非常愿意。」卡农小姐说。

罗伯特威吓地瞪了威廉一眼，领头往花园去。威廉浑然无事、兴致勃勃地跟在后面。

===Original===
“Can you tie knots what can’t come untied?” he demanded.

“No,” she said, “I wish I could.”

“I can. I’ll show you. I’ll get a piece of string and show you afterwards. It’s easy but it wants practice, that’s all. An’ I’ll teach you how to make aeroplanes out of paper what fly in the air when it’s windy. That’s quite easy. Only you’ve gotter be careful to get ’em the right size. I can make ’em and I can make lots of things out of matchboxes an’ things an’﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你会打解不开的结吗？」他发问。

「不会，」她说，「要是会就好了。」

「我会。我打给你看。回头我找根绳子来打给你看。容易得很，就是要练练，就这个。我还会教你用纸折飞机，刮风的时候能飞上天。那个也容易得很。就是得留神把它们折得尺寸正好。我会折，我还能用火柴盒啥的做好多好多东西，还有——」

===Original===
The infuriated Robert interrupted.

“These are my father’s roses. He’s very proud of them.”

“They’re beautiful.”

“Well, wait till you see my Virginia Stock! that’s all. Wait﻿—”

===Chinese===
恼怒至极的罗伯特打断了他。

「这些是我父亲的玫瑰。他可宝贝它们了。」

「真美。」

「哼，等你见了我的弗吉尼亚雪轮再说！就这样。等着——」

===Original===
“Will you have this tea-rose, Miss Cannon?” Robert’s face was purple as he presented it. “It﻿—it﻿—er﻿—it suits you. You﻿—er﻿—flowers and you﻿—that is﻿—I’m sure﻿—you love flowers﻿—you should﻿—er﻿—always have flowers. If I﻿—”

“An’ I’ll get you those red ones and that white one,” broke in the equally infatuated William, determined not to be outshone. “An’ I’ll get you some of my Virginia Stock. An’ I don’t give my Virginia Stock to *anyone*,” he added with emphasis.

When they reentered the drawing-room, Miss Cannon carried a large bouquet of Virginia Stock and white and red roses which completely hid Robert’s tea-rose. William was by her side, chatting airily and confidently. Robert followed﻿—a pale statue of despair.

===Chinese===
「您要这朵茶玫瑰吗，卡农小姐？」罗伯特献花时脸都憋紫了。「它——它——呃——它跟您相配。您——呃——花和您——就是说——我敢说——您爱花——您应该——呃——永远有花。如果我——」

「我去给你摘那些红的，还有那朵白的。」同样痴狂的威廉插了进来，打定主意不能被比下去。「我还给你摘我的弗吉尼亚雪轮。我的弗吉尼亚雪轮可是*谁也不给*的。」他加重语气补了一句。

他们再回到客厅时，卡农小姐捧着一大束弗吉尼亚雪轮和红白玫瑰，把罗伯特那朵茶玫瑰遮得严严实实。威廉在她身边，轻快而自信地聊着。罗伯特跟在后头——一座面无人色的绝望雕像。

===Original===
In answer to Robert’s agonised glance, Mrs. Brown summoned William to her corner, while Robert and Miss Cannon took their seat again upon the sofa.

“I hope﻿—I hope,” said Robert soulfully, “I hope your stay here is a long one?”

“Well, why shan’t I jus’ *speak* to her?” William’s whisper was loud and indignant.

“ ’Sh, dear!” said Mrs. Brown.

“I should like to show you some of the walks around here,” went on Robert desperately with a fearful glance towards the corner where William stood in righteous indignation before his mother. “If I could have that﻿—er﻿—pleasure﻿—er﻿—honour?”

===Chinese===
为了回应罗伯特那痛苦的眼神，布朗太太把威廉召唤到自己那个角落里，好让罗伯特和卡农小姐重新在沙发上落座。

「我希望——我希望，」罗伯特深情地说，「我希望您在这儿多住些日子？」

「唉，我怎么就不能*跟她说说话*？」威廉的低语又响又愤愤不平。

「嘘，亲爱的！」布朗太太说。

「我想带您走一走这一带散步的小路。」罗伯特不顾一切地接着说，一面胆战心惊地朝角落瞥了一眼——威廉正气壮山河地站在母亲跟前。「如果我能有幸——呃——享受那份——呃——荣幸？」

===Original===
“I was only jus’ *speaking* to her,” went on William’s voice. “I wasn’t doin’ any harm, was I? Only *speaking* to her!”

The silence was intense. Robert, purple, opened his lips to say something, anything to drown that horrible voice, but nothing would come. Miss Cannon was obviously listening to William.

“Is no one else ever to *speak* to her.” The sibilant whisper, raised in indignant appeal, filled all the room. “Jus’ ’cause Robert’s fell in love with her?”

The horror of the moment haunted Robert’s nights and days for weeks to come.

===Chinese===
「我不过是*跟她说说话*。」威廉的声音继续响着，「我又没干啥坏事，是不是？不过是*说说话*！」

满屋死一样的寂静。罗伯特满脸发紫，张开嘴想说点什么——什么都行，只要能淹没那可怕的声音——可什么也出不来。卡农小姐分明正在听威廉说话。

「别人就再也别想*跟她说话*了吗？」那咝咝的低语愤然拔高，充满了整个房间。「就因为罗伯特恋上她了吗？」

那一刻的恐怖，在此后好几个星期里日夜萦绕着罗伯特。

===Original===
Mrs. Brown coughed hastily and began to describe at unnecessary length the ravages of the caterpillars upon her husband’s favourite rose-tree.

William withdrew with dignity to the garden a minute later and Miss Cannon rose from the sofa.

“I must be going, I’m afraid,” she said with a smile.

Robert, anguished and overpowered, rose slowly.

“You must come again some time,” he said weakly but with passion undaunted.

“I will,” she said. “I’m longing to see more of William. I adore William!”

===Chinese===
布朗太太慌忙咳嗽一声，开始不厌其烦地大讲毛虫在她丈夫最心爱的玫瑰树上造下的浩劫。

一分钟后，威廉不失尊严地撤回花园，卡农小姐也从沙发上站起身来。

「恐怕我该走了。」她微笑着说。

罗伯特痛苦不堪、心力交瘁，缓缓起身。

「您一定得找时间再来。」他说得有气无力，激情却毫不气馁。

「我会的，」她说，「我巴不得多见见威廉。我太喜欢威廉了！」

===Original===
They comforted Robert’s wounded feelings as best they could, but it was Ethel who devised the plan that finally cheered him. She suggested a picnic on the following Thursday, which happened to be Robert’s birthday and incidentally the last day of Miss Cannon’s visit, and the picnic party was to consist of﻿—Robert, Ethel, Mrs. Clive and Miss Cannon, and William was not even to be told where it was to be. The invitation was sent that evening and Robert spent the week dreaming of picnic lunches and suggesting impossible dainties of which the cook had never heard. It was not until she threatened to give notice that he reluctantly agreed to leave the arrangements to her. He sent his white flannels (which were perfectly clean) to the laundry with a note attached, hinting darkly at legal proceedings if they were not sent back, spotless, by Thursday morning. He went about with an expression of set and solemn purpose upon his frowning countenance. William he utterly ignored. He bought a book of poems at a secondhand bookshop and kept them on the table by his bed.

They saw nothing of Miss Cannon in the interval, but Thursday dawned bright and clear, and Robert’s anxious spirits rose. He was presented with a watch and chain by his father and with a bicycle by his mother and a tin of toffee (given not without ulterior motive) by William.

They met Mrs. Clive and Miss Cannon at the station and took tickets to a village a few miles away whence they had decided to walk to a shady spot on the river bank.

===Chinese===
他们竭尽所能抚慰罗伯特受伤的感情，但最终让他振作起来的妙计是埃塞尔想出来的。她提议下星期四去野餐——那天恰好是罗伯特的生日，又碰巧是卡农小姐做客的最后一天——野餐队的成员是：罗伯特、埃塞尔、克莱夫太太和卡农小姐，而且连野餐的地点都不打算告诉威廉。邀请当晚就送了出去，那一整个星期罗伯特都做着野餐午餐的美梦，一会儿提议一种厨师闻所未闻的刁钻点心，直到厨师扬言要辞工不干，他才勉强同意把筹备事宜交给她。他把白法兰绒裤子（本来就干干净净）送去洗衣店，附上一张字条，阴沉沉地暗示：要是星期四早上不送回来、不雪白锃亮，就要诉诸法律。他皱着眉头走东走西，一脸破釜沉舟的庄严使命。威廉被他彻底无视。他在旧书店买了一本诗集，搁在床边的桌上。

这中间他们没再见到卡农小姐，但星期四天一亮便是个晴朗清澈的好日子，罗伯特焦灼的心绪也随之昂扬。父亲送了他一块怀表和表链，母亲送了他一辆自行车，威廉送了他一罐太妃糖（送得并非不怀私心）。

他们在车站与克莱夫太太和卡农小姐会合，买了去几英里外一座村庄的车票，打算从那里步行到河岸上一处阴凉的地方。

===Original===
William’s dignity was slightly offended by his pointed exclusion from the party, but he had resigned himself to it, and spent the first part of the morning in the character of Chief Red Hand among the rhododendron bushes. He had added an ostrich feather found in Ethel’s room to his headdress, and used almost a whole cork on his face. He wore the doormat pinned to his shoulders.

After melting some treacle toffee in rainwater over his smoking fire, adding orange juice and drinking the resulting liquid, he tired of the game and wandered upstairs to Robert’s bedroom to inspect his birthday presents. The tin of toffee was on the table by Robert’s bed. William took one or two as a matter of course and began to read the love-poems. He was horrified a few minutes later to see the tin empty, but he fastened the lid with a sigh, wondering if Robert would guess who had eaten them. He was afraid he would. Anyway he’d given him them. And anyway, he hadn’t known he was eating them.

He then went to the dressing-table and tried on the watch and chain at various angles and with various postures. He finally resisted the temptation to wear them for the rest of the morning and replaced them on the dressing-table.

Then he wandered downstairs and round to the shed, where Robert’s new bicycle stood in all its glory. It was shining and spotless and William gazed at it in awe and admiration. He came to the conclusion that he could do it no possible harm by leading it carefully round the house. Encouraged by the fact that Mrs. Brown was out shopping, he walked it round the house several times. He much enjoyed the feeling of importance and possession that it gave him. He felt loth to part with it. He wondered if it was very hard to ride. He had tried to ride one once when he was staying with an aunt. He stood on a garden bench and with difficulty transferred himself from that to the bicycle seat. To his surprise and delight he rode for a few yards before he fell off. He tried again and fell off again. He tried again and rode straight into a holly bush. He forgot everything in his determination to master the art. He tried again and again. He fell off or rode into the holly bush again and again. The shining black paint of the bicycle was scratched, the handle bars were slightly bent and dulled; William himself was bruised and battered but unbeaten.

===Chinese===
威廉的尊严因自己被明显排除在野餐队伍之外而略受伤害，但他已听天由命，一上午的前半段都以红手酋长的身份在杜鹃花丛中度过。他在头饰上添了一根从埃塞尔房里找到的鸵鸟毛，脸上几乎用掉了整整一块软木，肩上还用别针别着那块门垫。

他在冒着烟的火堆上用雨水化开几块糖浆太妃糖，兑上橙汁，把这份劳动成果喝了下去，随后玩腻了这个游戏，晃上楼到罗伯特的卧室去参观他的生日礼物。那罐太妃糖就在罗伯特床边的桌上。威廉顺理成章地拿了一两块，接着读起了那本情诗集。几分钟后他惊恐地发现罐子已经空了，但他叹了口气扣上盖子，寻思罗伯特会不会猜出是谁吃的。他怕会猜到。反正糖是他送的。再说，他一直不知道自己吃的就是那些糖呀。

随后他走到梳妆台前，以各种角度、各种姿势试戴那块怀表和表链。他终于抵挡住了把表戴完这一上午的诱惑，把它们放回了梳妆台上。

然后他晃下楼，绕到棚屋那儿，罗伯特的新自行车正雄姿英发地立在里头。它锃亮无瑕，威廉又敬畏又艳羡地凝视着它。他得出结论：小心翼翼推着它绕房子转转，绝不可能对它有任何损伤。想到布朗太太出门买东西去了，他胆气一壮，推着它绕房子走了好几圈。他十分享受它带给自己的那种重要感和占有感。他都舍不得跟它分开了。他想知道骑它到底难不难。他有次住在一个姨妈家时试过骑车。他站上花园里的长凳，费了九牛二虎之力才把自己从凳子上挪到车座上。他惊喜交加地骑出去几码远才摔下来。他又试了一次，又摔了下来。再试一次，一头骑进了冬青丛。他一门心思要学会这门技艺，把什么都忘了。他试了一遍又一遍。摔下车、或一头扎进冬青丛，一回又一回。自行车锃亮的黑漆刮花了，车把也撞得微微弯曲、失了光泽；威廉自己也摔得青一块紫一块、鼻青脸肿，但绝不认输。

===Original===
At last he managed to avoid the fatal magnet of the holly bush, to steer an unsteady ziz-zag course down the drive and out into the road. He had had no particular intention of riding into the road. In fact he was still wearing his befeathered headgear, blacked face, and the mat pinned to his shoulders. It was only when he was actually in the road that he realised that retreat was impossible, that he had no idea how to get off the bicycle.

What followed was to William more like a nightmare than anything else. He saw a motor-lorry coming towards him and in sudden panic turned down a side street and from that into another side street. People came out of their houses to watch him pass. Children booed or cheered him and ran after him in crowds. And William went on and on simply because he could not stop. His iron nerve had failed him. He had not even the presence of mind to fall off. He was quite lost. He had left the town behind him and did not know where he was going. But wherever he went he was the centre of attraction. The strange figure with blackened, streaked face, mat flying behind in the wind and a headdress of feathers from which every now and then one floated away, brought the population to its doors. Some said he had escaped from an asylum, some that he was an advertisement of something. The children were inclined to think he was part of a circus. William himself had passed beyond despair. His face was white and set. His first panic had changed to a dull certainty that this would go on forever. He would never know how to stop. He supposed he would go right across England. He wondered if he were near the sea now. He couldn’t be far off. He wondered if he would ever see his mother and father again. And his feet pedalled mechanically along. They did not reach the pedals at their lowest point; they had to catch them as they came up and send them down with all their might.

It was very tiring; William wondered if people would be sorry if he dropped down dead.

===Chinese===
终于，他设法躲开了冬青丛那块要命的磁石，歪歪扭扭地画着之字冲下车道，冲上了大路。他原本并没存心要骑到大路上去。事实上，他头上还顶着插满羽毛的头饰，脸还黑着，肩上还别着门垫。直到真真切切骑上了大路，他才意识到撤退已绝无可能——他压根不知道怎么从自行车上下来。

接下来发生的事，对威廉来说比什么都更像一场噩梦。他看见一辆卡车迎面驶来，惊慌之下拐进一条小街，又从那条街拐进另一条。人们走出家门看他经过。孩子们冲他喝倒彩或欢呼，成群结队地追着他跑。而威廉一路骑下去，只因为他停不下来。他那副钢铁神经垮了。他连从车上摔下来的镇定都没有了。他彻底迷了路。小镇已被他甩在身后，他不知道自己正往哪儿去。但无论他骑到哪儿，他都是万众瞩目的中心。那副奇怪的身影——涂得漆黑又一道道淌花的脸、迎风飞舞的门垫、不时飘走一根羽毛的头饰——把家家户户都引到了门口。有人说他从疯人院里逃了出来，有人说他是给什么东西做广告的。孩子们则倾向于认为他是马戏团的一分子。威廉本人早已越过了绝望。他的脸苍白而僵直。最初的恐慌已变成一种麻木的确信：这将永远继续下去。他永远学不会怎么停车了。他想自己怕是要一路横穿整个英格兰了。他寻思自己现在离海近不近。应该不远了。他不知道还能不能再见上爸爸妈妈一面。而他的双脚机械地一路蹬着。脚够不着转到最低点的踏板；只能等踏板转上来时一把够住，再拼尽全力把它蹬下去。

这太累人了；威廉寻思，要是他一头栽倒死掉，人们会不会难过。

===Original===
I have said that William did not know where he was going.

*But Fate knew.*

The picnickers walked down the hill from the little station to the river bank. It was a beautiful morning. Robert, his heart and hopes high, walked beside his goddess, revelling in his nearness to her though he could think of nothing to say to her. But Ethel and Mrs. Clive chattered gaily.

“We’ve given William the slip,” said Ethel with a laugh. “He’s no idea where we’ve gone even!”

“I’m sorry,” said Miss Cannon, “I’d have loved William to be here.”

===Chinese===
我说过，威廉不知道自己正去往何处。

*可命运知道。*

野餐的人们从小车站下山，朝河岸走去。这是个美丽的早晨。罗伯特心气与希望齐高，走在他女神身旁，陶醉于与她的咫尺之近，虽然想不出一句话可以对她说。埃塞尔和克莱夫太太倒聊得热火朝天。

「咱们把威廉甩掉啦，」埃塞尔笑着说，「他连咱们去了哪儿都不知道！」

「真遗憾，」卡农小姐说，「我本来倒盼着威廉也在呢。」

===Original===
“You don’t know him,” said Ethel fervently.

“What a beautiful morning it is!” murmured Robert, feeling that some remark was due from him. “Am I walking too fast for you﻿—Miss Cannon?”

“Oh, no.”

“May I carry your parasol for you?” he enquired humbly.

“Oh, no, thanks.”

===Chinese===
「你还不了解他。」埃塞尔恳切地说。

「多美的早晨啊！」罗伯特喃喃说道，觉得自己总该贡献一句话。「我是不是走得太快了——卡农小姐？」

「哦，不。」

「我能为您拿阳伞吗？」他谦恭地问。

「哦，不用了，谢谢。」

===Original===
He proposed a boat on the river after lunch, and it appeared that Miss Cannon would love it, but Ethel and Mrs. Clive would rather stay on the bank.

His cup of bliss was full. It would be his opportunity of sealing lifelong friendship with her, of arranging a regular correspondence, and hinting at his ultimate intentions. He must tell her that, of course, while he was at college he was not in a position to offer his heart and hand, but if she could wait﻿—He began to compose speeches in his mind.

They reached the bank and opened the luncheon baskets. Unhampered by Robert the cook had surpassed herself. They spread the white cloth and took up their position around it under the shade of the trees.

Just as Robert was taking up a plate of sandwiches to hand them with a courteous gesture to Miss Cannon, his eyes fell upon the long, white road leading from the village to the riverside and remained fixed there, his face frozen with horror. The hand that held the plate dropped lifelessly back again on to the tablecloth. Their eyes followed his. A curious figure was cycling along the road﻿—a figure with blackened face and a few drooping feathers on its head, and a doormat flying in the wind. A crowd of small children ran behind cheering. It was a figure vaguely familiar to them all.

===Chinese===
他提议午饭后到河上划船，卡农小姐看来正中下怀，可埃塞尔和克莱夫太太宁愿待在岸上。

他的幸福之杯已斟得满满当当。这将是他的机会——与她结下终生友谊，安排起固定的通信，并暗示自己的最终意向。他必须告诉她：当然，他还在念大学，尚不能向她献上自己的一颗心和一只手，可要是她肯等——他开始在脑子里起草起演说词来。

他们到了河岸，打开午餐篮。没了罗伯特指手画脚，厨师超水平发挥。他们铺开白桌布，在树荫下围着桌布各就各位。

罗伯特正端起一盘三明治，要彬彬有礼地递给卡农小姐，目光却落在了从村庄通向河边那条长长的白色大道上，僵住不动了，脸因恐惧而冻结。端盘子的手有气无力地垂了回去，盘子又落回桌布上。众人的目光顺着他的看去。一个奇怪的身影正沿着大路骑过来——黑脸、头顶几根耷拉下来的羽毛、门垫迎风飞舞的身影。一群小孩子欢呼着跟在后面跑。那身影让所有人都有种莫名的眼熟。

===Original===
“It can’t be,” said Robert hoarsely, passing a hand over his brow.

No one spoke.

It came nearer and nearer. There was no mistaking it.

“William!” gasped four voices.

William came to the end of the road. He did not turn aside to either of the roads by the riverside. He did not even recognise or look at them. With set, colourless face he rode on to the river bank, and straight amongst them. They fled from before his charge. He rode over the tablecloth, over the sandwiches, patties, rolls and cakes, down the bank and into the river.

===Chinese===
「不可能。」罗伯特哑着嗓子说，一只手抹过额头。

谁也没有说话。

它越来越近，越来越近。错不了了。

「威廉！」四个声音同时倒抽一口冷气。

威廉骑到了大路的尽头。他没有拐向河边那两条小道中的任何一条。他甚至没有认出他们，也没看他们一眼。他板着一张毫无血色的脸，径直骑上河岸，直直冲进他们中间。众人四散奔逃，躲避他的冲杀。他碾过桌布，碾过三明治、肉馅饼、小圆面包和蛋糕，冲下堤岸，一头扎进河里。

---

===Original===
They rescued him and the bicycle. Fate was against Robert even there. It was a passing boatman who performed the rescue. William emerged soaked to the skin, utterly exhausted, but feeling vaguely heroic. He was not in the least surprised to see them. He would have been surprised at nothing. And Robert wiped and examined his battered bicycle in impotent fury in the background while Miss Cannon pillowed William’s dripping head on her arm, fed him on hot coffee and sandwiches and called him “My poor darling Red Hand!”

She insisted on going home with him. All through the journey she sustained the character of his faithful squaw. Then, leaving a casual invitation to Robert and Ethel to come over to tea, she departed to pack.

Mrs. Brown descended the stairs from William’s room with a tray on which reposed a half-empty bowl of gruel, and met Robert in the hall.

“Robert,” she remonstrated, “you really needn’t look so upset.”

===Chinese===
他们把他和自行车都救了上来。即便在这一刻，命运也还在跟罗伯特作对。出手营救的是个路过的船夫。威廉浑身湿透、筋疲力尽地冒出水面，心里却隐隐觉得自己挺英勇。见到他们，他一点也不惊讶。他对什么都不可能再惊讶了。罗伯特在一旁狂怒而无可奈何地擦拭、检视他那辆伤痕累累的自行车，而卡农小姐让威廉那颗滴水的脑袋枕在她手臂上，喂他热咖啡和三明治，一口一个「我可怜的、亲爱的小红手！」

她坚持陪他一起回家。一路上她始终忠实地扮演着他的婆娘。随后，她随口留下一句请罗伯特和埃塞尔过去喝茶的邀请，便回住处收拾行李去了。

布朗太太端着一个托盘从威廉房里下楼，托盘上放着半碗喝剩的麦片粥，在门厅里遇见了罗伯特。

「罗伯特，」她劝道，「你实在不必摆出这么一副心烦意乱的样子。」

===Original===
Robert glared at her and laughed a hollow laugh.

“Upset!” he echoed, outraged by the inadequacy of the expression. “You’d be upset if your life was ruined. You’d be upset. I’ve a *right* to be upset.”

He passed his hand desperately through his already ruffled hair.

“You’re going there to tea,” she reminded him.

“Yes,” he said bitterly, “with other people. Who can talk with other people there? No one can. I’d have talked to her on the river. I’d got heaps of things ready in my mind to say. And William comes along and spoils my whole life﻿—and my bicycle. And she’s the most beautiful girl I’ve ever seen in my life. And I’ve wanted that bicycle for ever so long and it’s not fit to ride.”

===Chinese===
罗伯特瞪着她，干笑了一声。

「心烦意乱！」他重复道，这个词的软弱无力让他愤慨，「要是你的一生毁了，你也会心烦意乱的。你也会的。我心烦意乱可是*有权利*的。」

他绝望地用手抓过本来就乱作一团的头发。

「你不是要上那儿喝茶去嘛。」她提醒他。

「是啊，」他辛酸地说，「跟一大帮子人。有外人在场，谁谈得了话？谁也不能。我本来能在河上跟她谈的。我脑子里备好了满满一堆要说的话。结果威廉横插一杠，毁了我的一生——还搭上我的自行车。她是我这辈子见过的最美的姑娘。那辆自行车我又盼了那么久，如今却没法骑了。」

===Original===
“But poor William has caught a very bad chill, dear, so you oughtn’t to feel bitter to him. And he’ll have to pay for your bicycle being mended. He’ll have no pocket money till it’s paid for.”

“You’d think,” said Robert with a despairing gesture in the direction of the hall table and apparently addressing it, “you’d think four grown-up people in a house could keep a boy of William’s age in order, wouldn’t you? You’d think he wouldn’t be allowed to go about spoiling people’s lives and﻿—and ruining their bicycles. Well, he jolly well won’t do it again,” he ended darkly.

Mrs. Brown, proceeded in the direction of the kitchen.

“Robert,” she said soothingly over her shoulder, “you surely want to be at peace with your little brother, when he’s not well, don’t you?”

“*Peace?*” he said. Robert turned his haggard countenance upon her as though his ears must have deceived him. “*Peace!* I’ll wait. I’ll wait till he’s all right and going about; I won’t start till then. But﻿—peace! It’s not peace, it’s an *armistice*﻿—that’s all.”

===Chinese===
「可可怜的威廉得了很重的感冒，亲爱的，所以你不该对他心怀怨恨。再说，修自行车的钱也得由他出。赔清之前，他一点儿零花钱都别想有了。」

「你会以为，」罗伯特朝门厅的桌子做了一个绝望的手势，显然是在冲着桌子说话，「你会以为一屋子四个大人，总管得住一个威廉这般年纪的毛孩子吧，是不是？你会以为总不至于放任他到处毁人的一生——还——还毁人家的自行车吧。哼，他再有下次才怪。」他阴恻恻地收了尾。

布朗太太朝厨房方向去了。

「罗伯特，」她回头宽慰道，「他病着呢，你总归是想跟弟弟和和气气的，对不对？」

「*和气*？」他说。罗伯特把那张形容枯槁的脸转向她，仿佛自己的耳朵必定是听错了。「*和气*！我可以等。我等他病好、能下地乱跑了再说；在那之前我不会动手。可是——和气！这不是和气，这是*停战*——仅此而已。」
